\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})\text{ and }\operatorname{Tr}(F^{\prime}_{1},{\mathbf{o}}^{\prime}_{1})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha^{\prime},t^{\prime})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(F^{\prime}_{2},{\mathbf{o}}^{\prime}_{2})

of
\operatorname{Arr}(\Omega({\mathbf{O}}))
, respectively.

The map (
1.28
) amounts to a diagram

(1.29)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F^{\prime}_{1},{\mathbf{o}}^{\prime}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F^{\prime}_{2},{\mathbf{o}}^{\prime}_{2}),}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\beta_{1},G_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t)}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha^{\prime},t^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\beta_{2},G_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,\delta)}

where the new piece of data as compared to the 2-categorical trace is the 3-morphism that in the above diagram
we denote
\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,\delta)
.

1.5.5.

Consider the particular case, when

{\mathbf{o}}^{\prime}_{1}={\mathbf{o}}_{1},\,\,{\mathbf{o}}^{\prime}_{2}={\mathbf{o}}_{2},\,\,G_{1}=\operatorname{Id},\,\,G_{2}=\operatorname{Id},\,\,F_{1}=F^{\prime}_{1},\,\,F_{2}=F^{\prime}_{2},\,\,\beta_{1}=\operatorname{Id},\,\,\beta_{2}=\operatorname{Id}.

In this case:

•

\delta
is an
adjointable
2-morphism
t^{\prime}\Rightarrow t
;

•

\gamma
is a 3-morphism
\delta\circ\alpha^{\prime}\Rightarrow\alpha\circ\delta
, where the two sides are 2-morphisms

t^{\prime}\circ F_{1}\to F_{2}\circ t.

In this case, we obtain a 3-morphism

(1.30)

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha^{\prime},t^{\prime})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,\delta)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t),

where the two sides are 2-morphisms

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}).

1.6.
Functoriality of the 3-categorical trace

We continue to assume that
{\mathbf{O}}
is a symmetric monoidal 3-category.

1.6.1.

As in Sect.
1.2.9
, instead of
\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})
, for an index category
I
, we can consider the symmetric monoidal 2-category

\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}},

where

(\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}})^{1-\operatorname{Cat}}=\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}}^{2-\operatorname{Cat}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}},

and where 2-morphisms are defined as in Sect.
1.4.1
.

Parallel to Sect.
1.26
, we can identify

(1.31)

\Omega(\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}(I,\Omega({\mathbf{O}}))_{\operatorname{right-lax}}.

1.6.2.

Applying (
1.12
) to
\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}}
, we obtain a symmetric monoidal functor

(1.32)

{\mathbf{L}}(\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}}{\to}\Omega(\operatorname{Funct}(I,{\mathbf{O}})_{\operatorname{right-lax}})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}(I,\Omega({\mathbf{O}}))_{\operatorname{right-lax}}.

Moreover, the functors (
1.32
) are themselves functorial in
I
in the sense that they give rise to a natural
transformation between two functors

\operatorname{Cat}\to\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{SymMon}}.

1.6.3.

Let
{\mathbf{L}}({\mathbf{O}})
be the symmetric monoidal 2-category, whose underlying 1-category is
{\mathbf{L}}({\mathbf{O}}^{2-\operatorname{Cat}})
(see Sect.
1.2.10
),
and whose 2-morphisms are given by the data in Sect.
1.5.5
4
4

4

Note that in the formulas below we
notationally swap
t
and
t^{\prime}
.
with
\delta
adjointable.

I.e., a 2-morphism between the 1-morphisms

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2};}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

and

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2};}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}

is a datum of:

•

An
adjointable
2-morphism
t\overset{\delta}{\to}t^{\prime}
;

•

A 3-morphism

\delta\circ\alpha\overset{\gamma}{\to}\alpha^{\prime}\circ\delta,

where the two sides are 2-morphisms

t\circ F_{1}\to F_{2}\circ t^{\prime}.

1.6.4.

The functoriality in Sect.
1.6.2
implies that the trace construction extends to a symmetric monoidal functor
between 2-categories

(1.33)

{\mathbf{L}}({\mathbf{O}})\to\Omega({\mathbf{O}}).

1.7.
The notion of 2-adjointable morphism

Let
{\mathbf{O}}
be a 3-category. In this subsection, we will introduce the notion of
2-adjointable morphism
,
which will play a key role in the main body of the paper.

1.7.1.

We give the following definition:

Let

(1.34)

t:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2}

be 1-morphism in
{\mathbf{O}}
. We will say that
t
is
2-adjointable
if

•

t
is adjointable;

•

The unit and counit 2-morphisms

\operatorname{Id}\to t^{R}\circ t\text{ and }t\circ t^{R}\to\operatorname{Id}

are adjointable.

1.7.2.

We claim:

Lemma 1.7.3
.

If
t
is 2-adjointable, then the adjunction
(t,t^{R})
is ambidexterous.

Proof.

The right adjoints of the unit and the counit of the
(t,t^{R})
-adjunction
realize
t^{R}
as the left adjoint of
t
.

∎

1.7.4.

Assume now that
{\mathbf{O}}
carries a symmetric monoidal structure. We will now
explore how the notion of 2-adjointability interacts with the trace construction.

1.7.5.

Let us be given an endomorphism of (
1.34
) as an object of
\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})
,
represented by diagram

(1.35)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}.}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

Let
\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}
denote the resulting
2-morphism

F_{1}\circ t^{R}\to t^{R}\circ F_{2}.

Assume that
\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}
admits a right adjoint.
Then we obtain an endomorphism of the object

t^{R}:{\mathbf{o}}_{2}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})

represented by

(1.36)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}.}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{t^{R}}
\scriptstyle{t^{R}}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}})^{R}}

Assume that
t
is 2-adjointable. Then by Lemma
1.7.3
,
t^{R}
is adjointable, and hence
the diagram (
1.36
) gives rise to a 2-morphism

(1.37)

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}((\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}})^{R},t^{R})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}).

We claim:

Proposition 1.7.6
.

Under the above circumstances, the 1-morphism (
1.37
) is a right adjoint of

(1.38)

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t):\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}).

1.7.7.
Example

Here is a typical situation, when the conditions of Proposition
1.7.6
are satisfied: this happens when
\alpha
is an isomorphism
and satisfies the
right Beck-Chevalley condition
, i.e.,
\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}
is also an isomorphism.

The latter is, in turn, the case whenever
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
are both invertible.

1.7.8.
Proof of Proposition
1.7.6

We will construct the unit of the adjunction; the counit is constructed similarly.

By (
1.33
), it suffices to construct a 2-morphism in
{\mathbf{L}}({\mathbf{O}})
from the 1-morphism represented

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}

to the composition of 1-morphisms

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{t^{R}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{t^{R}}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}})^{R}}

In terms of Sect.
1.6.3
, the corresponding data is given by:

•

The unit of the
(t,t^{R})
-adjunction
\operatorname{Id}\to t^{R}\circ t
;

•

The 3-morphism
\gamma
in the diagram

(1.39)

\textstyle{F_{1}}
\textstyle{F_{1}}
\textstyle{t^{R}\circ t\circ F_{1}}
\textstyle{t^{R}\circ F_{2}\circ t}
\textstyle{F_{1}\circ t^{R}\circ t,}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{unit}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{unit}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{(\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}})^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma}

constructed below.

By the
(\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}},(\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}})^{R})
-adjunction, the datum of
\gamma
is equivalent to the datum
of a 3-morphism
\gamma^{\prime}
in the diagram

(1.40)

\textstyle{F_{1}}
\textstyle{F_{1}\circ t^{R}\circ t}
\textstyle{t^{R}\circ t\circ F_{1}}
\textstyle{t^{R}\circ F_{2}\circ t.}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{unit}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{unit}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\prime}}

However, by the definition of
\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}
, the diagram of 1-morphisms in (
1.40
) actually commutes,
and we take
\gamma^{\prime}
to be the natural isomorphism.

∎[Proposition
1.7.6
]

1.7.9.

Let us return to the setting of Sect.
1.7.7
: i.e.,
t
is 2-adjointable,
\alpha
is an isomorphism, and so is
\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}
.
Assume now that
t
is fully faithful, i.e. the unit of the adjunction

\operatorname{Id}\to t^{R}\circ t

is an isomorphism.

We claim:

Corollary 1.7.10
.

Under the above circumstances, the unit of the adjunction of Proposition
1.7.6
is an isomorphism;
in particular, the 2-morphism (
1.38
) is fully faithful.

Proof.

We need to show that under the conditions of the corollary, the 3-morphism (
1.39
)
is an isomorphism. However, this follows from the construction.

∎

1.8.
Interaction of 2-adjointable morphisms and 2-dualizability

In this subsection we will again use the notion of 2-adjointable morphism, but for a different purpose.

We continue to
assume that
{\mathbf{O}}
is a symmetric monoidal 3-category.

1.8.1.

The following is an extension of Lemma
1.2.3
:

Proposition 1.8.2
.

An object
t:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2}
of
\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})
is 2-dualizable if and only if the following conditions
hold:

•

{\mathbf{o}}_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}
are 2-dualizable as objects of
{\mathbf{O}}^{2-\operatorname{Cat}}
;

•

t
is 2-adjointable.

1.8.3.
Example

Let
{\mathbf{o}}_{1}={\mathbf{o}}_{2}={\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}}
, so that we can think of
t

as an object of
\Omega({\mathbf{O}})
. Then
t
is 2-adjointable as a 1-morphism in
{\mathbf{O}}
if and only if

t
is 2-dualizable as an object of
\Omega({\mathbf{O}})
.

Indeed, this follows from the fact that the composition and the tensor product operations in
\Omega({\mathbf{O}})

coincide.

Proof of Proposition
1.8.2
.

We will give a construction in one direction. Namely, we will show that

(t:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})

is 2-dualizable
assuming that the conditions of the proposition hold.

By Lemma
1.2.3
, the dual object is given by

(t^{R})^{\vee}:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}^{\vee}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2}^{\vee},

and the unit and counit for the duality are given by

(1.41)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{o}}_{1}^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}\otimes{\mathbf{o}}_{2}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{u}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{t\otimes(t^{R})^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{u}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime}}

and

(1.42)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{o}}_{1}^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{o}}_{1}^{\vee}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}},}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{t\otimes(t^{R})^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\prime\prime}}

where
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
are given by

(t\otimes(t^{R})^{\vee})\circ\operatorname{u}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}\simeq((t\circ t^{R})\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\operatorname{u}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}\overset{\gamma^{\prime}\otimes\operatorname{id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{u}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}

and

\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}\overset{\gamma^{\prime\prime}\otimes\operatorname{id}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}\circ((t^{R}\circ t)\otimes\operatorname{Id})\simeq\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}\circ(t\otimes(t^{R})^{\vee}),

where
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
are the counit and unit 2-morphisms for the
(t,t^{R})
-adjunction, respectively.

We need to show that (
1.41
) and (
1.42
) are adjointable 1-morphisms in
\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})
. However,
this follows from Lemma
1.4.3
:

Indeed,
\operatorname{ev}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{i}}
and
\operatorname{u}_{{\mathbf{o}}_{i}}
are adjointable, by assumptions. Now, the
2-morphisms
\alpha^{\prime}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}
are obtained from the 2-morphisms
\gamma^{\prime}
and
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
respectively, while the latter are adjointabile by assumption.

∎

1.8.4.

Let

t:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2}

be an object of
\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}})
, endowed with an endomorhism
F
.

By definition, this datum consists of a diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{o}}_{2};}
\scriptstyle{F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{t}
\scriptstyle{F_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

1.8.5.

Assume now that:

•

(t:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2})
is 2-dualizable, i.e., by Proposition
1.8.2
:

–

{\mathbf{o}}_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}
are 2-dualizable;

–

t
is 2-adjointable;

•

F
is adjointable, i.e., by Lemma
1.4.3
,

–

F_{1},F_{2}
are adjointable;

–

The 2-morphism
\alpha
is adjointable.

In this case, we can form the diagrams

\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t)}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t)}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}

and

\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{1})}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}).}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},t)}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},F_{1})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},t)}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},F_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

Furthermore, applying the functor (
1.33
), and using Lemma
1.3.5
and Proposition
1.8.2
, we obtain that:

•

The objects
\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}),\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})\in\Omega({\mathbf{O}})

are dualizable;

•

The 2-morphisms
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},t)
and
\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,t)
(i.e., 1-morphisms in
\Omega({\mathbf{O}})
)
are adjointable.

Hence, we have well-defined morphisms in
\Omega^{2}({\mathbf{O}})
:

(1.43)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}))\overset{t}{\to}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}))

and

(1.44)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},F_{1}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}))\overset{t}{\to}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},F_{2}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})).

1.8.6.

Finally, applying Corollary
1.3.10
to the object

({\mathbf{o}}_{1}\overset{t}{\to}{\mathbf{o}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Arr}({\mathbf{O}}),

equipped with the endomorphisms given by
(F,\alpha)
and
(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{id})
, we obtain:

Corollary 1.8.7
.

The following diagram in
\Omega^{2}({\mathbf{O}})
commutes:

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}(F_{1},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}))@>{t}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}(F_{2},{\mathbf{o}}_{2}))\\
@V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},F_{1}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{1}))@>{t}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},F_{2}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{o}}_{2})).\end{CD}

2.
Categorical representations

In this section we define the main object of study of this paper–the notion
of categorical representation of a group.

2.1.
The 3-category of 2-DG categories

In this subsection we will be referring to the terminology and notations from
[
GRV
, Sect. A.2]
.

Our goal here
is to define the ambient 3-category, to be denoted
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
, in which (the 2-category of) representations of
a given algebraic group will be an object.

2.1.1.

For a regular cardinal
\kappa
, let
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
be as in
[
GRV
, Sect. A.2.5]
.
Let

(2.1)

\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}\in\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}

be as in
[
GRV
, Sect. A.2.8]
.

We regard
\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa}
is a symmetric monoidal category with respect to the Lurie tensor product.
The object (
2.1
) has a natural structure of symmetric monoidal algebra in it.

In particular,
\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}
acquires a structure of symmetric monoidal category.

Since
\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}
is closed as a monoidal category, the assignment

{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in\operatorname{DGCat}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\in\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Cat}

makes
\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}
into a (symmetric monoidal)
(\infty,2)
-category.

2.1.2.

We set

2\operatorname{-DGCat}_{\kappa}:=\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{Cat}^{\operatorname{pres}}_{\kappa})\in\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}.

Recall that in
[
GRV
, Sect. A.2.8]
, we define

\operatorname{DGCat}:=\underset{\kappa}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\operatorname{DGCat}_{\kappa},

where the colimit is taken in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}
.

Similarly, set

2\operatorname{-DGCat}:=\underset{\kappa}{\operatorname{colim}}\,2\operatorname{-DGCat}_{\kappa}\in\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}.

This is a (closed) symmetric monoidal category. Its unit is
\operatorname{DGCat}
, viewed as a module over itself.

For a pair of objects
\mathfrak{O}_{1},\mathfrak{O}_{2}\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}
, we let

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{2\operatorname{-DGCat}}(\mathfrak{O}_{1},\mathfrak{O}_{2})\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}

the corresponding internal Hom object.

2.1.3.
Example

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be an associative algebra object in
\operatorname{DGCat}
. We will associate to it the object

{\mathbf{A}}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{DGCat})\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}.

For a pair of algebras
{\mathbf{A}}_{1},{\mathbf{A}}_{2}
, we have

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{2\operatorname{-DGCat}}({\mathbf{A}}_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{DGCat}),{\mathbf{A}}_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{DGCat}))\simeq({\mathbf{A}}_{1}^{\operatorname{rev}}\otimes{\mathbf{A}}_{2}){\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{DGCat}).

2.1.4.

For an object
\mathfrak{O}\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}
, we can consider the underlying large category (which we denote by the same symbol
\mathfrak{O}
).
For a pair of objects
{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in\mathfrak{O}
we have a well-defined

relative internal Hom

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathfrak{O},\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\in\operatorname{DGCat},

characterized by the property that for
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}

\operatorname{Maps}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}},\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathfrak{O},\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{Maps}_{\mathfrak{O}}({\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})

as objects in
\operatorname{Spc}
.

2.1.5.

The assignment

{\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2}\in\mathfrak{O}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathfrak{O},\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\in\operatorname{DGCat}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\mathfrak{O},\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Cat}

upgrades
\mathfrak{O}
to an
(\infty,2)
-category, i.e., we obtain a forgetful functor

2\operatorname{-DGCat}\to 2\operatorname{-Cat}.

Now, the assignment

\mathfrak{O}_{1},\mathfrak{O}_{2}\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{2\operatorname{-DGCat}}(\mathfrak{O}_{1},\mathfrak{O}_{2})\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{2\operatorname{-DGCat}}(\mathfrak{O}_{1},\mathfrak{O}_{2})\in 2\operatorname{-Cat}

makes
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
into a (closed, symmetric monoidal)
(\infty,3)
-category.

2.1.6.

Recall now (see
[
GRV
, Sect. 2.3.2]
) the commutative algebra object

\operatorname{AGCat}\in\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}.

It is equipped with a homomorphism (of commutative algebra objects in
\operatorname{Cat}_{\operatorname{large}}
)

{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat},

see
[
GRV
, Sect. 2.4.1]
.

By its construction,
\operatorname{AGCat}
naturally upgrades to a commutative algebra object in
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
, and
{\mathbf{i}}

upgrades to a homomorphism of commutative algebras in
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
, supplying
\operatorname{AGCat}
with a unit.

2.1.7.

Finally, set

2\operatorname{-AGCat}\simeq\operatorname{AGCat}{\operatorname{-mod}}(2\operatorname{-DGCat}).

2.1.8.

The structure of (closed, symmetric monoidal)
(\infty,3)
-category on
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
induces one on
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.
Its unit object is
\operatorname{AGCat}
, viewed as a module over itself.

2.1.9.
Example

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}
be an associative algebra object in
\operatorname{AGCat}
. We will associate to it the object

\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{AGCat})\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}.

For a pair of algebras
\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{2}
, we have

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{2\operatorname{-AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{AGCat}),\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq(\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{1}^{\operatorname{rev}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{A}}}_{2}){\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

2.1.10.

For an object
\underline{\mathfrak{O}}\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}
, we can consider the underlying object of
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
, which we denote by the same symbol
\underline{\mathfrak{O}}
.
For a pair of objects
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}\in\underline{\mathfrak{O}}
we have a well-defined

relative internal Hom

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{\mathfrak{O}},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})\in\operatorname{AGCat},

characterized by the property that for
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat},\operatorname{DGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\underline{\mathfrak{O}},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{Maps}_{\underline{\mathfrak{O}},\operatorname{DGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})

as objects of
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

2.1.11.
Notational conventions

In the rest of the paper, we will (do our best to) adhere to the following conventions:

For a monoidal
(\infty,1)
-category
{\mathbf{O}}
and
A\in\operatorname{AssocAlg}({\mathbf{O}})
we will denote by

A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})

the usual
(\infty,1)
-category of
A
-modules in
{\mathbf{O}}
.

For a monoidal
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathfrak{O}
and
{\mathbf{A}}\in\operatorname{AssocAlg}(\mathfrak{O})
we will denote by

{\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\mathfrak{O})

the
(\infty,2)
-category of
{\mathbf{A}}
-modules in
\mathfrak{O}
. I.e., we use boldface (
\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
vs
{\operatorname{-mod}}
)
to emphasize the level of categoricity.

In addition, we will differentiate objects of
\operatorname{AGCat}
from objects of
\operatorname{DGCat}
by using the underline
(
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
vs
{\mathbf{C}}
) for the former.

2.1.12.
The tensoring up operation

We have a pair of adjoint functors

(2.2)

2\operatorname{-DGCat}\rightleftarrows 2\operatorname{-AGCat},

given by restriction and tensoring up, respectively.

In particular, for
{\mathfrak{E}}\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}
, we can consider the object

(2.3)

\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}{\mathfrak{E}}\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat},

and further

\operatorname{Res}^{\operatorname{AGCat}}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}{\mathfrak{E}})\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat},

which, by a slight abuse of notation, we denote by the same symbol (
2.3
).

The unit of the (
2.2
) gives rise to a functor

(2.4)

({\mathbf{i}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathfrak{E}}}):{\mathfrak{E}}\to\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}{\mathfrak{E}}\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}

in
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
.

Note, however, that the functor
{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
is fully faithful and admits
a
\operatorname{DGCat}
-linear right adjoint, denoted
{\mathbf{e}}
, see
[
GRV
, Sect. 2.4.1]
. This implies
that the functor (
2.4
) is fully faithful and also admits a right adjoint,
namely,

{\mathfrak{E}}\leftarrow\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}{\mathfrak{E}}:({\mathbf{e}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathfrak{E}}}).

Moreover, by
[
GRV
, Corollary 4.5.6]
, the above functor (
2.4
)
is
2-adjointable
. This implies that the

({\mathbf{i}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathfrak{E}}})\leftrightarrow({\mathbf{e}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathfrak{E}}})

adjunction is actually ambidexterous.

2.2.
The notion of categorical representation

2.2.1.

Recall that to any
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
we assign an object

(2.5)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)\in\operatorname{AGCat}.

Since any
X
naturally upgrades to a cocommutative coalgebra object in
\operatorname{Sch}
,
the object
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(X)
is naturally a cocommutative coalgebra in
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

Remark 2.2.2
.

Note that the latter uses the fact that the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(-):\operatorname{Sch}\to\operatorname{AGCat}

is
strictly
symmetric
monoidal. This is the key difference between
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(-)
and
the functor

\operatorname{Shv}:\operatorname{Sch}\to\operatorname{DGCat},

which is only right-lax symmetric monoidal.

2.2.3.

Let now
G
be an algebraic group, i.e., a group-object in
\operatorname{Sch}
. We obtain that

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
carries a structure of
cocommutative
Hopf algebra in
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

2.2.4.

Recall also that for every
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
, the object (
2.5
) is canonically
self-dual.

In particular, by duality,
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
obtains a structure of
commutative
Hopf algebra.
In order to avoid confusion, we will notationally differentiate the two as follows:

•

We will keep the symbol
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
for the (commutative) Hopf algebra structure, in which the

comultiplication
is induced by the functor
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
;

•

We will use the symbol
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
for the (cocommutative) Hopf algebra structure, in which the

multiplication
is induced by the functor
\operatorname{mult}_{*}
.

Above and in the rest of the paper,

\operatorname{mult}:G\times G\to G

is the group structure on
G
.

Note that the functors
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
and
\operatorname{mult}_{*}
are each others’ duals, as they should be.

2.2.5.

Set

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}:=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}.

Equivalently,

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

For
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, we will denote by

\operatorname{act}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

the corresponding 1-morphism that encodes the module structure.

2.2.6.
Example

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack equipped with an action of
G
. Then pullback with respect to the action map

G\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
makes
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
an object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, thought of as
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat})
.

The action of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
is given by pushforward along the (schematic) map
G\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Let
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
be a map between prestacks acted on by
G
. The functor

f^{!}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

naturally upgrades to a 1-morphism in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

Assume now that
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
are
AG tame
(see
[
GRV
, Sect. 3.4.7]
). In this
case we have the 1-morphism

f_{\blacktriangle}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

(see
[
GRV
, Sect. 3.7.3]
), which also naturally upgrades to a 1-morphism in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

In the sequel, given a diagram of AG tame prestacks acted on by
G

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}@>{h}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\\
@V{v}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},\end{CD}

we will refer to the 1-morphism
h_{\blacktriangle}\circ v^{!}
in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

as “pull-push” along this diagram.

2.2.7.

Let us write down explicitly the data involved in specifying an object
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
(from the comodule perspective).

First,
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
, i.e., an assignment

(X\in\operatorname{Sch})\rightsquigarrow(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\in\operatorname{DGCat}),

with the functorialities specified in
[
GRV
, Sect. 0.1.4]
.

The action of
G
is given by a map

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\overset{\operatorname{coact}}{\to}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G),

equipped with a data of associativity.

The above coaction functor amounts to a collection of functors

(2.6)

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\overset{\operatorname{coact}_{X}}{\to}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G))(X)\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G),

(functorial in
X
), and the associativity is encoded by the commutativity of the diagrams

(2.7)

\begin{CD}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)@>{\operatorname{coact}_{X}}>{}>\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G)\\
@V{\operatorname{coact}_{X}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{mult}^{!}}V\\
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G)@>{\operatorname{coact}_{X\times G}}>{}>\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G\times G),\end{CD}

and diagrams that involve higher powers of
G
for the data of higher coherence.

2.2.8.

The structure on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
(resp.,
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
) of
cocommutative (resp., commutative) Hopf algebra induces a symmetric monoidal
structure on
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, compatible with the forgetful functor

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}.

The unit object in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is
\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}
, equipped with the trivial action.

2.2.9.

The functor
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}
admits a left and a right adjoint. The left adjoint is given by

\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}\mapsto\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}\in\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})=G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

The right adjoint is given by

\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}\mapsto{\mathbf{D}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\in\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat})=G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

In particular, we obtain that this adjunction is
ambidexterous
.

2.2.10.

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be a pair of groups. Since

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1}\times G_{2})

as cocommutative Hopf algebras, we obtain that the natural functor

(2.8)

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\otimes G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is an equivalence in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.

2.2.11.

One can ask the following question: are there (interesting) examples of objects of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, such that
the underlying object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
is
restricted
, i.e., lies in the essential image of the functor

{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}?

Note that the data of such an object amounts, in particular, to
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
, equipped with a coaction functor

(2.9)

{\mathbf{C}}\overset{\operatorname{coact}}{\to}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G),

which makes the following diagram commute:

(2.10)

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}&@>{\operatorname{coact}}>{}>&{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\\
@V{\operatorname{coact}}V{}V&&@V{}V{\operatorname{mult}^{!}}V\\
{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)@>{\operatorname{coact}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)@>{\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\boxtimes)}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G\times G).\end{CD}

When
G
is connected, we will (almost) classify such objects in Sect.
4.2
.

2.2.12.

We end this subsection with the following observation:

Lemma 2.2.13
.

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}
be a 1-morphism in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
. Then it admits a right/left
adjoint if and only if the induced 1-morphism

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1})\to{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})

does.

Proof.

We will give a proof for right adjoints; the proof for left adjoints is similar.
Suppose that
F:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}
is such that

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\leftarrow\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}:F^{R}

(of the underlying objects of
\operatorname{AGCat}
) exists. Then a priori,
F^{R}
has a structure of
right-lax
compatibility with
the action of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
. But we claim that this structure is automatically strict.

This claim amounts to the following. Let
X
be a scheme and let
g
be an
X
-point
of
G
. This point gives rise to an automorphism

g\cdot(-):{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(X)\to{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(X),\quad i=1,2.

The right-lax compatibility structure says that we have a natural transformation

(g\cdot(-))\circ F^{R}\overset{\alpha_{g}}{\to}F^{R}\circ(g\cdot(-)).

We need to show that
\alpha_{g}
is invertible.

We will write down the inverse of
\alpha_{g}
explicitly. Namely, it is given by

F^{R}\circ(g\cdot(-))\simeq(g\cdot(-))\circ(g^{-1}\cdot(-))\circ F^{R}\circ(g\cdot(-))\overset{\alpha_{g^{-1}}}{\to}\\
\to(g\cdot(-))\circ F^{R}\circ(g^{-1}\cdot(-))\circ(g\cdot(-))\simeq(g\cdot(-))\circ F^{R}.

∎

Remark 2.2.14
.

Note that for the validity of Lemma
2.2.13
, it is essential that
G
is a group rather than a monoid.

2.3.
Invariants and coinvariants

In this subsection we start investigating the basic structures on
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, by which we mean that we start probing
it by 1-morphisms from other (simpler) objects of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.

2.3.1.

Let

{\mathbf{triv}}_{G}:\operatorname{AGCat}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

be the the functor of trivial representation.

This functor admits a right and a left adjoint, denoted
\mathbf{inv}_{G}
and
\mathbf{coinv}_{G}
, respectively.
They are explicitly computed as follows:

•

\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\overset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}:=\operatorname{Tot}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G^{\bullet})),

where
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G^{\bullet})
is the
cosimplicial
object
of
\operatorname{AGCat}
that encodes the
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
-coaction on
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
.

Note that since limits in
\operatorname{AGCat}
are computed value-wise, we have

\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})(X)\simeq\operatorname{Tot}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet})),

where the latter totalization is taken in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

•

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}\simeq|\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G^{\bullet})_{\operatorname{co}}|,

where
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G^{\bullet})_{\operatorname{co}}
is the
simplicial
object
of
\operatorname{AGCat}
that encodes the
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
-action on
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
.

Note that since colimits on
\operatorname{AGCat}
are also computed value-wise, we have

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})(X)\simeq|\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet})|,

where the latter geometric realization is taken in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

2.3.2.
Example

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack with an action of
G
, and consider the corresponding object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

Unwinding, we obtain that

(2.11)

\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}/G).

2.3.3.

For a pair of objects
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, we can form the objects

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})\text{ and }\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}

in
\operatorname{AGCat}
, and both naturally upgrade to objects of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
using the Hopf algebra structure.

Notational convention
: In the above formulas, by a slight abuse of notation, we write
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
instead of
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})
,
and we will do so in the rest of the paper unless this is likely to cause a confusion.

Unwinding, we obtain:

(2.12)

\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})

(where
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(-,-)
denotes the internal Hom in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
relative to
\operatorname{AGCat}
,
see
[
GRV
, Sect. 1.1.2]
), and

(2.13)

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}.

2.3.4.

Consider the forgetful functor (in
\operatorname{AGCat}
):
5
5

5

We draw the reader’s attention to the distinction between the notations

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}
and
\operatorname{oblv}_{G}
: the former is a functor between 2-categories (that forgets the categorical
G
-action); the former
is a functor between 1-categories (that forgets the structure of
G
-equivariance, which is a generalization of the notion of usual

G
-action on an object of a category).

\operatorname{oblv}_{G}:\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}.

It is easy to see that it admits a right adjoint (in
\operatorname{AGCat}
), to be denoted
\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}
.

Explicitly, the composition of
\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}
with
\operatorname{oblv}_{G}
is the comonad on
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
, given by

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\overset{\operatorname{act}}{\to}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},

where the second arrow is the 1-morphism induced by

\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\,\Leftrightarrow\,\operatorname{Vect}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G),

given by the constant sheaf
\operatorname{const}_{G}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)
.

Remark 2.3.5
.

One can view the
(\operatorname{oblv}_{G},\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*})
-adjunction as follows:

We have an adjunction

(2.14)

\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G,-)}{\overset{\operatorname{const}_{G}}{\rightleftarrows}}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, and the adjunction

\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\underset{\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}}{\overset{\operatorname{oblv}_{G}}{\rightleftarrows}}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

by taking

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(-,\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

from the two sides of (
2.14
).

2.3.6.

The above 1-morphism

\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

is
G
-invariant. Hence, it canonically factors as

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}).

In particular, we obtain a natural transformation

(2.15)

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}\to\mathbf{inv}_{G}

as functors
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
.

2.3.7.

We claim:

Proposition 2.3.8
.

The natural transformation (
2.15
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

When calculating

\operatorname{Tot}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet}))\text{ and }|\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet})|,

we can replace the cosimplicial limit (resp., simplicial colimit) by the corresponding
semi-cosimplicial (resp., semi-simplicial) one:

(2.16)

\operatorname{Tot}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet}))^{\operatorname{semi}}\text{ and }|\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet})|^{\operatorname{semi}}.

Note now that the functor of cohomological shift by
[-2\cdot\bullet\cdot\dim(G)]
gives rise to an isomorphism
between the semi-cosimplicial category
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet})
in the left-hand side of (
2.16
)
with one obtained from the semi-simplicial category
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G^{\bullet})
in the right-hand side of (
2.16
)
by passing to right adjoints.

By
[
GR
, Chapter 1, Proposition 2.5.7]
, this gives rise to
an
equivalence between the two sides of (
2.16
).
However, unwinding the definitions, it is easy to see that the resulting functor
\leftarrow
in (
2.16
)
is the value of (
2.15
) on
X
.

∎

2.3.9.

As a corollary, we obtain:

Corollary 2.3.10
.

The functor

\mathbf{inv}_{G}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}

commutes with colimits and with tensors by objects of
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

2.4.
Dualilzability for categorical representations

Recall that our ability to perform the trace construction necessitated the dualizability
assumption on objects.

In this subsection we will see what this condition means for
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

(at the 3-categorical and 2-categorical levels).

2.4.1.

First, we claim that

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}

is dualizable and self-dual:

(2.17)

(G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee}\simeq G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

Indeed, this is the case for objects of the form

A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})\in{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

where:

•

{\mathbf{O}}
is a symmetric monoidal category, so
{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is a symmetric monoidal 2-category;

•

A
is an associative algebra on
{\mathbf{O}}
, equipped with an
anti-involution.
6
6

6

A priori, the dual is
A^{\operatorname{rev}}{\operatorname{-mod}}
, but the anti-involution
provides an identification
A\simeq A^{\operatorname{rev}}
(here and elsewhere, the superscript “rev” means
“algebra with reversed multiplication”).

In our case
{\mathbf{O}}:=\operatorname{AGCat}
and
A:=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
, with the anti-involution provided by the antipode.

2.4.2.

Let
H
be a cocommutative Hopf algebra in a symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{O}}
, so that

H{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})
acquires a symmetric monoidal structure compatible with the forgetful functor

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{H}:H{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})\to{\mathbf{O}}.

Note that in this situation, an object of
H{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})
is dualizable if and only if the underlying
object
{\mathbf{o}}\in{\mathbf{O}}
is dualizable, and the dual is given by
{\mathbf{o}}^{\vee}
with the natural
right

action, which we turn into a left action using the antipode on
H
.

2.4.3.

In particular, we obtain that an object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is dualizable if and only if the underlying
object
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
is dualizable, and the dual is given by
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}
with the
natural action.

In particular, we obtain that if
{\mathcal{Y}}
is an
AG tame
prestack (see
[
GRV
, Sect. 3.4.7]
) equipped with
an action of
G
, then the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is dualizable and self-dual (by
[
GRV
, Theorem 3.6.2 and Sect. 3.5.11]
).

In particular, this is the case whenever
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a
pseudo-scheme
(in particular, an ind-scheme)
(see
[
GRV
, Lemma 3.4.5]
).

Crucially for us, this is the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}
is an algebraic stack that is locally a quotient of a scheme
by an algebraic group: this follows from
[
GRV
, Proposition 4.6.4]
and
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem F.2.8]
.

In fact, according to
[
AGKRRV1
, Conjecture F.2.7]
, we expect this to be the case for any quasi-compact algebraic stack with a schematic diagonal.

2.4.4.

Recall that an object
M
of the category of the form
A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})
(here
A
is an associative algebra in some symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{O}}
)
is said to be
totally
{\mathbf{O}}
-compact
(a.k.a dualizable as an
A
-module, see
[
GRV
, Lemma B.4.4(ii)]
)
if there exists an object
M^{\vee}\in A^{\operatorname{rev}}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})
and
an isomorphism

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}),{\mathbf{O}}}(M,-)\simeq M^{\vee}\underset{A}{\otimes}-

as functors
A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})\to{\mathbf{O}}
, where
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}),{\mathbf{O}}}(-,-)
denotes the functor of
internal Hom in
A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})
relative to
{\mathbf{O}}
,
see
[
GRV
, Sect. 1.1.2]
.

In this case, we call
M^{\vee}
the dual of
M
as an
A
-module.

2.4.5.

Take
{\mathbf{O}}=\operatorname{AGCat}
and
A=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
. We claim:

Proposition 2.4.6
.

An object

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})

is totally compact if and only if the object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
underlying
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

is dualizable. Moreover, in this case, the dual of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
as an object of

(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}})^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

identifies with
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}
with the natural action.

Proof.

One direction is easy: if
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is dualizable as a
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
-module with dual
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}
, then for any

\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
and

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

we have

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{D}}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{{\mathbf{D}}})

and

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{D}}})\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{D}}},

so we obtain an equivalence

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{{\mathbf{D}}})\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{D}}},

functorial in
\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}
. This implies that
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is dualizable as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
with dual
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}
.

Vice versa, assume that
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is dualizable as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
. For
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, we write

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:inv Funct}}}{\simeq}\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}))\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:coinv to inv}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime}))\simeq\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:coinv ten}}}{\simeq}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\prime},

i.e.,
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is indeed dualizable as a
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
-module with dual
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}
.

∎

2.4.7.

We now claim:

Corollary 2.4.8
.

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
be dualizable (in the sense of the symmetric monoidal structure). Then
\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\in\operatorname{AGCat}
is dualizable.

Proof.

Follows from Propositions
2.3.8
and
2.4.6
, using
the fact that the functor
\mathbf{coinv}_{G}

sends totally compact objects to dualizable ones (see, e.g.,
[
GRV
, Lemma B.4.4]
).

∎

2.5.
Adjoints of 1-morphisms

Recall that the trace operation in a 2-category is functorial with respect to 1-morphisms that are

adjointable
.

In this subsection we will see how the adjointability condition plays out for 1-morphisms

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

2.5.1.

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be a pair of groups, and recall the equivalence (
2.8
).
We obtain that

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{2\operatorname{-AGCat}}(G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq(G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee}\otimes G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq\\
\simeq G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\otimes G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

as objects of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.

In particular, given

{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\in(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

we obtain a functor, to be denoted

T_{{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}}:G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

2.5.2.

Recall that for three of algebras
A_{1},A_{2},A_{3}
in a symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{O}}
, under the identifications

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}(A_{i}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}),A_{j}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}))\simeq(A_{i}^{\operatorname{rev}}\otimes A_{j})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}({\mathbf{O}}),

the composition functor

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}(A_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}),A_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}))\times\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}(A_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}),A_{2}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}))\to\\
\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}(A_{1}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}),A_{3}{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}))

corresponds to

(A_{1}^{\operatorname{rev}}\otimes A_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}({\mathbf{O}})\times(A_{2}^{\operatorname{rev}}\otimes A_{3})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}({\mathbf{O}})\to(A_{1}^{\operatorname{rev}}\otimes A_{3})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}({\mathbf{O}}),\quad M_{1,2},M_{2,3}\mapsto M_{2,3}\underset{A_{2}}{\otimes}M_{1,2}.

Hence, for

{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\in(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\text{ and }{\mathbf{M}}_{2,3}\in(G_{2}\times G_{3})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

we have

T_{{\mathbf{M}}_{2,3}}\circ T_{{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}}\simeq T_{{\mathbf{M}}_{1,3}},

where

{\mathbf{M}}_{1,3}={\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{M}}_{2,3}\simeq\mathbf{coinv}_{\Delta(G_{2})}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{2,3})\in(G_{1}\times G_{3})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

For example, let
{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2})
and
{\mathbf{M}}_{2,3}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3})
, where
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3}
)
is a prestack acted on by
G_{1}\times G_{2}
(resp.,
G_{2}\times G_{3}
), and let us assume that one of these prestaks is AG tame.

Then
{\mathbf{M}}_{1,3}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,3})
for

{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,3}=({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3})/\Delta(G_{2}).

Indeed, this follows from

(2.18)

\mathbf{coinv}_{G_{2}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3}))\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:coinv to inv}}}{\simeq}\mathbf{inv}_{G_{2}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3}))\overset{\text{\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GRV}{}{}, Cor. 3.6.7]}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\mathbf{inv}_{G_{2}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:inv as quot}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,3})/\Delta(G_{2})).

2.5.3.

For
{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}
as above, the functor
T_{{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}}
is adjointable if and only if
{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}
is totally compact as an object of
G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
. However, according to
Proposition
2.4.6
this is equivalent to
{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}
being dualizable as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

Moreover, in this case
the adjoint functor is given by
{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}^{\vee}
, viewed as an object of
(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

2.5.4.

Explicitly, the unit of the adjunction is the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1})\to\mathbf{inv}_{G_{2}}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{2,1})\simeq{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2})_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{M}}_{2,1},

where the action of
G_{1}
on the unit of the duality

\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}\to{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{2,1},

which automatically factors via

\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}\to\mathbf{inv}_{G_{2}}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\otimes{\mathbf{M}}_{2,1}).

The counit is described similarly.

2.5.5.

In particular, we obtain that if

{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}),

where
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}
is an AG tame prestack acted on by
G_{1}\times G_{2}
, then the corresponding functor

T_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}}:=T_{{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}}

is adjointable.

Moreover,

(T_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}})^{R}\simeq T_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,1}},

where
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,1}
is the same as
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}
as a prestack,
viewed as acted on by
G_{2}\times G_{1}
.

2.5.6.

Let us describe explicitly the adjunction data for the 1-morphisms
T_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}}
and
T_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,1}}
above.
Namely, applying
[
GRV
, Sect. 3.7]
and using Sect.
2.5.4
, we obtain the following:

The unit of the adjunction is given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}G_{1}\times({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}/G_{2})@>{h}>{}>({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2,1})/\Delta(G_{2})\\
@V{v}V{}V\\
G_{1},\end{CD}

where:

•

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1})
is viewed as an object in
(G_{1}\times G_{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
giving rise to the identity endofunctor of
G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
;

•

We view
G_{1}\times({\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2}/G_{2})
as a prestack acted on by
G_{1}\times G_{1}
by

(g^{\prime}_{1},g^{\prime\prime}_{1})\cdot(g_{1},y)=(g^{\prime}_{1}\cdot g_{1}\cdot(g^{\prime\prime}_{1})^{-1},g^{\prime\prime}_{1}\cdot y);

•

The map
v
is the projection on the
G_{1}
factor;

•

The map
h
is
(g_{1},y)\mapsto(g_{1}\cdot y,y)
.

The counit of the adjunction is defined similarly using the diagram

\begin{CD}G_{2}\times({\mathcal{Y}}_{2,1}/G_{1})@>{}>{}>G_{2}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
({\mathcal{Y}}_{2,1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{1,2})/\Delta(G_{1}).\end{CD}

2.5.7.

Let us return to the data of self-duality of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
as an object of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
. By construction, the unit and counit
are given by
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
, viewed as an object of

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\otimes G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

Since
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\in\operatorname{AGCat}
is dualizable, we obtain:

Corollary 2.5.8
.

The object
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}
is 2-dualizable.

Remark 2.5.9
.

The description of the adjoints in Sect.
2.5.5
implies that the Serre functor on
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is canonically isomorphic to the identity. I.e.,
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is a
Calabi-Yau
object of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.

2.6.
Patterns of 2-adjointability for categorical representations

2.6.1.

Let us now be given a pair of groups
G_{1},G_{2}
and a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
acted on by
G_{1}\times G_{2}
. Consider the corresponding 1-morphism

(2.19)

T_{\mathcal{Y}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

We would like to give sufficient conditions for (
2.19
) to be 2-adjointable.

2.6.2.

We will say that a map

f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}

between AG tame prestacks is
mock-proper
, if the corresponding 1-morphism

f_{\blacktriangle}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})

is adjointable (in
\operatorname{AGCat}
).

We will say that an AG tame prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
is
mock-proper
if its map to
\operatorname{pt}

is mock-proper.

It is easy to see that if
f
is mock-proper, then so is the map

f\times\operatorname{id}:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Z}}

for any AG tame prestack
{\mathcal{Z}}
(recall that the class of AG tame prestacks is closed under products, see

[
GRV
, Proposition 3.6.11]
).

Also, it is clear that a composition of mock-proper maps is mock-proper.

2.6.3.
Examples

•

A proper schematic map between AG tame prestacks is mock-proper.

•

For any algebraic group
G
, the stack
\operatorname{pt}/G
is mock proper.

•

Combining the previous two examples, we obtain that if
Y
is a proper scheme and

G
is an algebraic group acting on
Y
, then
Y/G
is mock-proper.

•

The stack
{\mathbb{A}}^{n}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
is mock-proper, where
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
acts on
{\mathbb{A}}^{n}
by dilations.

•

A cotruncative open embedding (see
[
DrGa2
, Sect. 3]
) is mock-proper.

2.6.4.

One can show that if a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between schemes is
mock-proper, them it is actually proper.

Indeed, it follows from
[
AGKRRV2
, Theorem B.6.3(a) and Proposition B.4.4(b)]
that if
f
is mock-proper,
then
f_{!}\to f_{*}
is an isomorphism. However, the latter implies that
f
is proper.

2.6.5.

We will say that a map

f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}

between prestacks is
mock-smooth
, if the corresponding 1-morphism

f^{!}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})

is adjointable (in
\operatorname{AGCat}
).

Remarks pertaining to the notion of mock-properness in Sect.
2.6.2

are equally applicable to the notion of mock-smoothness.

2.6.6.
Examples

•

A smooth schematic map is mock-smooth.

•

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a smooth quasi-compact algebraic stack. Then it is mock-smooth if and
only if it is
safe
7
7

7

“Safe” means that any constructible sheaf on
{\mathcal{Y}}
is compact,
see
[
DrGa1
, Sect. 9]
.
(see
[
GaVa
, Sect. 1.3(d)]
).

•

A truncative locally closed embedding (see
[
DrGa2
, Sect. 3]
) is mock-smooth.

•

In particular, the map

\operatorname{pt}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{n}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}

is mock-smooth.

2.6.7.

One can show that a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
between schemes is mock-smooth if and only if
the constant sheaf of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
is ULA with respect to
f
.

2.6.8.

The following assertion results from the description of the unit and counit of the adjunction in Sect.
2.5.6
:

Proposition 2.6.9
.

Assume that:

(i) The action map
G_{1}\times({\mathcal{Y}}/G_{2})\to({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})/G_{2}
is mock-proper;

(ii) The stack
{\mathcal{Y}}/G_{1}
is mock-proper;

(iii) The action map
G_{2}\times({\mathcal{Y}}/G_{1})\to({\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}})/G_{1}
is mock-smooth;

(iv) The stack
{\mathcal{Y}}/G_{2}
is mock-smooth.

Then the 1-morphism (
2.19
) is 2-adjointable.

2.6.10.

Combining Propositions
2.6.9
and
1.8.2
with Corollary
2.5.8
, we obtain:

Corollary 2.6.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack equipped with an action of
G_{1}\times G_{2}
, satisfying
the assumptions of Proposition
2.6.9
. Then the object of

\operatorname{Arr}(2\operatorname{-AGCat})
given by (
2.19
) is 2-dualizable.

2.6.12.
A non-example

Let us take
G_{1}=1
and
G_{2}=G
, so we are dealing with a 1-morphism

{\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

given by a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}=Y
is a smooth separated scheme and
G
is connected.
We claim that the conditions of Proposition
2.6.9
are
never
satisfied unless
Y
is a union of points
and
G
is unipotent (which is not an interesting case).

Indeed, condition (ii) implies that
Y
is proper (see Sect.
2.6.4
).
Condition (iii) implies that the map

G\times Y\to Y\times Y

is ULA, which implies that
G
acts transitively on each connected component of
Y
,

Hence, (ii) and (iii) together imply that each connected component of
Y
is of the form

G/P
, where
P\subset G
is a parabolic
subgroup. Now, condition (iv) says that
\operatorname{pt}/P
is mock-smooth, and hence safe.
This implies that
P
is unipotent, which in turn implies that
P=G
is unipotent.

2.6.13.
The key example

Let us take
G_{2}=G
to be a connected reductive group, and
G_{1}=M=P/N(P)
to be the Levi
quotient of a parabolic
P
. Take

Y:=G/N(P),

with the natural action of
G\times M
.

We claim that this
Y
satisfies the conditions of Proposition
2.6.9
.

Condition (i) holds because the map in question is schematic and proper. Indeed, the map

M\times Y\to Y\times Y

is a closed embedding; this is an expression of the fact that
M
acts freely on
Y
with quotient
G/P
.

Condition (ii) holds because
Y/M=G/P
is proper.

Condition (iii) holds because the map in question is schematic and smooth. Indeed, the map

G\times Y\to Y\times Y

is smooth because the action of
G
on
Y
is transitive.

Finally, condition (iv) holds because
Y/G=\operatorname{pt}/N(P)
is smooth and safe, hence it is mock-smooth.

2.6.14.

Thus, by Corollary
2.6.11
, we conclude that for
Y=G/N(P)
, the resulting object of
\operatorname{Arr}(2\operatorname{-AGCat})

is 2-dualizable.

We will apply to this example the paradigm of Sect.
1.8.4
and in particular
Corollary
1.8.7
in order to obtain Deligne-Lusztig representations, and compute their
traces.

3.
Traces of categorical representations

In this section we will be concerned with calculating traces (in the ambient 3-category
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
) of the objects
of the form
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, equipped with endomorphisms (and between morphisms between these traces).

Note that the above traces are objects of

\Omega(2\operatorname{-AGCat})={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{2\operatorname{-AGCat}}({\mathbf{1}}_{2\operatorname{-AGCat}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}_{2\operatorname{-AGCat}}(\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq\operatorname{AGCat}.

3.1.
The trace on
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

3.1.1.

Let us be given a prestack
{\mathcal{X}}
acted on by
G\times G
, and thereby giving rise to a 1-morphism

F_{\mathcal{X}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

see Sect.
2.5.1
.

We claim:

Proposition 3.1.2
.

The object

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\mathcal{X}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\in\operatorname{AGCat}

identifies canonically with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}/\Delta(G)),

where
G
acts on
{\mathcal{X}}
via the diagonal embedding
G\to G\times G
.

Proof.

Let
H
be a commutative Hopf algebra in a symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{O}}
. Let us view
H{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})

as an object of the symmetric monoidal (2-)category
{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
. Let
M
be an
H
-bimodule, and let

F_{M}:H{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})\to H{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}})

be the corresponding functor, viewed as a (1-)morphism in
{\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
. Then

(3.1)

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{M},H{\operatorname{-mod}})\simeq M\underset{H}{\otimes}{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}},

where:

•

H
acts on
M
via the diagonal map
H\otimes H
, followed by the antipode on one of the factors;

•

{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{O}}
is viewed as a trivial
H
-module.

Applying this to
{\mathbf{O}}=\operatorname{AGCat}
and
H=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
, we obtain that

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\mathcal{X}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\mathbf{coinv}_{\Delta(G)}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}})).

Combining with Proposition
2.3.8
, we rewrite the latter expression as

\mathbf{inv}_{\Delta(G)}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}))\overset{\text{\eqref{e:inv as quot}}}{\simeq}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}/\Delta(G)).

∎

3.1.3.

Let
\phi
be an endomorphism of
G
as an algebraic group. Take
{\mathcal{Y}}:=G_{\phi}
, which equals
G
as a scheme,
with right copy of
G
acting by right multiplication, and the left copy acting by left multiplication precomposed
with
\phi
.

We will denote the resulting endofunctor of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
by
F_{\phi}
. We can view it as follows:

•

When we think of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
as
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
,
F_{\phi}
is given by precomposing
the action with the endomorphism of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
given by
\phi_{*}
;

•

When we think of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
as
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
,
F_{\phi}
is given by composing
the coaction with the endomorphism of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
given by
\phi^{!}
.

Note that the resulting diagonal action of
G
on
G_{\phi}
is the action of
G
on itself by
\phi
-twisted
conjugacy:

\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(g)(g_{1}):=\phi(g)\cdot g_{1}\cdot g^{-1}.

Hence, in this particular case of Proposition
3.1.2
says:

Corollary 3.1.4
.

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G)).

3.1.5.

Let us specialize to the case when
\phi=\operatorname{id}
. We obtain:

Corollary 3.1.6
.

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

3.1.7.

Let now the ground field
k
be
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
, and assume that
G
is defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, so that
it carries an action of the geometric Frobenius, denoted
{\operatorname{Frob}}
. We denote the endofunctor

F_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}
of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
simply by
{\operatorname{Frob}}
.

From Corollary
3.1.4
we obtain:

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\operatorname{Frob}}(G)).

3.1.8.

Assume now that
G
is connected. In this case, Lang’s theorem implies that the
\operatorname{Ad}_{\operatorname{Frob}}(G)
-action is
transitive, and

G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\operatorname{Frob}}(G)\simeq\operatorname{pt}/G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}).

We now observe:

Lemma 3.1.9
.

For a finite group
\Gamma
, the canonical map

{\mathbf{i}}({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\Gamma))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(\operatorname{pt}/\Gamma)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We have to show that for a scheme
X
, the (fully faithful) functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\Gamma)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}((\operatorname{pt}/\Gamma)\times X)

is an equivalence.

Note, however, that every object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}((\operatorname{pt}/\Gamma)\times X)
splits canonically
as

\underset{\rho}{\oplus}\,(\rho\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{\rho}),

where
\rho
runs over the set of isomorphism classes of irreducible representations of
\Gamma
.

This makes the assertion manifest.
8
8

8

In Corollary
4.4.7
we will show that a generalization of Lemma
3.1.9

holds for any algebraic stack that has finitely many isomorphism classes of points.

∎

3.1.10.

Summarizing, we obtain:

Corollary 3.1.11
.

Let
G
be connected. Then

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq{\mathbf{i}}({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})):=\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

3.2.
Morphisms between traces

3.2.1.

Let us now be given a pair of groups
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, each equipped with a 1-endomorphism of

F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{i}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{i})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, given by a
(G_{i}\times G_{i})
-prestack
{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
. Let us also be given a 1-morphism

T_{\mathcal{Y}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

given by a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
equipped with an action of
G_{1}\times G_{2}
.
Let us assume that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is AG tame, which implies that
T_{\mathcal{Y}}
is adjointable.

Finally, let us be given a 2-morphism in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}

T_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{1}}\overset{\alpha_{\mathcal{Z}}}{\Rightarrow}F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{2}}\circ T_{\mathcal{Y}},

given by pull-push along to a stack
{\mathcal{Z}}
acted on by
G_{1}\times G_{2}
and equipped with

(G_{1}\times G_{2})
-equivariant maps

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Z}}@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{G_{2}}{\times}{\mathcal{X}}_{2}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{X}}_{1}\overset{G_{1}}{\times}{\mathcal{Y}}.\end{CD}

3.2.2.

We wish to calculate

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha_{\mathcal{Z}},T_{\mathcal{Y}}):\operatorname{Tr}(F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{1}},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\to\operatorname{Tr}(F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{2}},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}),

which is a 1-morphism in
\operatorname{AGCat}

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}_{1}/\Delta(G_{1}))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}_{2}/\Delta(G_{2})).

3.2.3.

Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}
denote the fiber product

{\mathcal{Z}}\underset{({\mathcal{X}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}})/\Delta(G_{1})}{\times}({\mathcal{X}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

It is acted on by
G_{1}\times G_{1}\times G_{2}
. Consider the action on it of
G_{1}\times G_{2}
,
where
G_{1}
acts via its diagonal embedding into
G_{1}\times G_{1}
.

Define the
(G_{1}\times G_{2})
-stack
\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}
similarly. We have naturally defined

(G_{1}\times G_{2})
-equivariant maps

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}\leftarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2},

where each factor of
G_{1}\times G_{2}
acts on
{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
diagonally.

Finally, set

{\mathcal{W}}:=\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{1}\underset{{\mathcal{Z}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}{\times}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{2}.

This is a stack, equipped with an action of
G_{1}\times G_{2}
. The projection

{\mathcal{W}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{i}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{i}

ia
G_{i}
-equivariant (for the diagonal action of
G_{i}
on
{\mathcal{X}}_{i}
).

3.2.4.

We claim:

Proposition 3.2.5
.

Under the above circumstances, the 1-morphism

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}_{1}/\Delta(G_{1}))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{X}}_{2}/\Delta(G_{2}))

corresponding to

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha_{\mathcal{Z}},T_{\mathcal{Y}})
is given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{W}}/(G_{1}\times G_{2})@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{X}}_{2}/\Delta(G_{2})\\
@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{X}}_{1}/\Delta(G_{1}).\end{CD}

Proof.

Follows by applying (
1.9
) using the calculation of the right adjoints to
F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{i}}
given by
Sect.
2.5.6
.

∎

3.2.6.

Recall that
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is self-dual as an object of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
. Moreover, the unit and counit for this self-duality are
adjointable.

Let
\phi
be an endomorphism of
G
. By Lemma
1.3.5
, we obtain that

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))

is dualizable, and its dual identifies with

\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}).

3.2.7.

Note that, by Sect.
2.5.5
, the endomorphism
(F_{\phi})^{R}
is represented by
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((G_{\phi})^{R})
,
where
(G_{\phi})^{R}
is a copy of
G
, on which:

•

The left action of
G
is given by left multiplication;

•

The right action of
G
is given by right multiplication, precomposed with
\phi
.

Hence,

\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}^{\prime}_{\phi}(G)),

where

\operatorname{Ad}^{\prime}_{\phi}(g)(g_{1})=g\cdot g_{1}\cdot\phi(g^{-1}).

Note, however, that the inversion operation on
G
identifies

G/\operatorname{Ad}^{\prime}_{\phi}(G)\simeq G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G),

and hence we obtain:

\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G)).

3.2.8.

Unwinding, from Proposition
3.2.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 3.2.9
.

The resulting identification

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))

is the (Verdier duality) identification of
[
GRV
, Sect. 3.7]
.

3.2.10.

Consider now the 1-morphism

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}.

It is given by
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{pt}
, viewed as a prestack acted on by
G
.

Let
\phi
be an endomorphism of
G
. We have a tautological natural transformation

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}\circ F_{\phi}\overset{\alpha}{\to}\mathbf{coinv}_{G}:

(3.2)

\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat}}
\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat},}
\scriptstyle{F_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{coinv}_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\mathbf{coinv}_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

Consider the resulting functor

(3.3)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\phi,G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha,\mathbf{inv}_{G})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}.

Unwinding, from Proposition
3.2.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 3.2.11
.

The functor (
3.3
) identifies with

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G),-):\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}.

3.3.
The Grothendieck-Springer and Jacquet functors

In this subsection we take
G
to be a connected reductive group.

3.3.1.

Let
P\subset G
be a parabolic subgroup, and let
M
be the Levi quotient of
P
. Let us denote by

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

the 1-morphism given by the
(G\times M)
-scheme
G/N(P)
.

3.3.2.

Consider the corresponding 1-morphism

(3.4)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M))\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\mathbf{ind}}_{P})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

We claim:

Proposition 3.3.3
.

The 1-morphism (
3.4
) identifies canonically with the Grothendieck-Springer functor

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}P/\operatorname{Ad}(P)@>{{\mathsf{p}}}>{}>G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{{\mathsf{q}}}V{}V\\
M/\operatorname{Ad}(M).\end{CD}

Proof.

We apply Proposition
3.2.5
to the case when
F_{{\mathcal{X}}_{i}}
is the identity 1-morphism,
and the maps

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{Z}}@>{}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{G_{2}}{\times}G_{2}@>{\simeq}>{}>{\mathcal{Y}}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}\overset{G_{1}}{\times}G_{1}\\
@V{\simeq}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{Y}}\end{CD}

are the identity maps.

The resulting 1-morphism
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{1}/\operatorname{Ad}(G_{1}))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G_{2}/\operatorname{Ad}(G_{2}))
is given by pull-push along the diagram
