\begin{CD}\left((G_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}{\times}(G_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\right)/(G_{1}\times G_{2})@>{}>{}>G_{2}/\operatorname{Ad}(G_{2})\\
@V{}V{}V\\
G_{1}/\operatorname{Ad}(G_{1}).\end{CD}

Suppose now that the action of
G_{1}\times G_{2}
on
{\mathcal{Y}}
is transitive. Fix a point
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
, and
let
P:=\operatorname{Stab}_{G_{1}\times G_{2}}(y)
. In this case, we can rewrite the fiber product

\left((G_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\underset{{\mathcal{Y}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}{\times}(G_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}})\right)/(G_{1}\times G_{2})

as
P/\operatorname{Ad}(P)
.

We apply this to the case
G_{1}=M
,
G_{2}=G
and
{\mathcal{Y}}=G/N(P)
, and the assertion of the proposition follows.

∎

3.3.4.
Trace and inversion

Recall that
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is self-dual as an object of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
. Hence, we obtain a canonical
identification

(3.5)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Tr of dual}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},(G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:G mod self-dual}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

It is easy to see that the map (
3.5
) is induced by the
inversion
on
G
, to be denoted

\sigma_{G}
.

Note that with respect to the identifications

(G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee}\simeq G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\text{ and }(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee}\simeq M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

we have

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\vee}\simeq\mathbf{jacq}_{P}.

Hence, we obtain a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))@>{\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{ind}}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},(({\mathbf{ind}}_{P})^{R})^{\vee})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},(G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})^{\vee})\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{ind}}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))@>{\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),\end{CD}

in which the composite vertical arrows are given by
\sigma_{M}
and
\sigma_{G}
, respectively.

Thus, we obtain an isomorphism

(3.6)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{G}\simeq\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{G}\circ\sigma_{M}.

Note, however, that the pull-push functor in Proposition
3.3.3
is also equipped
with an isomorphism

(3.7)

{\mathsf{p}}_{*}\circ{\mathsf{q}}^{!}\simeq\sigma_{G}\circ({\mathsf{p}}_{*}\circ{\mathsf{q}}^{!})\circ\sigma_{M}.

It is easy to see that the isomorphisms (
3.6
) and
(
3.7
) are compatible via the identification of Proposition
3.3.3
.

3.3.5.
The intertwining operator

Let
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
be two parabolic subgroups, such that the projections

M_{1}\twoheadleftarrow P_{1}\hookleftarrow P_{1}\cap P_{2}\hookrightarrow P_{2}\twoheadrightarrow M_{2}

are surjective. Hence, these projections each identify
M_{i}
,
i=1,2
with the Levi quotient of
P_{1}\cap P_{2}
.
Denote this Levi quotient by
M
, so that we have

M_{1}\simeq M\simeq M_{2}.

We claim that there is a canonical 1-morphism in
\operatorname{Arr}(2\operatorname{-DGCat})

(3.8)

(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\to(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}).

Namely, the 1-morphisms

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)\text{ and }\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

are the identity ones,
and the 2-morphism

I_{P_{1},P_{2}}:{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}\Rightarrow{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}}

is given by pull-push along the following diagram
of
(M\times G)
-schemes

(3.9)

\begin{CD}G/N(P_{1}\cap P_{2})@>{r_{2}}>{}>G/N(P_{2})\\
@V{r_{1}}V{}V\\
G/N(P_{1}).\end{CD}

Thus, we indeed obtain the desired 1-morphism (
3.8
).

3.3.6.

One can imagine wanting to compare the Grothendieck-Springer functors for different choices of a parabolic with the same Levi
quotient, using the above 2-morphism
I_{P_{1},P_{2}}
.

The problem is, however, that
this 2-morphism is
not adjointable
, and so we cannot apply Sect.
1.5.1
.
In particular, it does
not
induce a natural transformation

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}\to\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}.

And indeed, it is know that no such (non-zero) natural transformation exists:
this is evidenced by
[
Gu
, Theorem E]
.

We will show how to remedy this by replacing the objects
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

and
M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
by their relatives, denoted
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
and
M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
,
respectively.

3.3.7.

Let us denote by

\mathbf{jacq}_{P}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

the 1-morphism
also
given by the variety
(G\times M)
-scheme
G/N(P)
.

3.3.8.

Consider the corresponding 1-morphism

(3.10)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G))\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\mathbf{jacq}_{P})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

We claim:

Proposition 3.3.9
.

The 1-morphism (
3.4
) identifies canonically with the restriction functor

{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)),

given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}P/\operatorname{Ad}(P)@>{{\mathsf{q}}}>{}>M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)\\
@V{{\mathsf{p}}}V{}V\\
G/\operatorname{Ad}(G).\end{CD}

Proof.

Obtained by reading the proof of Proposition
3.3.3
backwards.

∎

3.3.10.

Recall now that according to Sect.
2.6.13
, the 1-morphism
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
is 2-adjointable. Moreover,
its right adjoint is given by
\mathbf{jacq}_{P}
. Hence, by
Proposition
1.7.6
, we obtain that the functor (
3.10
) is the right adjoint of (
3.4
).

However, unwinding, we obtain:

Corollary 3.3.11
.

Under the identifications of Propositions
3.3.3
and
3.3.9
, the above
adjunction corresponds to the usual
(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P},{\mathfrak{r}}_{P})
-adjunction, given by
9
9

9

In the formula below, we have
{\mathsf{q}}^{*}\simeq{\mathsf{q}}^{!}
because
the morphism
{\mathsf{q}}
is smooth of relative dimension
0
.

({\mathsf{p}}_{*})^{R}\simeq({\mathsf{p}}^{!})\text{ and }({\mathsf{q}}^{!})^{R}\simeq({\mathsf{q}}^{*})^{R}\simeq{\mathsf{q}}_{*}.

3.4.
The Deligne-Lusztig functor

We continue to assume that
G
is a connected reductive group.

3.4.1.

Let
P
be a parabolic in
G
, and let
M
be its Levi quotient. Choose a Levi splitting

M\hookrightarrow P,

and let us assume that
M
, viewed as a subgroup of
G
, is defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.

Note that we are
not
assuming that
P
is defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
.
Let
P^{\prime}
be the preimage of
P
under the geometric Frobenius endomorphism
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
of
G
.

Let us observe that the parabolic subgroups
P
and
P^{\prime}
automatically satisfy
the conditions of Sect.
3.3.5
. Indeed,

M\subset P\cap P^{\prime}.

Note also that the geometric Frobenius
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}
on
M
can be identified with

M=P/N(P)\simeq P\cap P^{\prime}/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\simeq P^{\prime}/N(P^{\prime})\overset{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}}{\longrightarrow}P/N(P)=M.

3.4.2.

We now claim that geometric Frobenius on
G
defines a 1-endomorphism of the object

(3.11)

(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\in\operatorname{Arr}(2\operatorname{-AGCat}),

to be denoted
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
.

Namely, the 1-endomorphism
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
is the composition of

(3.12)

(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\to(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})

and

(3.13)

(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\to(M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\overset{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}),

where:

•

The 1-morphism (
3.12
) is given by the action of the geometric Frobenius on
G
, i.e., by the diagram

\textstyle{M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}}

where the 2-morphism is given by pushforward along

G/N(P^{\prime})\overset{M,{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}}{\times}M\to G/N(P),\quad(g,m)\mapsto{\operatorname{Frob}}(g)\cdot m;

•

The 1-morphism (
3.13
) is given by the 2-morphism
I_{P,P^{\prime}}
.

3.4.3.

In other words, in the datum of
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
, the 1-endomorphisms

M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\text{ and }G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

are given by
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}
and
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
, respectively.

The 2-morphism

(3.14)

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\Rightarrow{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}

is explicitly described as follows:

The left-hand side in (
3.14
) is given by the
(G\times M)
-scheme

(G/N(P)\times M)/M,

where the quotient is taken with respect to the action given by

m\cdot(g,m_{1})=(g\cdot m^{-1},{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}(m)\cdot m_{1}),

with
G
and
M
acting on the left and right naturally.

The right-hand side in (
3.14
) is given by
G/N(P)
, where the
G
-action
is given by left multiplication precomposed with
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
, and
M
acting naturally on the right.

The 2-morphism in (
3.14
) is given by the diagram

\begin{CD}(G/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\times M)/M@>{}>{}>(G/N(P^{\prime})\times M)/M@>{(g,m)\mapsto{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G/N(P)}(g)\cdot m}>{}>G/N(P)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(G/N(P)\times M)/M,\end{CD}

where:

•

The action of
G
is precomposed with
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
on the right-most term, and is the natural
action by left multiplication everywhere else;

•

The action of
M
is by right multiplication.

3.4.4.

Taking the trace of the above 1-endomorphism
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
of (
3.11
), we obtain an object of
\Omega(\operatorname{Arr}(2\operatorname{-AGCat}))
, i.e., a map

(3.15)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}).

Applying Corollary
3.1.11
, and using the fact that
{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
is fully faithful, we obtain a functor that we denote

\operatorname{DL}_{P}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

3.4.5.

Let us describe the functor
\operatorname{DL}_{P}
explicitly. Applying Proposition
3.2.5
, we obtain that it is given by the correspondence

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{W}}_{P}/(G\times M)@>{}>{}>G/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}}(G)@>{\sim}>{}>\operatorname{pt}/G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})\\
@V{}V{}V\\
M/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}}(M)\\
@V{\sim}V{}V\\
\operatorname{pt}/M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\end{CD}

where:

•

{\mathcal{W}}_{P}
is the variety

\{g\in G,\,m\in M,\,x\in G/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\,|\,r(x)={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}(g)\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G/N(P)}(r^{\prime}(x))\cdot m^{-1}\},

where

G/N(P)\overset{r}{\leftarrow}G/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\overset{r^{\prime}}{\to}G/N(P^{\prime});

•

The
G
-action on
{\mathcal{W}}_{P}
is given by

g_{1}\cdot(g,m,x)=(g_{1}\cdot g\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}(g_{1})^{-1},m,{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}(g_{1})\cdot x);

•

The
M
-action on
{\mathcal{W}}_{P}
is given by

m_{1}\cdot(g,m,x)=(g,m_{1}\cdot m\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}(m_{1})^{-1},x\cdot m_{1}^{-1}).

3.4.6.

Note that the fiber product

\operatorname{pt}\underset{\operatorname{pt}/G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})}{\times}{\mathcal{W}}_{P}/(G\times M)\simeq\operatorname{pt}\underset{G}{\times}({\mathcal{W}}_{P}/M)

is the usual parabolic Deligne-Lusztig variety

\mathcal{DL}_{P}:=\{m\in M,\,x\in G/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\,|\,r(x)={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G/N(P)}(r^{\prime}(x))\cdot m^{-1}\}/M\simeq\\
\simeq\{x\in G/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\,|\,r(x)={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G/N(P)}(r^{\prime}(x))\}/M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),

which is equipped with its natural projection to
\operatorname{pt}/M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

Note also that the projection

G/N(P\cap P^{\prime})\to G/(P\cap P^{\prime})

defines an isomorphism

\mathcal{DL}_{P}\overset{\sim}{\to}\{y\in G/(P\cap P^{\prime})\,|\,\overline{r}(y)={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G/P}(\overline{r}^{\prime}(y))\},\quad G/P\overset{\overline{r}}{\leftarrow}G/(P\cap P^{\prime})\overset{\overline{r}^{\prime}}{\to}G/P^{\prime}.

3.4.7.

In other words, the functor
\operatorname{DL}_{P}
is explicitly given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}\mathcal{DL}_{P}/G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{pt}/G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})\\
@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{pt}/M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}).\end{CD}

In other words, it sends
\rho\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))
to

(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{DL}_{P},\omega_{\mathcal{DL}_{P}})\otimes\rho)^{M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})}.

Remark 3.4.8
.

Note that what we call the Deligne-Lusztig functor differs from the usual convention by duality: the latter involves

\operatorname{C}_{c}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{DL}_{P})

rather than

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{DL}_{P},\omega_{\mathcal{DL}_{P}}).

3.4.9.

We now consider the object (
3.11
) as equipped with two 1-endomorphisms: one is
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
constructed
above, and the other is
\operatorname{Id}
. They are equipped with a natural datum of commutation (see Sect.
1.3.7
): indeed
any 1-endomorphism is equipped with a datum of commutation against the identity endomorphisms.

We would have liked to apply Corollary
1.8.7
and conclude that certain two maps

\operatorname{Funct}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))\to\operatorname{Funct}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))

are equal.

However, we cannot do this yet: namely, the endomorphism
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}}
is
not
adjointable
(see Sect.
1.8.5
for what this means). Namely, the 2-morphism (
3.14
)
is not adjointable.

In the next section we will remedy this by replacing
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

and
M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
by the objects
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
and
M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
,
mentioned above.

Remark 3.4.10
.

Recall that in the case of the identity endomorphism, the right adjoint
\mathbf{jacq}_{P}
of
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
induces a right
adjoint
{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}
of the Grothendieck-Springer functor
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}
.

We would have liked to produce a right adjoint to the Deligne-Lusztig functor, but at this stage we cannot do that:
we cannot apply Proposition
1.7.6
, because the corresponding 2-morphism
\alpha^{\operatorname{BC}}
(see Sect.
1.7.5
; here

\alpha
is (
3.14
)) does
not
admit a right adjoint.

This will also be remedied in the next section.

Remark 3.4.11
.

The failure of the independence of the Deligne-Lusztig functor of
P
happens
10
10

10

And such independence is obviously false:
for
M=T
and the trivial character, different choices of the Borel lead to DL-varieties of different dimensions.

for an even simpler reason (and cannot be remedied
by passing to the
T
-monodromic subcategories): the reason is that for two parabolics
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
,
the diagram of natural transformations

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}@>{I_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}}>{}>{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}@>{\sim}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}\\
@V{I_{P_{1},P_{2}}}V{}V@V{}V{I_{P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}}V@V{}V{I_{P_{1},P_{2}}}V\\
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}@>{I_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}}>{}>{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}_{2}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}@>{\sim}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}}\end{CD}

does
not
commute up to any 3-isomorphism. In fact, the diagram

(3.16)

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}@>{I_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}}>{}>{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}\\
@V{I_{P_{1},P_{2}}}V{}V@V{}V{I_{P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}}V\\
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}}@>{I_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}}>{}>{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}_{2}}\end{CD}

does
not
commute up to any 3-isomorphism.

Remark 3.4.12
.

That said, one can define a variant of the Deligne-Lusztig functor using
any
2-morphism

\widetilde{I}_{P,P^{\prime}}:{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}\to{\mathbf{ind}}_{P^{\prime}}

in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
, i.e., a 1-morphism

\widetilde{I}_{P,P^{\prime}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P^{\prime}))

in
(G\times M)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

One can replace the diagram (
3.16
) by a commutative diagram

(3.17)

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}@>{I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}}>{}>{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}\\
@V{I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}}V{}V@V{}V{I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}}V\\
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2}}@>{\widetilde{I}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}}>{}>{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{\prime}_{2}},\end{CD}

of 1-morphisms
between the spectrally finite subcategories
of the two sides for a suitably defined 1-morphism

\widetilde{I}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}
.

This will allow us to conclude that

\operatorname{DL}_{P_{1}}\simeq\widetilde{\operatorname{DL}}_{P_{2}},

where
\widetilde{\operatorname{DL}}_{P_{2}}
is a version of the Deligne-Lusztig functor constructed using
\widetilde{I}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}
.

3.5.
The class of an object

3.5.1.

Let
\phi
be an endomorphism of
G
. Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
be an object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, equipped with a 1-morphism

(3.18)

\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to F_{\phi}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}).

In other words,
\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}
is an endomorphism of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
that intertwines the action of
G

with one obtained by precomposition with
\phi
.

3.5.2.

We can think of this data as

(3.19)

\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat}}
\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat}}
\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{F_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}}

Assume that
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is dualizable. Then, using Sect.
2.5.3
and (
1.7
),
we obtain that (
3.19
) induces a map

\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}=\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{AGCat})\to\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G)).

I.e., we obtain an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))
, which we will denote by

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}).

3.5.3.

Consider the 1-morphism

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}.

Given a point
g\in G
, we endow
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}
with a natural transformation

(3.20)

\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat}}
\textstyle{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat},}
\scriptstyle{F_{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}}
\scriptstyle{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha_{g}}

given by the action of
g
.

From Proposition
3.2.5
we obtain that the resulting functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha_{g},{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}

is given by
\iota_{g}^{!}
, where
\iota_{g}
is the map

\operatorname{pt}\to G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

corresponding to
g
.

3.5.4.

Concatenating (
3.19
) and (
3.20
), we obtain:

Corollary 3.5.5
.

We have a canonical isomorphism (in
\operatorname{Vect}
):

\iota_{g}^{!}(\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(g\circ\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}).

3.5.6.

Let us now concatenate (
3.19
) with (
3.2
). We obtain:

Corollary 3.5.7
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G),\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}},\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})),

where by a slight abuse of notation we denote by the same symbol
\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}
the endofunctor of
\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

induced by
\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}
.

3.5.8.

Let
(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}})
and
(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}})
be a pair of objects as above.
It is clear that

(3.21)

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}}\otimes\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}})\simeq\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}})\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}}).

In particular, we obtain:

Corollary 3.5.9
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{\blacktriangle}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi}(G),\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}})\overset{!}{\otimes}\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2},\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}}\otimes\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}),

where by a slight abuse of notation we denote by the same symbol
\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}}\otimes\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}}
the endofunctor of

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}
induced by
\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}}\otimes\phi_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}}
.

3.5.10.

Here is an application of Corollary
3.5.9
to the theory of Deligne-Lusztig representations.

Let
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
be two maximal tori in
G
defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Let
\chi_{i}
be a character sheaf on
T_{i}
, equipped
with a Weil structure. Let
\chi^{0}_{i}
denote the corresponding character of
T_{i}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

Let
T_{i}\subset B_{i}
be Borel subgroups, and consider the corresponding Deligne-Lusztig representations

\operatorname{DL}_{B_{i}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}_{i}}),

where
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi_{i}^{0}}
denotes the 1-dimensional representation of
T_{i}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
corresponding to
the character
\chi_{i}^{0}
.

We claim:

Theorem 3.5.11
.

Suppose that
\chi^{-1}_{1}\neq\chi^{w}_{2}
for any element
w\in W
. Then

(\operatorname{DL}_{B_{1}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}_{1}})\otimes\operatorname{DL}_{B_{2}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}_{2}}))^{G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})}=0.

3.6.
Proof of Theorem
3.5.11

3.6.1.

Let
\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi_{i}}
be a copy of
\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}
, viewed as an object of
T_{i}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, with the
action given by
\chi_{i}
.

The Weil structure on
\chi_{i}
upgrades the identity endomorphism of
\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}
to a structure
(
3.18
) for
\phi={\operatorname{Frob}}_{T_{i}}
; denote it by
\alpha^{T}_{i}
.

Unwinding, we obtain:

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi_{i}},\alpha^{T}_{i})=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi_{i}^{0}}

as objects of

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{T_{i}},T_{i}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(T_{i}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

3.6.2.

Consider the object

{\mathbf{ind}}_{B_{i}}(\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi_{i}})\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

It identifies with

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}:=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T,\chi_{i}}.

The datum of
\alpha^{T}_{i}
, combined with the endomorphism
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{{\mathbf{ind}}_{B_{i}}}
,
(see Sect.
3.4.2
) equips
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i}
with a structure
(
3.18
) for
\phi={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
; denote it by
\alpha^{G}_{i}
.

By the compatibility of (
1.7
) with compositions, we have

\operatorname{DL}_{B_{i}}(\operatorname{cl}(\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi_{i}},\alpha_{i}^{T}))\simeq\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i},\alpha_{i}^{G}).

Hence,

\operatorname{DL}_{B_{i}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}_{i}})\simeq\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{i},\alpha_{i}^{G}).

3.6.3.

We now apply Corollary
3.5.9
. We obtain

(\operatorname{DL}_{B_{1}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}_{1}})\otimes\operatorname{DL}_{B_{2}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}_{2}}))^{G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})}\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha^{G}_{1}\otimes\alpha^{G}_{2},\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})).

We rewrite

\mathbf{coinv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})\simeq\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\otimes\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G\backslash(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T,\chi_{1}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T,\chi_{2}}))\simeq\\
\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{T\times T,(\chi^{-1}_{1},\chi_{2})}.

3.6.4.

Now, the condition that
\chi^{-1}_{1}\neq\chi^{w}_{2}
for any element
w\in W
implies that the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{T\times T,(\chi^{-1}_{1},\chi_{2})}
is zero.

∎[Theorem
3.5.11
]

3.7.
Iterated traces

3.7.1.

Let
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
be a pair of
commuting
endomorphisms of
G
. Consider the corresponding
endomorphisms
F_{\phi_{1}}
,
F_{\phi_{2}}
and
(F_{\phi_{2}})^{R}
of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

By Corollary
3.1.4
,

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi_{1}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G)).

We also have

\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi_{2}})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G)),

see Sect.
3.2.7
.

3.7.2.

The fact that
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
commute implies that we have a canonical isomorphism

F_{\phi_{2}}\circ F_{\phi_{1}}\overset{\beta}{\to}F_{\phi_{1}}\circ F_{\phi_{2}}.

By Proposition
3.2.5
, the map

\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi_{1}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\beta,F_{\phi_{2}})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi_{1}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})

identifies with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))\overset{\phi_{2}^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G)).

Similarly,

(\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi_{2}})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\beta^{\operatorname{BC}},F_{\phi_{1}})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi_{1}})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})

identifies with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))\overset{\phi_{1}^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G)),

where
\beta^{\operatorname{BC}}
is as in Sect.
1.3.7
.

3.7.3.

Now, by
[
GRV
, Corollary 5.2.7]
, we obtain:

\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{2}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G)))\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}},\omega_{(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}}}\right)

and

\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{1}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G)))\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}},\omega_{(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}}}\right).

Finally, we note

(3.22)

(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}}=((\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1}})^{\phi_{2}}=(\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}=((\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{2}})^{\phi_{1}}=(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}}.

3.7.4.

We claim:

Lemma 3.7.5
.

The diagram

(3.23)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\beta,F_{\phi_{2}}),\operatorname{Tr}(F_{\phi_{1}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}))@>{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:2 traces}}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\beta^{\operatorname{BC}},F_{\phi_{1}}),\operatorname{Tr}((F_{\phi_{2}})^{R},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}))\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{2}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G)))&&\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{1}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G)))\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{\sim}V{}V\\
\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}},\omega_{(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}}}\right)@>{\sim}>{\text{\eqref{e:double fixed points}}}>\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}},\omega_{(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}}}\right)\end{CD}

commutes.

Proof.

Unwinding, we identity the 2-morphism in both (
1.17
) and (
1.19
)
with

\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}},\omega_{(\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}}\right),

and these identifications are equal to the composition of the left vertical arrow
in (
3.23
) with

\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}},\omega_{(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{1}}(G))^{\phi_{2}}}\right)\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}},\omega_{(\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}}\right),

and the composition of the left vertical arrow in (
3.23
) with

\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}},\omega_{(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{\phi_{2}}(G))^{\phi_{1}}}\right)\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\blacktriangle}\left((\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}},\omega_{(\operatorname{pt}/G)^{\phi_{1},\phi_{2}}}\right),

respectively.

∎

3.7.6.

Let us see what the commutative diagram (
3.23
) gives when
\phi_{1}={\operatorname{Frob}}
and
\phi_{2}=\operatorname{Id}
.

We identify

(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(G))^{\operatorname{id}}\simeq(\operatorname{pt}/G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))^{\operatorname{id}}\simeq G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),

and it is easy to see that the identification of (
3.22
) corresponds to the canonical map

G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))\to(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q})\simeq(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))^{{\operatorname{Frob}}}.

The left vertical arrow in (
3.23
) is the map

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))\simeq\\
\simeq\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}K^{0}({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))\simeq\mathsf{Funct}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}),

where the last isomorphism sends the class of a finite-dimensional representation to its character (this is true for
G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
replaced
by any finite group
\Gamma
).

The right vertical arrow is the
Local Term
map
\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

of
[
GRV
, Sect. 5.2.4]
(this is true for
G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)
replaced by an arbitrary AG tame
algebraic stack equipped with an endomorphism).

3.7.7.

Hence, we obtain:

Corollary 3.7.8
.

The diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}))@>{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\operatorname{Frob}}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}))\\
@V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Frob}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Id}}}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))&&\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@V{\rm{taking\,\,characters}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}V\\
\mathsf{Funct}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})@>{\sim}>{}>\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\end{CD}

commutes.

4.
The condition of (quasi-)monodromicity

As was explained in the Introduction, in order to obtain a theory with interesting
representation-theoretic applications, we will need to replace the 2-category
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

by its full subcategory
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
. Its definition is based on the
notion of
monodromicity
, which we develop in this section.

Throughout this section, we will assume that
G
is connected.

4.1.
The category of quasi-monodromic sheaves on a group

4.1.1.

Let
X
be a connected scheme. Let

(4.1)

\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(X)

be the full subcategory, consisting of objects all of whose cohomologies (with respect to either the perverse or the usual
t-structure) are (possibly infinite) extensions of the (shifted) constant sheaf.

4.1.2.

We will be interested in the case when
X=G
is a group.

In Sect.
A.1.10
we will prove:

Lemma 4.1.3
.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
is compactly generated.
11
11

11

Note, however, that the embedding (
4.1
) does
not

preserves compactness.

4.1.4.

We claim:

Lemma 4.1.5
.

For a pair of groups
G_{1},G_{2}
, the functor

\boxtimes:\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1})^{\operatorname{q-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G_{2})^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2})

is an equivalence onto

\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2})^{\operatorname{q-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2}).

The lemma will be proved in Sect.
A.3
.

Remark 4.1.6
.

One can formulate statements parallel to Lemmas
4.1.3
and
4.1.5
, when
instead of group(s) we have arbitrary (connected) scheme(s). However, we are not sure whether the
corresponding statements are true.

That said, they are true for
simply-connected
schemes; in fact our proofs apply in this
case.

4.1.7.

Since the !-pullback functor (along a smooth morphism) maps the q-constant categories
to one another, from Lemma
4.1.5
we obtain:

Corollary 4.1.8
.

The functor

\operatorname{mult}^{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(G)\to\operatorname{Shv}(G\times G)

induces a comonoidal structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
.

4.1.9.

Let
\chi
be a multiplicative 1-dimensional local system (a.k.a., abelian character sheaf) on
G
. Let

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)

be the full subcategory equal to the essential image of

(-)\otimes\chi:\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G).

It follows from Corollary
4.1.8
that the functor
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
induces a comonoidal
structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}
.

4.1.10.
Example

Here is a typical example of an object in
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
: we take the underlying object of
\operatorname{DGCat}
to be
\operatorname{Vect}
,
with the coaction functor

\operatorname{Vect}\to\operatorname{Vect}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}

given by
\chi
.

Denote this object by
\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi}
.

Remark 4.1.11
.

It is
not
true that
\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi}
generates
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
, i.e.,
the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod},\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi},-):\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\to\operatorname{DGCat}

is
not
necessarily conservative.

A parallel fact is true once we replace
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}
by a closely related category
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}
(see Sect.
4.6.1
).

4.1.12.

Set

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}:=\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi},

where the direct sum is taken over the set of (isomorphism classes of)
\chi
’s.

Note that the tautological functor

(4.2)

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G)

is fully faithful:

It is clear that
\chi_{1}\neq\chi_{2}\,\Rightarrow\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)}(\chi_{1},\chi_{2})=0
, and then we use the fact that

\operatorname{Shv}(G)
is left-complete in its t-structure, see
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 1.1.6]
.

4.1.13.

By Corollary
4.1.8
, we obtain that the functor
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
induces a comonoidal
structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
.

In addition, the
\overset{!}{\otimes}
operation makes
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}

into a symmetric monoidal category. These two structures combine to a commutative Hopf algebra
structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
.

4.1.14.

Note that we have an equivalence in
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
:

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\simeq\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat}).

4.1.15.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)

be the full subcategory, consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)
, for which

\operatorname{mult}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})
belongs to the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G\times G).

By Corollary
4.1.8
, we have

(4.3)

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}

In Sect.
A.5
we will prove:

Theorem 4.1.16
.

The inclusion (
4.3
) is an equivalence.

4.2.
Quasi-monodromic categorical representations

4.2.1.

Denote

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}:={\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

Note that since
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
is dualizable, we can identify

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\simeq\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}\left(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\right).

By a slight abuse of notation, we will denote by
{\mathbf{i}}
the (fully faithful functor)

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}.

4.2.2.
Example

We have

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi})=\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi},

where the latter is as in Sect.
3.6.1
.

4.2.3.

Denote

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}:=\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\simeq{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

Note that we can identify

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\simeq\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})),

and let us denote by
{\mathbf{i}}
the corresponding (fully faithful) functor

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}.

4.2.4.

Note now that by the construction of the comonoidal structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
we have a naturally defined map
of coalgebras in
\operatorname{AGCat}
:

(4.4)

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

Hence, we obtain a functor

(4.5)

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

Remark 4.2.5
.

In fact, the map (
4.4
) is compatible with commutative Hopf algebra structures on the two sides.
This makes (
4.5
) into a symmetric monoidal functor.

4.2.6.

We claim:

Theorem 4.2.7
.

(a) The functor (
4.5
) is fully faithful.

(b) Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
be an object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, whose underlying object
of
\operatorname{AGCat}
is of the form
{\mathbf{i}}({\mathbf{C}})
, where
{\mathbf{C}}
is a
dualizable
DG category.
Then
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
lies in the essential image of the composition

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

We can restate Theorem
4.2.7
(b) as follows:

Corollary 4.2.8
.

An object
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, for which the underlying object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
is of
the form
{\mathbf{i}}({\mathbf{C}})
, where
{\mathbf{C}}
is a
dualizable
DG category, splits as a direct sum

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,{\mathbf{i}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\chi}),

where

{\mathbf{C}}^{\chi}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat}).

4.2.9.

We conjecture that the assertion of Theorem
4.2.7
(b) holds without the dualizability
assumption. Namely, we propose:

Conjecture 4.2.10
.

The essential image of the composition

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\to}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

consists of those objects of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, for which the underlying object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
lies
in the essential image of the functor
{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
.

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.2.7

4.3.1.
Proof of Theorem
4.2.7
(a)

To prove point (a), it suffices to show that for any
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
, the functor

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G)

is fully faithful.

We factor the above functor as

(4.6)

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\simeq(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)))(X)\to(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G))(X)\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G),

and we note that the third arrow is fully faithful for any
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
(since
{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
is fully faithful
and admits a right adjoint).

Hence, the required assertion follows from the next proposition, which will be proved in Sect.
A.4
:

Proposition 4.3.2
.

The functor

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)

is fully faithful for any
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
.

4.3.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.2.7
(b)

It suffices to show that if
{\mathbf{C}}
is equipped with a map (
2.9
) that makes
(
2.10
) commute, then the essential image of (
2.9
) lies
in

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\subset{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G),

provided that
{\mathbf{C}}
is dualizable.

I.e., it suffices to show that the functor

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes(\operatorname{Shv}(G)/\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}})\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\left(\operatorname{Shv}(G\times G)/(\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G))\right),

induced by
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
, is conservative.

The functor

\operatorname{Shv}(G)/\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G\times G)/(\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G))

is conservative by Theorem
4.1.16
.

Now, for any conservative functor
{\mathbf{D}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{2}
, the functor

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{D}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{D}}_{2}

stays conservative if
{\mathbf{C}}
is dualizable: indeed, we can rewrite
{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\mathbf{D}}
as

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\mathbf{C}}^{\vee},{\mathbf{D}}).

∎

4.4.
The maximal quasi-monodromic subcategory

In this subsection we will show that given an object
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, there is a well-defined operation that extracts its
maximal quasi-monodromic subobject.

4.4.1.

Consider again the functor
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
of (
4.5
). We claim that it admits a right adjoint, to be denoted

(4.7)

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\mapsto\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}.

Namely, the category
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}(X)

is the full subcategory of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)
that consists of objects
{\mathbf{c}}
for which

\operatorname{coact}_{X}({\mathbf{c}})\in\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G)

belongs to the full subcategory

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\subset\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}(X\times G)

(see (
4.6
) above).

The tautological embedding

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

is the counit of the
(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}},(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}})^{R})
-adjunction.

The unit of the adjunction is an isomorphism, since
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
is fully faithful.

4.4.2.

Let us describe the functor (
4.7
) in the geometric situation.

For a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
equipped with an action of
G
, denote by

(4.8)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

the full subcategory consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}
, for which

\operatorname{act}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}),\quad\operatorname{act}:G\times{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}

lies in the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G\times{\mathcal{Y}}).

It is clear that (for any
{\mathcal{Y}}
), the object
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
naturally upgrades to an object of

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
.

4.4.3.

For
{\mathcal{Y}}
as above, consider
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
. Consider the corresponding object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

see (
4.7
).

It is clear that for
X\in\operatorname{Sch}
,

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}(X)=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}

as subcategories of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})(X)=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X).

4.4.4.

We will now describe the sub-object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\subset\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})

in a particular situation.

Proposition 4.4.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{Y}}
is algebraic stack on which
G
acts with finitely many orbits. Then the natural map

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}

is an isomorphism. In particular, the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}},\quad X\in\operatorname{Sch}

is an equivalence.

4.4.6.

Note that as a particular case (for
G=1
) we obtain:

Corollary 4.4.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be an algebraic stack with finitely many isomorphism classes of points. Then the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})\in\operatorname{AGCat}

is restricted, i.e., the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X),\quad X\in\operatorname{Sch}

is an equivalence.

4.4.8.

As another particular case, for
{\mathcal{Y}}=G
equipped with the left action on itself, we obtain:

Corollary 4.4.9
.

The object
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}(X):=\operatorname{Shv}(X\times G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}

is restricted, i.e., the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}},\quad X\in\operatorname{Sch}

is an equivalence.

4.5.
Proof of Proposition
4.4.5

4.5.1.

We have to show that for any scheme
X
, the map

(4.9)

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}

is an equivalence. Since the functor in question is fully faithful, we only have to establish the essential surjectivity.

By devissage, we can assume that
G
acts transitively on
{\mathcal{Y}}
, i.e.,

{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq G/H,

where
H
is an algebraic group that maps to
G
(not necessarily injectively). We will use this assumption
later in the proof.

4.5.2.

It suffices to show that the right adjoint of (
4.9
) is conservative. Consider the right
adjoint
\boxtimes^{R}
of the functor

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X).

We claim that it sends
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)
,
thereby providing a right adjoint to (
4.9
).

Indeed, this follows from the fact that the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)@<{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\boxtimes^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)@<{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\boxtimes^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(G\times{\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)@<{\boxtimes^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(G\times{\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\\
@A{\operatorname{act}^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}A{}A@A{}A{(\operatorname{act}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)@<{\boxtimes^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\end{CD}

commutes (the commutation of the bottom square is obtained by passing to the left adjoints along all arrows, and the commutation
of the middle square is Lemma
4.5.7
below).

Thus, it remains to show that the functor
\boxtimes^{R}
is conservative when restricted to
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
.

4.5.3.

Let us compose the functor
\boxtimes^{R}
with the functor

(\iota_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}})^{!}\otimes\operatorname{Id}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X),

where
\iota_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}}
is the map
\operatorname{pt}\to G/H\simeq{\mathcal{Y}}
corresponding to the unit point.

This composition is isomorphic to

(\iota_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X)

(one sees that by passing to left adjoints).

Hence, it is sufficient to show that the functor
(\iota_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}
is conservative, when restricted to
the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
.

4.5.4.

Let us now take
{\mathcal{Y}}=G/H
. We can rewrite the functor
(\iota_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}
as

(\iota_{1,G}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}\circ(\operatorname{p}\times\operatorname{id})^{!},\quad p:G\to G/H\simeq{\mathcal{Y}}.

The functor
(p\times\operatorname{id})^{!}
is conservative. We claim that it sends
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
to

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X).

Indeed, this follows by rewriting
(p\times\operatorname{id})^{!}
as

(\operatorname{id}_{G}\times\iota_{1,{\mathcal{Y}}}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}\circ(\operatorname{act}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}.

Hence, we are reduced to showing that the functor

(\iota_{1,G}\times\operatorname{id})^{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X)

is conservative, when restricted to
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)
.

4.5.5.

Note, however, that the latter functor identifies with

\iota_{1,G}^{!}\otimes\operatorname{id}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}.

Now the assertion follows from the fact that
\iota_{1,G}^{!}
is conservative on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
: indeed, since

\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is dualizable, tensoring by it preserves conservativity.

∎[Proposition
4.4.5
]

4.5.6.

We claim:

Lemma 4.5.7
.

For a triple of tame prestacks stacks
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1},{\mathcal{Y}}_{2},{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{3})@<{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\boxtimes^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{3})@<{\boxtimes^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{3}),\end{CD}

which a priori commutes up to a natural transformation, actually commutes.

Proof.

The tameness assumption readily reduces the assertion to the case when
{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}=Y_{i}
are schemes. Fix objects

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Y_{1}),\,\,{\mathcal{F}}_{23}\in\operatorname{Shv}(Y_{2}\times Y_{3}),\,\,{\mathcal{F}}_{12}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),\,\,{\mathcal{F}}_{3}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{3}).

It suffices to show that the map

(4.10)

\langle{\mathcal{F}}_{12}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{3},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes(\boxtimes\circ\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}_{23}))\rangle_{Y_{1}\times Y_{2}\times Y_{3}}\to\langle{\mathcal{F}}_{12}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{3},\boxtimes\circ\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{23})\rangle_{Y_{1}\times Y_{2}\times Y_{3}}

is an isomorphism, where

\langle-,-\rangle_{Y}:\operatorname{Shv}(Y)\times\operatorname{Shv}(Y)\to\operatorname{Vect}

denotes the Verdier duality pairing.

We rewrite the left-hand side of (
4.10
) as

(4.11)

\langle(p_{2})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{12}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{3},\boxtimes\circ\boxtimes^{R}({\mathcal{F}}_{23})\rangle_{Y_{2}\times Y_{3}}.

Note that for a pair of schemes
Z_{1}
and
Z_{2}
, the functors

\boxtimes:\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{1})\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{2})\leftrightarrows\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{1}\times Z_{2}):\boxtimes^{R}

are mutually
dual
under the Verdier duality identifications

\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{i})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(Z_{i}).

(Indeed, this is a formal consequence of the fact that the functor
\boxtimes
commutes with Verdier duality on compact objects.)

Hence, we can rewrite (
4.11
) as

(4.12)

\langle(p_{2})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}_{12}\overset{!}{\otimes}p_{1}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}_{1}))\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{3},{\mathcal{F}}_{23}\rangle_{Y_{2}\times Y_{3}},

and further as

(4.13)

\langle{\mathcal{F}}_{12}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{3},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{23}\rangle_{Y_{1}\times Y_{2}\times Y_{3}}.

Similarly, we rewrite right-hand side of (
4.10
) as

\langle{\mathcal{F}}_{12}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{3},{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{23}\rangle_{Y_{1}\times Y_{2}\times Y_{3}},

which is the same as (
4.13
).

Unwinding, we obtain that this is the same identification as the one induced by the natural transformation.

∎

4.6.
Quasi-monodromic vs monodromic

In this subsection we introduce a variant of the “quasi-constant/quasi-monodromic” condition, which
exhibits better functorial properties.

We note, however, that when
G=T
is a torus (which is the case of primary interest), the two conditions
are the same.

4.6.1.

Let

(4.14)

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)

be the full DG subcategory generated under colimits by the constant sheaf.

We have an inclusion

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}},

but it is
not
an equality as long as
G
has a non-trivial semi-simple part, see Sect.
A.1.10
.

4.6.2.

We have the following counterpart of Lemma
4.1.5
:

Lemma 4.6.3
.

For a pair of groups
G_{1},G_{2}
, the functor

\boxtimes:\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G_{2})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2})

is an equivalence onto

\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2}).

Proof.

The functor in question is fully faithful, preserves compacts, and
sends the compact generator of the left-hand side to the
compact generator of the right-hand side.

∎

4.6.4.

The material in Sects.
4.1
-
4.2
and
4.4
-
4.5
readily adapts to the present
context. In particular:

•

The functor
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
induces comonoidal structures on

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}:=\chi\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}:=\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi};

•

Consider the (symmetric monoidal) category

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}:=\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}\simeq{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq\\
\simeq\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})).

We have the fully faithful embeddings

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\hookrightarrow}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}\overset{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod};

•

The functor
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}
admits a right adjoint

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\mapsto\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}

and the counit of the adjunction

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

is fully faithful;

•

For a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
with an action of
G
, the subcategory

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}(\operatorname{pt})\subset\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})(\operatorname{pt})=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

equals

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}),

i.e., it consists of those
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
for which

\operatorname{act}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G\times{\mathcal{Y}});

•

A counterpart of Proposition
4.4.5
holds: if
{\mathcal{Y}}
is an algebraic stack with finitely many
G
-orbits, then
the natural map

(4.15)

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}

is an isomorphism.

We now highlight some points of difference between the “almost” and “quasi” situations.

4.6.5.

We claim that for a given character sheaf
\chi
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
is
generated by
\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi}
.

In fact, we claim that for any
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
, we have

(4.16)

{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi}\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat}),\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi},{\mathbf{C}}),

where we identify

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat}),\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi},\operatorname{Vect}^{\chi}),

(see
[
Ga
, Theorem B.4.3]
for the proof).

It follows formally that
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}
is generated by
\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi}
and for
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{alm-}\!\chi}
we have

(4.17)

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi}\underset{{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G))}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}),

4.6.6.

For
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
, the right adjoint,
\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}
of the forgetful functor

(4.18)

\mathbf{inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}}):=\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat}),\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Vect},{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}}

is conservative. This follows formally from (
4.16
).

In particular the essential image of (
4.18
) generates the target.

It follows formally that for
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, the essential image of

\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,\mathbf{inv}_{G,\chi}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})(X)\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}(X),\quad X\in\operatorname{Sch}

generates the target, where

\mathbf{inv}_{G,\chi}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}):=\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Vect}}^{\chi}).

4.6.7.

Applying Sect.
4.6.6
to
{\mathbf{C}}:=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
, where
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a prestack with an action of
G
, we obtain that the essential
image of

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/G)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{G}:=\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})):=\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))(\operatorname{pt})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}

generates the target, where

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}

is the direct summand corresponding to the trivial
\chi
.

Similarly, the essential image of

\underset{\chi}{\oplus}\,\mathbf{inv}_{G,\chi}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}

generates the target, where

\mathbf{inv}_{G,\chi}(\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})):=\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{G,\chi}.

4.7.
The existence of left adjoints

In this subsection we prove (a seemingly, technical) assertion that says that the
\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{!}
-averaging functor
is defined on the
G
-monodromic subcategory. We will need this assertion in the sequel.

That said, this subsection can be skipped on the first pass, and returned to when necessary.

4.7.1.

Consider

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}:={\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}({\mathbf{C}})\text{ and }{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\mathbf{inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}})

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\to\operatorname{DGCat}.

They are connected by the usual adjunction

(4.19)

\operatorname{oblv}_{G}:\mathbf{inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}})\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{C}}:\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}.

4.7.2.

However, we claim:

Proposition 4.7.3
.

The natural transformation
\operatorname{oblv}_{G}
in (
4.19
) admits a
left
adjoint when restricted
to

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat}),

Furthermore, we have

\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{!}\simeq\operatorname{Av}^{G}_{*}[\dim(G_{\operatorname{red}})],

where
G_{\operatorname{red}}
denotes the reductive quotient of
G
.

4.7.4.

Before we prove this proposition, let us see how it applies to
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.
Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a prestack equipped with an action of
G
; let
\pi_{\mathcal{Y}}
denote the projection

{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}/G
.

Consider the functor

(\pi_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}/G).

For a map
f:{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, we have the Beck-Chevalley natural transformations

(4.20)

(\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})_{!}\circ f_{*}\to(f/G)_{*}\circ(\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})_{!}\text{ and }(\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}})_{!}\circ f^{!}\to(f/G)^{!}\circ(\pi_{{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}})_{!}

We obtain:

Corollary 4.7.5
.

(a)
The natural transformations (
4.20
) are isomorphisms when restricted to

\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})^{\operatorname{mon}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{1})\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2})^{\operatorname{mon}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}_{2}),

respectively.

(b) There exists a canonical isomorphism

(\pi_{\mathcal{Y}})_{!}\simeq(\pi_{\mathcal{Y}})_{*}[\dim(G_{\operatorname{red}})]

as functors
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})
.

4.7.6.
Proof of Proposition
4.7.3

It is clear that for the proof of the proposition, we can replace
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
by
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
.

For
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
, by (
4.16
), we identify

{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)}{\otimes}\mathbf{inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}}).

In terms of this identification
\operatorname{oblv}_{G}
corresponds to the functor

{\mathbf{C}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)}{\otimes}\mathbf{inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}})\to\operatorname{Vect}\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)}{\otimes}\mathbf{inv}_{G}({\mathbf{C}}),

induced by

(4.21)

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)\overset{p^{*}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}.

4.7.7.

We identify

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}/G)\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G){\operatorname{-mod}},

where
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G)
is an associative algebra with respect to convolution.

In terms of this identification, (
4.21
) corresponds to the forgetful functor

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

The left and right adjoints of this functor are given by sending the 1-dimensional vector space to

\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G)\text{ and }(\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G))^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G),

respectively.

4.7.8.

This makes the assertion manifest, since

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)\simeq\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G)[-\dim(G_{\operatorname{red}})]

as
\operatorname{C}_{\cdot}(G)
-modules.

∎[Proposition
4.7.3
]

4.8.
The 2-adjointability of the embedding of the monodromic subcategory

We now come to the key point of difference between the monodoromic vs quasi-monodromic situations. Namely,
in this subsection we will show that the 1-morphism

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}\hookrightarrow G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is 2-adjointable, in contrast with
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}
, which is not in general 2-adjointable.

4.8.1.

Note now that since
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}
is generated by objects that are compact in
\operatorname{Shv}(G)
, for any scheme
X
, the embedding

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)

admits a right adjoint.
12
12

12

We emphasize again
that this is
not
the case for the corresponding functor
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{q-mon}}:\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G)
.

(We draw the reader’s attention to the notational distinction:
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}

denotes the 2-categorical functor, whereas
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}
denotes the 1-categorical one.)

Moreover, Verdier duality on
G\times X
induces an identification

(\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)^{\operatorname{mon}}.

and under the self-duality and the Verdier self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}(G\times X)
, the right adjoint of
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}

identifies with its dual.

The latter interpretation makes it clear that for a map
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
, the natural transformations

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes f)^{!}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\to(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times f)^{!}\text{ and }(\operatorname{Id}\otimes f)_{*}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\to(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times f)_{*}

are isomorphisms.

Hence, when we view
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}
as a map

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

in
\operatorname{AGCat}
, it admits a right adjoint (see
[
GRV
, Proposition 2.7.2]
).

4.8.2.

By duality, the structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}
of commutative Hopf algebra in
\operatorname{DGCat}
induces on
it a structure of cocommutative Hopf algebra. When viewed as such we will denote it by
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}
.

Taking the dual of the canonical map

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

we obtain a map

(4.22)

(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\to{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}),

compatible with the Hopf algebra structures.

The functor (
4.22
) admits a left adjoint, namely,

((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee})^{L}\simeq((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R})^{\vee}.

When we think of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}
)
as
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
(resp.,
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}
), then
((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee})^{L}
is simply
the original embedding

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

4.8.3.

Let us consider
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
and
{\mathbf{i}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})

as modules over
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
, where the action on
{\mathbf{i}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})
is
via
(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}
.

A priori, the functor
((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee})^{L}
is
left-lax
compatible with the actions
of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
. However, we claim:

Lemma 4.8.4
.

The structure on
((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee})^{L}
of left-lax compatibility with the
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}
-actions
is strict.

Proof.

By definition, we have to show that the natural transformation from

(4.23)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{mult}^{!}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

to

(4.24)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{mult}^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G\times G)=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\\
{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

is an isomorphism.

We rewrite (
4.23
) as

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{mult}^{!}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G\times G)=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\otimes(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G),

which is the same as the composition of (
4.24
) with

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

Thus, the statement is equivalent to the assertion that (
4.24
)
factors through the (fully faithful) embedding

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

By duality, this is equivalent to the statement that the functor

(4.25)

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{mult}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

factors via

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\twoheadrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}).

The key observation is that the functor (
4.25
) factors via

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

Hence, the functor (
4.25
) is isomorphic to the composition

(4.26)

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{mult}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

In its turn, (
4.26
) is isomorphic to

(4.27)

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\otimes(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}\\
