\to{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{mult}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G),

which is the same as

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\otimes{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{mult}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\\
\to{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}})\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G),

making the required factorization of (
4.25
) manifest.

∎

4.8.5.

Consider the adjunction

(4.28)

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}\leftrightarrows G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}:(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}.

We claim:

Proposition 4.8.6
.

The unit and counit of the adjunction in (
4.28
) admit right adjoints (i.e., the functor
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}

is 2-adjointable).

Proof.

We will think of

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
(resp.,
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}
) as

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\text{ and }{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}),

respectively.

In these terms, the functor
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}
is given by restriction along the homomorphism (
4.22
),
and its right adjoint is given by

\operatorname{Hom}_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}({\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}),-).

In terms of these identifications, the unit of the adjunction amounts to the equivalence

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}({\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}),\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}),\quad\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}),

which follows from the fact that

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})\to{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}})

is an equivalence (which in turn follows from the fact that the functor
(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}
admits a fully faithful (left) adjoint).

The counit of the adjunction is the functor

\operatorname{Hom}_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}({\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}),\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\simeq\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\quad\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}),

given by precomposition with
(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}
.

This functor admits a right adjoint, given by precomposition with
((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee})^{L}
, which is well-defined thanks
to Lemma
4.8.4
.

∎

4.8.7.

Concretely, Proposition
4.8.6
means that the for
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, the tautological embedding

(4.29)

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

admits a right adjoint.

The idempotent comonad
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}
of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is given by
the action of the idempotent

((\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee})^{L}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)})\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}.

From here we obtain:

Lemma 4.8.8
.

Suppose that
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is dualizable. Then so is
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
, and we have a canonical
identification

(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}\simeq(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee})^{\operatorname{mon}}

so that

(\operatorname{emb}_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{\vee}\simeq(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}})^{R}.

4.8.9.

In particular, taking
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})
for a prestack
{\mathcal{Y}}
acted on by
G
, we obtain that the (fully faithful)
1-morphism

(4.30)

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})

admits a right adjoint.

Evaluating on
X=\operatorname{pt}
, we obtain a right adjoint to the fully faithful embedding

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Remark 4.8.10
.

The content of the existence of (
4.30
) is that the rights adjoints to

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X)^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}\times X),\quad X\in\operatorname{Sch}

are compatible with the
(-)^{!}
and
(-)_{*}
functors for maps
X_{1}\to X_{2}
.

That said, the last commutation is easy to see directly by passing to the left adjoints.

4.9.
The case of a torus

In this subsection, we will show that when
G=T
is a torus, there is no difference between the
“almost” and “quasi” conditions.

4.9.1.

We claim:

Proposition 4.9.2
.

Let
G=T
be a torus. Then the inclusion

(4.31)

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

is an equality.

As an immediate corollary, we obtain:

Corollary 4.9.3
.

The inclusion

(4.32)

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}

is an equality.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
4.9.2
.

4.9.4.

Note that the subcategories

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(T)

are compatible with the (perverse) t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(T)
.

Moreover, it is easy to see that the embedding (
4.32
) induces an equivalence
on the corresponding bounded subcategories. Hence, it induces an equivalence on the bounded
below categories (as both categories are right-complete in their t-structures).

Recall also (see
[
AGKRRV1
, Theorem 1.1.6]
) that
\operatorname{Shv}(T)
is left-complete in its t-structure. This
implies that
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
is also left-complete in its t-structure. Hence, the embedding
(
4.31
) identifies
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
with the left completion of
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
.

Therefore, the assertion of the proposition is equivalent to the statement that
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is actually
left-complete in its t-structure.

4.9.5.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
is compactly generated by the constant sheaf
\operatorname{const}_{T}
. Hence,

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(T){\operatorname{-mod}}.

We identify
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(T)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(V[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}
, where
V
is a finite-dimensional
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
-vector space
(placed in cohomological degree
0
); in fact
V=H^{1}(T,\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
.

The functor of !-fiber at
1\in T
identifies (up to a cohomological shift) with the functor

(4.33)

(-)\underset{\operatorname{Sym}(V[-1])}{\otimes}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell},\quad\operatorname{Sym}(V[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

4.9.6.

By Koszul duality, we can identify

\operatorname{Sym}(V[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)_{\{0\}},

where the subscript
\{0\}
indicates set-theoretic support at
0
.

In terms of this equivalence, the functor (
4.33
) corresponds to the functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)_{\{0\}}\hookrightarrow{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)\overset{\Gamma(V,-)}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Vect}.

In particular, the t-structure
13
13

13

Usual or perverse–the two differ by a cohomological shift on our subcategory.

on
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
corresponds (possibly, up to an overall cohomological shift)
to the t-structure on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)_{\{0\}}
,
induced by the natural t-structure on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)
.

4.9.7.

This makes the assertion manifest: the category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)
is left-complete in its t-structure,
and hence so is
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(V)_{\{0\}}
.

∎[Proposition
4.9.2
]

5.
Spectrally finite and restricted categorical representations

In this section we will finally define the subcategory

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\subset G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

and show that as an object of
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
, it is tensored up from
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

We will also show that the induction functor

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

has an additional adjointability property when restricted to
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
.

In this section we let
G
be a connected reductive group.

5.1.
The notion of restricted categorical representation

5.1.1.

We first consider the case when
G=T
is a torus. In this case we give the following definitions:

T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}};

T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}:=T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\operatorname{AGCat}}{\times}\operatorname{DGCat},

where

T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}\overset{{\mathbf{oblv}}_{G}}{\to}\operatorname{AGCat}\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{DGCat}.

In other words,
T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
is a full subcategory of
T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}
equal to the essential image
of the forgetful functor

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\to}{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

5.1.2.

We now consider the case of a general reductive group.

Notational convention:
We will denote by
N
the unipotent radical of (a chosen) Borel subgroup
B\subset G
.
We denote by
T
the quotient
B/N
; this is the (abstract) Cartan of
G
.

Notational convention:
For the rest of this paper (excluding the Appendix), the subscript “
\operatorname{mon}
” will refer to the condition
of monodromicity with respect to the Cartan subgroup of
G
(rather than
G
itself).

5.1.3.

We set

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\underset{T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}{\times}T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},

where
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is the functor
\mathbf{jacq}_{B}
.

We set

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}:=G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\underset{T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}{\times}T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}.

5.1.4.

Note that we can rewrite

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}=G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\underset{\operatorname{AGCat}}{\times}\operatorname{DGCat},

where

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\overset{\mathbf{inv}_{N}}{\to}\operatorname{AGCat}\overset{{\mathbf{i}}}{\leftarrow}\operatorname{AGCat}.

We claim:

Proposition 5.1.5
.

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
be dualizable. Then its belongs to
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
if and only if

\mathbf{inv}_{N}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})
is restricted as an object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
(i.e., belongs to the essential image of
the functor
{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
).

Proof.

Follows from Theorem
4.2.7
(b) (combined with Corollary
4.9.3
).

∎

Remark 5.1.6
.

Note that if we assume Conjecture
4.2.10
(for tori), then it would follow that the assertion of Proposition
5.1.5

holds without the assumption that
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
be dualizable.

5.1.7.
Examples

The objects
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/B)
and
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}

belong to
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
. If we tensor them
by an object
\underline{{\mathbf{D}}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
that is
not
in

\operatorname{DGCat}
, we obtain objects in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
that
are no longer in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
.

By contrast
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\notin G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
.

5.1.8.

It follows from Lemma
4.8.8
that if a dualizable
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
belongs to

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
, then so does
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\vee}
.

It follows formally that the same is true for
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
.

5.1.9.

Note that
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
is naturally tensored over
\operatorname{DGCat}
, and the tautological embedding

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

is
\operatorname{DGCat}
-linear.

Hence, we obtain a functor

(5.1)

\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.

We are going to prove:

Theorem 5.1.10
.

(a) The embedding

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\overset{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}{\longrightarrow}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is 2-adjointable.

(a’) The Beck-Chevalley natural transformation

\mathbf{jacq}_{B}\circ(\mathbf{emb}_{G}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}\to(\mathbf{emb}_{T}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}\circ\mathbf{jacq}_{B}

as functors

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

is an isomorphism.

(b) The functor (
5.1
) is an equivalence.

5.2.
Categorical representations as modules over the Hecke algebra

The proof of Theorem
5.1.10
is based on interpreting
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
as modules
(in
\operatorname{AGCat}
) over the (categorical) Hecke algebra.

5.2.1.

Consider the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

Denote

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}:=\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N))\in\operatorname{AssocAlg}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

The usual manipulation shows that as a plain object of
\operatorname{AGCat}

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N),

and the algebra structure on it is given by convolution.

5.2.2.

Consider the functor

(5.2)

\mathbf{inv}_{N}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}.

It is co-represented (in the sense of
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}}(-,-)
) by
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)
;
in particular, it admits a left adjoint (see Sect.
2.5.5
). Hence, we can identify

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\simeq\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm End}}}}_{\operatorname{Maps}_{2\operatorname{-AGCat}}(G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},\operatorname{AGCat}),\operatorname{AGCat}}(\mathbf{inv}_{N}),

i.e.,
\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}
is the monad on
\operatorname{AGCat}
corresponding to (
5.2
).

Thus, the functor
\mathbf{inv}_{N}
upgrades to a functor

(5.3)

\mathbf{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{N}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

5.2.3.

We are going to prove:

Theorem 5.2.4
.

The functor (
5.3
) is an equivalence.

Remark 5.2.5
.

The above Theorem
5.2.4
is an adaptation to the context of
\operatorname{AGCat}
of a theorem
of
[
BGO
]
, which was initially proved in the context of D-modules.

5.2.6.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of this theorem.

Recall
that by Corollary
2.3.10
, the functor
\mathbf{inv}_{G}
commutes with colimits
and tensor products by objects of
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

Hence, by the Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem, in order to prove Theorem
5.2.4
, it suffices
to show that the functor
\mathbf{inv}_{N}
(equivalently,
\mathbf{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{N}
) is conservative.

5.2.7.

In order to show that
\mathbf{inv}^{\operatorname{enh}}_{N}
is conservative, it suffices to prove that the
functor

\mathbf{jacq}_{B}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is conservative.

For the latter, it suffices to show that the endofunctor

{\mathbf{ind}}_{B}\circ\mathbf{jacq}_{B}

of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is conservative.

5.2.8.

Consider the counit of the
({\mathbf{ind}}_{B},\mathbf{jacq}_{B})
-adjunction. It is given by a map in
(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

(5.4)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G),

where:

•

\overset{T}{\times}
means the quotient by the diagonal action of
T
;

•

The map in (
5.4
) is given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}G\overset{B}{\times}G@>{}>{}>G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G\\
@V{}V{}V\\
G.\end{CD}

5.2.9.

Note, however, that the vertical map in the above diagram is proper and the horizontal is smooth.
Hence, the functor (
5.4
) admits a right adjoint.

We claim:

Proposition 5.2.10
.

The adjunction

\operatorname{counit}:{\mathbf{ind}}_{B}\circ\mathbf{jacq}_{B}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\rightleftarrows\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}:\operatorname{counit}^{R},\quad\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is monadic.

5.2.11.

Let us assume Proposition
5.2.10
for a moment, and finish the proof of Theorem
5.2.4
:

By Proposition
5.2.10
, we recover
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
as

(\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit}){\operatorname{-mod}}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\right).

Note that the monad
(\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit})
acting on

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

is given by

(\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit})\otimes\operatorname{Id}_{\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}},

where the left factor is the corresponding monad on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)
.

In particular, for a morphism
F:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}
in
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, the diagram

(5.5)

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}@>{\operatorname{Id}\otimes F}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}\\
@V{\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit}}V\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}@>{\operatorname{Id}\otimes F}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}\end{CD}

commutes.

Let
F
be such that

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes F}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}

is an equivalence. Then from (
5.5
) we obtain that
\operatorname{Id}\otimes F
induces an equivalence

(\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit}){\operatorname{-mod}}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{1}\right)\to\\
(\operatorname{counit}^{R}\circ\operatorname{counit}){\operatorname{-mod}}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N\overset{T}{\times}N\backslash G)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)_{\operatorname{co}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}_{2}\right),

as required.

∎[Theorem
5.2.4
]

5.2.12.
Proof of Proposition
5.2.10

By Barr-Beck-Lurie, it suffices to show that the functor
\operatorname{counit}^{R}
is conservative. We will
show that the composition

(5.6)

\operatorname{counit}\circ\operatorname{counit}^{R}

contains the identity endofunctor of
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
as a direct summand.

In fact, we will show that the functor (
5.6
)
viewed as an endofunctor of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\in(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

contains the identity as a direct summand.

However, unwinding, we obtain:

Lemma 5.2.13
.

The functor (
5.6
) is given by convolution
with the Springer sheaf

\operatorname{Spr}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

The required assertion follows now from the fact that
\operatorname{Spr}
contains
\delta_{1,G}
as a direct summand.

∎[Proposition
5.2.10
]

5.3.
The monodromic Hecke algebra

5.3.1.

Consider the stack
N\backslash G/N
. We view it as acted on by the group
T\times T
. We observe:

Lemma 5.3.2
.

The inclusions

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{left-}\!T\operatorname{-mon}}\hookleftarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{(T\times T)\operatorname{-mon}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{right-}\!T\operatorname{-mon}}

are equalities.

Proof.

By devissage, it is sufficient to prove that for a single Bruhat cell
BwB
, the inclusions

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash BwB/N)^{\operatorname{left-}\!T\operatorname{-mon}}\hookleftarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash BwB/N)^{(T\times T)\operatorname{-mon}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash BwB/N)^{\operatorname{right-}\!T\operatorname{-mon}}

are equalities.

We have

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash BwB/N)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)

as objects of
(T\times T)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
,
with the left action of
T
given by multiplication, and the right action given by multiplication precomposed with
w
.

Hence, for a given character sheaf

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash BwB/N)^{\operatorname{left-alm-}\!\chi}=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash BwB/N)^{\operatorname{right-alm-}\!w(\chi)},

making the assertion of the lemma manifest.

∎

5.3.3.

In what follows, we will denote the object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
that appears in Lemma
5.3.2
by

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}.

Consider the (fully faithful) map

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\hookrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N).

By Sect.
4.8.9
, the above 1-morphism admits a right adjoint (in
\operatorname{AGCat}
).

(5.7)

(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)\twoheadrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}.

It follows from Lemma
5.3.2
that the algebra structure on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)

induces a (uniquely defined) algebra structure on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}
so that
(
5.7
) is a homomorphism.

5.3.4.

Denote the corresponding object of
\operatorname{AssocAlg}(\operatorname{AGCat})
by
\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
.

From the isomorphism (
4.15
) we obtain:

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\simeq{\mathbf{i}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}),

where

{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}:=\operatorname{Shv}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\in\operatorname{AssocAlg}(\operatorname{DGCat}).

5.3.5.

For future use we record also the following consequence of Lemma
5.3.2
:

Corollary 5.3.6
.

The full subcategory

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\hookrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)

is a monoidal ideal.

5.3.7.

We have a closed embedding

T\to N\backslash G/N,

which induces a map of algebras

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)_{\operatorname{co}}\to\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}.

Note that we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)_{\operatorname{co}}@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\end{CD}

of algebra objects in
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

Denote by
{\mathbf{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{T}
the corresponding restriction functors

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\text{ and }\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}.

5.3.8.

Restriction along (
5.7
) gives rise to a (fully faithful) functor

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}}:\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\hookrightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}).

It admits a right adjoint given by

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}}(\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}},\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}).

We claim:

Proposition 5.3.9
.

(a) The functor
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}}
is 2-adjointable, i.e., the unit and counit of the

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}},(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}})^{R})
-adjunction admit right adjoints;

(b) An object
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\in\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
belongs to the essential image of
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}}

if and only if
{\mathbf{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{T}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})
belongs to the essential image of

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{T}:T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

(c) The Beck-Chevalley natural transformation

{\mathbf{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{T}\circ(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}})^{R}\to(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{T})^{R}\circ{\mathbf{Res}}^{\mathcal{H}}_{T},

as functors

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\to T\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Follows from Sect.
4.8
and the fact that the (quotient) objects

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{left}}_{T})^{R}(\underline{{\mathcal{H}}})\twoheadleftarrow\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{right}}_{T})^{R}(\underline{{\mathcal{H}}})

both identify with
\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
, which is a reformulation of Lemma
5.3.2
.

∎

5.3.10.
Semi-rigidity

Recall the notion of
semi-rigid
monoidal category, see
[
AGKRRV1
, Appendix C]
.
We claim:

Proposition 5.3.11
.

The monoidal category
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
is semi-rigid.

Proof.

We will deduce the assertion from Proposition
4.7.3
. Namely, we will show that the functor
right adjoint to the monoidal operation on
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
is given, up to a cohomological twist,
bu pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}N\backslash G\overset{N}{\times}G/N@>{}>{}>(N\backslash G/N)\times(N\backslash G/N)\\
@V{{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{mult}}V{}V\\
N\backslash G/N,\end{CD}

where the map
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{mult}
is induced by the multiplication map.

Indeed, by definition, the monoidal operation on
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
is given by pull-push along

\begin{CD}N\backslash G\overset{N}{\times}G/N@>{{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{mult}}>{}>N\backslash G/N\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(N\backslash G/N)\times(N\backslash G/N),\end{CD}

where:

•

The vertical map is smooth, so !-pullback with respect to it is isomorphic to the *-pullback, up to a cohomological shift;

•

The horizontal map factors as

N\backslash G\overset{N}{\times}G/N\to N\backslash G\overset{B}{\times}G/N\to N\backslash G/N,

where the second arrow is proper.

However, Corollary
4.7.5
(b) implies that the right adjoint to the *-pushforward along

N\backslash G\overset{N}{\times}G/N\to N\backslash G\overset{B}{\times}G/N,

resctricted to the monodromic subcategory
,
is given pullback along the same map (up to a cohomological shift).

∎

5.3.12.

Combining Proposition
5.3.11
with
[
AGKRRV1
, Propositions C.5.5 and C.5.7]
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.3.13
.

Let
F:{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{C}}_{2}
a 1-morphism in
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
.
If the functor between the underlying DG categories
admits a right/left adjoint, then so does
F
.

5.4.
Restricted representations as modules over the monodromic Hecke algebra

In this subsection we will finally prove Theorem
5.1.10
.

5.4.1.

First, combining Theorem
5.2.4
with Proposition
5.3.9
(b), we obtain:

Corollary 5.4.2
.

There exists a unique isomorphism

(5.8)

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})

that makes the diagram

\begin{CD}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}@>{\sim}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\\
@V{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}V{}V@V{}V{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\mathcal{H}}}V\\
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}@>{\sim}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\end{CD}

commute.

5.4.3.

The fact that
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
is 2-adjointable follows now from Proposition
5.3.9
(a).

Point (a’) of Theorem
5.1.10
follows from Proposition
5.3.9
(c).

5.4.4.

We now prove point (b) of Theorem
5.1.10
.

By Corollary
5.4.2
, we can identify

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\underset{\operatorname{AGCat}}{\times}\operatorname{DGCat}.

Recall the object

{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\in\operatorname{AssocAlg}(\operatorname{DGCat}),

see Sect.
5.3.4
.

We obtain

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\simeq\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})

and

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\simeq{\mathbf{i}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\underset{\operatorname{AGCat}}{\times}\operatorname{DGCat}.

Since the functor
{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}
is fully faithful, the latter equivalence implies that

{\mathbf{i}}({\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\underset{\operatorname{AGCat}}{\times}\operatorname{DGCat}\simeq{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat}).

∎[Theorem
5.1.10
]

5.4.5.

In what follows we will use a short-hand notation

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\mapsto\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

for the functor
(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}
.

5.4.6.

Note that in the course of the proof we have also established:

Corollary 5.4.7
.

There exists a unique equivalence

(5.9)

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\simeq{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})

that makes the diagram

\begin{CD}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}@>{\sim}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\\
@V{{\mathbf{i}}}V{}V@V{}V{{\mathbf{i}}}V\\
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}@>{\sim}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})\end{CD}

commute.

5.4.8.

As a corollary of Corollaries
5.4.2
and
5.4.7
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.4.9
.

(a)
For a pair of reductive groups
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
, the (fully faithful) functor

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\otimes G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\otimes G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is an equivalence onto
(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
.

(b) There exists a canonical equivalence

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\otimes G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\simeq(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}

compatible with the equivalence of point (a) via the functor
{\mathbf{i}}
.

Proof.

Follows from the fact that the functor

{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{G_{1}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{G_{2}}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}_{G_{1}\times G_{2}}

is an equivalence.

∎

5.5.
Inherited properties of restricted representations

In Sect.
2
we established some general properties of the objects
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}

and 1-morphisms between them. In this subsection we will see that many of these properties pass
on to the spectrally finite subcategories.

5.5.1.

First, since
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
(resp.,
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
) is of the form
A\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})

(resp.,
A\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
), with
A={\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{mon}}
, it is a dualizable object in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
(resp.,
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
).

Moreover, the inversion involution on
G
makes
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
(resp.,
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
)
self-dual, see Sect.
2.4.1
.

5.5.2.

The unit of the self-duality on
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
is represented by the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{left-}\!G\operatorname{-spec.fin}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{(G\times G)\operatorname{-spec.fin}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{right-}\!G\operatorname{-spec.fin}},

where the equivalences in the above formula follow from Lemma
5.3.2
.

From there we obtain that the
\operatorname{unit}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}
is isomorphic to

((\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}\otimes\operatorname{Id})(\operatorname{unit}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}})\simeq\\
\simeq((\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}\otimes(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R})(\operatorname{unit}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}})\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R})(\operatorname{unit}_{G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}}).

Hence, we obtain:

Corollary 5.5.3
.

With respect to the self-dualities

G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\vee}\simeq G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\text{ and }(G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{\vee}\simeq G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

we have

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{\vee}\simeq(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}.

5.5.4.

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be a pair of reductive groups, and let

F:G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

be a 1-morphism in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}
.

Denote

F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}})^{R}\circ F\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}},\quad G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

We claim:

Lemma 5.5.5
.

Suppose that
F
is adjointable. Then so is
F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
, and

(F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}\simeq(F^{R})^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

Proof.

Follows from the fact that

((\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G})^{R})^{R}\simeq\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G},

which in turn follows from the fact that
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G}
is 2-adjointable
(see Lemma
1.7.3
).

∎

5.5.6.

Combining with Corollary
2.5.8
, we obtain:

Corollary 5.5.7
.

The object
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\in 2\operatorname{-AGCat}
is 2-dualiazable.

And further:

Corollary 5.5.8
.

The object
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\in 2\operatorname{-DGCat}
is 2-dualiazable.

5.5.9.

For
F
as above, we shall say that it is
spec.fin-adapted
if it sends

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\subset G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

to

G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\subset G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

This is equivalent to the requirement that the natural
transformation

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}}\circ(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}})^{R}\circ F\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}\circ(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}\to F\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}\circ(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}

as functors

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

is an isomorphism.

Still equivalently, the above condition is equivalent to the requirement that the diagram

(5.10)

\begin{CD}G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}@>{F}>{}>G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\\
@A{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}}A{}A@A{}A{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}}}A\\
G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}@>{F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}>{}>G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},\end{CD}

which a priori commutes up to a natural transformation, actually commutes.

5.5.10.

The above condition can be rewritten as follows:

Let

{\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}\in(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

be the object that represents
F
. Then
F
is
spec.fin-adapted
if and only if

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2})
, viewed as an object of

(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
(or, equivalently, just
G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
) belongs to

(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
(resp.,
G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
).
By definition this means that the object

(5.11)

(\mathbf{jacq}_{N_{1}\times N_{2}}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}))^{T_{1}\operatorname{-mon}}\in T_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

belongs to
T_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{mon}}
.

5.5.11.

We shall say that
F
is
restricted-adapted
if it is
spec.fin-adapted

and the functor
F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
sends

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}.

This is equivalent to the requirement that the object
(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2})

above belongs to the subcategory
(G_{1}\times G_{2})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
(equivalently, to

G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
, when viewed as an object of just
G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
).

This is further equivalent to the requirement that the object

(\mathbf{jacq}_{N_{1}\times N_{2}}({\mathbf{M}}_{1,2}))^{T_{1}\operatorname{-mon}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}

belongs to the essential image of
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

Under the above circumstances, we will denote by
F^{\operatorname{restr}}
the resulting
1-morphism

G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}.

5.5.12.

We claim:

Lemma 5.5.13
.

Let
F:G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
be 2-adjointable. If it is
spec.fin-adapted, then the 1-morphism

F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

is also 2-adjointable.

Proof.

We need to show that the unit and counit of the adjunction admit right adjoints.
For the counit, this follows without the condition of being spec.fin-adapted:

The counit is the composition

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}})^{R}\circ F\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}\circ(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}\circ F^{R}\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}}\to\\
\to(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}})^{R}\circ F\circ F^{R}\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}}\to(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}})^{R}\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{Id},

where:

•

The first arrow is the counit for the

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}},(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R})
-adjunction, and hence
admits a right adjoint by Theorem
5.1.10
(a);

•

The second arrow is the counit of the
(F,F^{R})
-adjunction, and hence admits a
right adjoint by the hypothesis on
F
;

•

The last isomorphism is due to the fact that
\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{2}}

is fully faithful.

We now consider the unit. It is the composition

\operatorname{Id}\to(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}\to(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R}\circ F^{R}\circ F\circ\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}\simeq(F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}\circ F^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},

where:

•

The first arrow is the unit for the

(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}},(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}})^{R})
-adjunction, and hence
admits a right adjoint by Theorem
5.1.10
(a); in fact, it is an isomorphism since

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{G_{1}}
is fully-faithful;

•

The second arrow is the unit of the
(F,F^{R})
-adjunction, and hence admits a
right adjoint by the hypothesis on
F
;

•

The last isomorphism is due to the fact that
F
is spec.fin-adapted.

∎

Corollary 5.5.14
.

Let
F:G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
be 2-adjointable. Then if it is
restricted-adapted, then the 1-morphism

F^{\operatorname{restr}}:G_{1}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G_{2}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}

is also 2-adjointable.

5.6.
The induction functor on restricted representations

In this subsection we will encounter a key feature that distinguishes
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

from
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
: the intertwining functor
I_{P_{1},P_{2}}
becomes an
isomorphism

when restricted to the spectrally finite subcategory.

5.6.1.

We apply the material of Sect.
5.5
to
G_{1}=M
and
G_{2}=G
,
where
M
is a Levi subgroup in
G
and
F={\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
.

We claim that
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
is spec.fin-adapted. Indeed, the corresponding object
(
5.11
) is

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{left-}\!T\operatorname{-mon}},

and the assertion follows from Lemma
5.3.2
(see Sect.
5.5.10
).

Moreover, from Proposition
4.4.5
it follows that
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
is restricted adapted.

5.6.2.

From Lemma
5.5.13
and Corollary
5.5.14
, combined with Sect.
2.6.13

we obtain:

Corollary 5.6.3
.

The 1-morphisms

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

and

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}

are 2-adjointable.

5.6.4.

Recall the 2-morphism

I_{P_{1},P_{2}}:{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}\to{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}},

see Sect.
3.3.5
.

This 2-morphism automatically induces a 2-morphism

I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}:{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1}}\to{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2}},

between 1-morphisms

M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

and a 2-morphism

I^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}:{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{1}}\to{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{2}},

between 1-morphisms

M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

5.6.5.

Here is a key assertion that makes the spectrally finite theory work better than
the entire one:

Proposition 5.6.6
.

The 2-morphism
I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}
is an
isomorphism
;
in particular, it admits a right adjoint.

As an immediate corollary we obtain:

Corollary 5.6.7
.

The 2-morphism
I^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}
is an isomorphism;
in particular, it admits a right adjoint.

5.6.8.
Proof of Proposition
5.6.6

Choose a maximal unipotent
N_{M}\subset M
so that the preimage
N_{i}
under
P_{i}\to M
is a maximal
unipotent in
G
. Since the functor

\mathbf{inv}_{N_{M}}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{AGCat}

is conservative (by Theorem
5.2.4
), it is enough to show that the 2-morphism
I^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}

induces an equivalence (in
\operatorname{AGCat}
) after taking
N_{M}
-invariants.

Note that the resulting functor is given by pull-push along

\begin{CD}G/N_{1}\cap N_{2}@>{r^{\prime}_{2}}>{}>G/N_{2}\\
@V{r^{\prime}_{1}}V{}V\\
G/N_{1}.\end{CD}

Thus, we need to show that for a scheme
Y
, the functor

(\operatorname{id}\times r^{\prime}_{2})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times r^{\prime}_{1})^{!}

induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/N_{1}))^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/N_{2}))^{T\operatorname{-mon}}.

The relative position of
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
is a well-defined element
w(N_{1},N_{2})\in W
. It is easy to see that
for a triple of maximal unipotents
N_{1},N_{2},N_{3}
with

\ell(w(N_{1},N_{2}))+\ell(w(N_{2},N_{3}))=\ell(w(N_{1},N_{3})),

we have

I_{B_{1},B_{3}}\simeq I_{B_{2},B_{3}}\circ I_{B_{1},B_{2}},

where
B_{i}
is the Borel containing
N_{i}
.

Hence, we can assume that the relative position of
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
is a simple reflection
s_{i}
. Let
P
be
the subminimal parabolic that contains both
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
. The diagram

G/N_{1}\to G/P\leftarrow G/N_{2}

étale-locally with respect to
G/P
is isomorphic to a product situation with
G
of semisimple rank
1
,
and
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
are a Borel subgroup and its opposite.

5.6.9.

Thus, we can assume that
G
has semi-simple rank
1
. Further, it is easy to reduce the assertion to the
case when
G=SL_{2}
. We will analyze the latter case explicitly.

We identify
G/N
with
(V-0)
, where
V
is a 2-dimensional vector space with a trivialized determinant
line; in particular we obtain an identification
V\simeq V^{*}
.

Under these identofications,
I_{B,B^{-}}
corresponds to the Radon transform

\operatorname{Rad}_{V}:(\operatorname{id}\times r_{V^{*}})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times r_{V})^{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(V-0))\to\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(V^{*}-0)),

where

(V-0)\overset{r_{V}}{\longleftarrow}{\mathsf{H}}\overset{r_{V^{*}}}{\longrightarrow}(V^{*}-0),\quad{\mathsf{H}}=\{(v,v^{*})\,|\,\langle v,v^{*}\rangle=1\}.

Now, it is known that
\operatorname{Rad}^{*}_{V}
is an equivalence
on the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-monodromic subcategories
with inverse

\widetilde{\operatorname{Rad}}_{V^{*}}:(\operatorname{id}\times r_{V})_{!}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times r_{V^{*}})^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(V^{*}-0))\to\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(V-0)).

∎[Proposition
5.6.6
]

5.7.
Jacquet functor on restricted representations

In this subsection we will encounter another new feature of the induction
functor in the spectrally finite situation.

5.7.1.

Recall that the fact that the 1-morphism
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
is 2-adjointable implies that the adjunction

({\mathbf{ind}}_{P},\mathbf{jacq}_{P})

is ambidexterous.

In particular, the 1-morphism
\mathbf{jacq}_{P}
is adjointable. But the above does not say (and it is
not
true) that

\mathbf{jacq}_{P}
is 2-adjointable.

5.7.2.

Note that by the same logic as in the case of
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
, the 1-morphism
\mathbf{jacq}_{P}
is spec.fin-adapted (and also
restricted-adapted).

In this subsection will prove:

Theorem 5.7.3
.

The 1-morphism

\mathbf{jacq}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

is 2-adjointable.

As an immediate corollary we obtain:

Corollary 5.7.4
.

The 1-morphism

\mathbf{jacq}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}

is 2-adjointable.

5.7.5.

Note that by combining Theorem
5.7.3
with Lemma
1.7.3
, we obtain a
new
adjunction between
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}
(as a left adjoint)
and
\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}
(as a right adjoint).

This adjunction is obtained from the initial
({\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P},\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})
-adjunction by replacing the
unit/counit by their respective
double right adjoints
.

5.7.6.

More generally, let

F:{\mathbf{o}}_{1}\to{\mathbf{o}}_{2}

be an adjointable 1-morphism in a 3-category, such that its unit and counit admits double right adjoints.

By the above, we obtain a
new
adjunction

F\leftrightarrow F^{R}.

We will call such
F
a
vacillating
14
14

14

The name was suggested by S. Raskin.
functor.

5.7.7.

The rest of the subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
5.7.3
.

By definition, we need to show that the 2-morphisms

(5.12)

\operatorname{Id}\to\mathbf{jacq}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\text{ and }{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\circ\mathbf{jacq}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\operatorname{Id}

that give rise to the initial
({\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P},\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})
-adjunction admit double right adjoints.

Using Corollary
5.4.2
, we interpret the 2-morphisms (
5.12
) as the morphisms

(5.13)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N_{M}\backslash M/N_{M})^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\overset{i_{*}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}

(in
(\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}_{M}^{\operatorname{mod}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}_{M}^{\operatorname{mod}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})
), and

(5.14)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\overset{\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext}}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}

(in
(\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}_{G}^{\operatorname{mod}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}_{G}^{\operatorname{mod}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat})
), respectively, where

i:N_{M}\backslash M/N_{M}\to N_{M}\backslash(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/N_{M}\simeq N\backslash G/N

is the natural map, and
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext}}
is pull-push along

\begin{CD}N\backslash G\overset{P}{\times}G/N@>{}>{}>N\backslash G/N\\
@V{}V{}V\\
N\backslash G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G/N.\end{CD}

5.7.8.

Note now that by Proposition
4.4.5
, the 1-morphisms (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) are obtained by applying the functor
{\mathbf{i}}:\operatorname{DGCat}\to\operatorname{AGCat}

to the 1-morphisms

(5.15)

\operatorname{Shv}(N_{M}\backslash M/N_{M})^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\overset{i_{*}}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(N\backslash G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}

in
({\mathcal{H}}_{M}^{\operatorname{mod}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{M}^{\operatorname{mod}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
, and

(5.16)

\operatorname{Shv}(N\backslash G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\overset{\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Shv}(N\backslash G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}

in
({\mathcal{H}}_{G}^{\operatorname{mod}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{G}^{\operatorname{mod}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})
,
respectively.

So, it is enough to show that the functors (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) admit double right adjoints
in

({\mathcal{H}}_{M}^{\operatorname{mod}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{M}^{\operatorname{mod}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\text{ and }({\mathcal{H}}_{G}^{\operatorname{mod}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}_{G}^{\operatorname{mod}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{DGCat}),

respectively.

5.7.9.

However, applying Corollary
5.3.13
, we obtain that it suffices that the 1-morphisms (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) admit double
right adjoints in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

5.7.10.

The right adjoints to the maps in (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) are given by

(5.17)

i^{!}\text{ and }\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext}}

respectively,
where
\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext}}
is given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}N\backslash G\overset{P}{\times}G/N@>{}>{}>N\backslash G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G/N\\
@V{}V{}V\\
N\backslash G/N.\end{CD}

In order for these functors to admit right adjoints in
\operatorname{DGCat}
, it is necessary and sufficient that they
preserves compactness.

Note, however, that all the stacks involved are
safe
, so compactness is equivalent to constructibility.
Now, the fact that the functors (
5.17
) preserve constructibility is obvious.

∎[Theorem
5.7.3
]

5.8.
The Serre structure on the induction functor

Building on the previous subsection, we will show that the induction functor admits an additional

structure
in the spectrally finite situation.

5.8.1.

Let us return to the context of Sect.
5.7.6
. Thus, we start with an initial adjunction

F\leftrightarrow F^{R}

and by passing to double right adjoints of the unit and counit, we obtain a
new
adjunction

F\overset{\operatorname{new}}{\leftrightarrow}F^{R}.

Thus, the functor
F
admits a canonical automorphism, called the Serre functor,
to be denoted
\operatorname{Se}_{F}
that intertwines the two adjunctions.

In other words,

(5.18)

\operatorname{unit}^{\operatorname{new}}\simeq(\operatorname{id}_{F^{R}}(\operatorname{Se}_{F}))\circ\operatorname{unit}

or

(5.19)

\operatorname{counit}\simeq\operatorname{counit}^{\operatorname{new}}\circ(\operatorname{Se}_{F}(\operatorname{id}_{F^{R}})),

where we denote by
\operatorname{id}_{F^{R}}(\operatorname{Se}_{F})
and
\operatorname{Se}_{F}(\operatorname{id}_{F^{R}})
the
corresponding automorphisms of
F^{R}\circ F
and
F\circ F^{R}
, respectively.

5.8.2.

We apply this to the vacillating functor
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}
. Denote the corresponding Serre automorphism of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\in(G\times M)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

by
\operatorname{Se}_{G/N(P)}
.

By definition,
\operatorname{Se}_{G/N(P)}
is uniquely characterized by
either
of the following two isomorphisms

(5.20)

\operatorname{unit}^{\operatorname{new}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Se}_{G/N(P)})\circ\operatorname{unit}

or

(5.21)

\operatorname{counit}\simeq\operatorname{counit}^{\operatorname{new}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{Se}_{G/N(P)}).

5.8.3.

In this subsection we will prove the following result:

Theorem 5.8.4
.

The functor
\operatorname{Se}_{G/N(P)}
equals the composition

I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P}\circ I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}}[-2\dim(N(P))]

viewed as an endomorphism of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\in(G\times M)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
5.8.4
.

5.8.5.

We will prove that
\operatorname{Se}_{G/N(P)}
satisfies (
5.20
), while (
5.21
) will be a
(non-obvious) consequence, to be used in Sect.
7.5.13
.

5.8.6.

Recall that the unit of the initial
({\mathbf{ind}}_{P},\mathbf{jacq}_{P})
-adjunction is given by
the functor

\iota_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P)),

equal to pull-push along

\begin{CD}N(P)\backslash P/N(P)@>{}>{}>N(P)\backslash G/N(P)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
M.\end{CD}

Set

\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}:=\iota_{P}|_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}.

Thus, we need to construct an isomorphism

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P}\circ I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}}))\circ\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}[-2\dim(N(P))]\simeq((\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})^{R})^{R}

as maps

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

in
(M\times M)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, where the symbol
\operatorname{id}\otimes I_{?,?}
refers to the action of the corresponding
intertwining functor on the right.

This is equivalent to an isomorphism

(5.22)

((\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})^{R}\simeq((\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})^{L}[2\dim(N(P))]

as maps

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

in
(M\times M)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

5.8.7.

Consider the
open
embedding

(5.23)

M\hookrightarrow N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}).

Let
\jmath_{P,P^{-}}
denote the corresponding functor of restriction

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M).

It admits a right adjoint, given by *-extension along (
5.23
).

5.8.8.

Unwinding, we obtain:

(5.24)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I_{P,P^{-}})\circ\iota_{P}\simeq(\jmath_{P,P^{-}})^{R}

Hence,

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}\simeq(\jmath_{P,P^{-}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R},

where

\jmath_{P,P^{-}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=\jmath_{P,P^{-}}|_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}.

5.8.9.

Consider the functor

\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

It is the value-wise left adjoint of

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P}):\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-})))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N(P)\backslash G/N(P)))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

Hence, it is given by
*-pull followed by !-push
along the diagram

\begin{CD}Y\times(N(P)\backslash G)@>{}>{}>Y\times(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))\\
@V{}V{}V\\
Y\times(N(P)\backslash G/N(P)).\end{CD}

Hence, the functor

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}

is given value-wise by

((\jmath_{P,P^{-}})^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{L}[-2\dim(N(P))],

thanks to the fact that (
5.23
) is an open embedding.

5.8.10.

This makes (
5.22
) manifest, since both sides identify with

\jmath_{P,P^{-}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}[2\dim(N(P))].

∎[Theorem
5.8.4
]

Remark 5.8.11
.

A somewhat parallel statement (originally proved in
[
BBM
]
and then reproved in
[
CGY
]
) says
that the usual Serre functor on the category
\operatorname{Shv}(N\backslash G/B)
is equal to

I_{B^{-},B}\circ I_{B,B^{-}}[-2\dim(N)],

where we view the intertwining functor as acting on the right.

We were not able to formally deduce the above description of the
Serre functor for
\operatorname{Shv}(N\backslash G/B)
from Theorem
5.8.4
.

We would be curious to know whether such a formal deduction exists.

6.
Applications to character sheaves

In this subsection we will recast the theory of character sheaves from the point
of view of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
by taking the trace.

We will encounter some (non-obvious) features of character sheaves
(e.g., the functional equation for the Grothendieck-Springer functor, and the ambidexterity
of the
(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P},{\mathfrak{r}}_{P})
-adjunction) that follow from the 3-categorical
formalism.

6.1.
Character sheaves–a reminder

6.1.1.

For a scheme
Y
, consider the pair of categories

(6.1)

\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T)),

where
\operatorname{Ad}(T)
refers to the diagonal action of
T
on
N\backslash G/N
.

We regard the two sides of (
6.1
) as monoidal categories
with respect to
convolution
.

6.1.2.

We have a monoidal functor

\operatorname{HC}:\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T)),

given by pull-push along the diagram

(6.2)

\begin{CD}Y\times G/\operatorname{Ad}(B)@>{}>{}>Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
Y\times G/\operatorname{Ad}(G).\end{CD}

Since the horizontal morphism in (
6.2
) is smooth and the vertical one is proper, the functor

\operatorname{HC}
admits a left adjoint, to be denoted
\operatorname{CH}
, which commutes with !-pullbacks and *-pushforwards
along
Y
.

The functor
\operatorname{CH}
is left-lax monoidal, but is strictly compatible with the convolution
action of the monoidal category
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
on either side.

In particular, we obtain an adjunction between the corresponding objects in
\operatorname{AGCat}
:

\operatorname{CH}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))\rightleftarrows\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))):\operatorname{HC}.

6.1.3.

We note:

Lemma 6.1.4
.

The endofunctor
\operatorname{CH}\circ\operatorname{HC}
of
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
is isomorphic to the convolution with the
Springer sheaf.

Proof.

This is obtained from Lemma
5.2.13
by passing to
G
-invariants on both sides.

∎

6.1.5.

Since the Springer sheaf contains
\delta_{1,G}
as a direct summand, we obtain:

Corollary 6.1.6
.

The functor
\operatorname{HC}
is conservative.

6.1.7.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\subset\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))

be the full subcategory consisting of objects whose image along
\operatorname{HC}
lands in

\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T)).

Since
\operatorname{HC}
is monoidal, from Corollary
5.3.6

we obtain that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
is a monoidal ideal
inside
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
.

It is clear that for
f:Y\to Y^{\prime}
, the *-pushforward and !-pullback functors along
f\times\operatorname{id}

map the categories
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
and
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y^{\prime}\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))

to one another.

6.1.8.

Thus, we obtain a well-defined object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\in\operatorname{AGCat},

equipped with a fully-faithful 1-morphism
15
15

15

The notion of fully faithfulness for a 1-morphism is intrinsic to any 2-category:
it means that the induced functor on
{\mathbf{Maps}}(-,-)
is fully faithful. In the case of
\operatorname{AGCat}
, this is equivalent to the fact that
the given 1-morphism evaluates to a fully faithful functor in
\operatorname{DGCat}
on every scheme.

(6.3)

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

6.1.9.

We observe:

Lemma 6.1.10
.

The functor
\operatorname{CH}
maps
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}
to

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
.

Proof.

We have to show that the endofunctor

\operatorname{HC}\circ\operatorname{CH}
of
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))
preserves the subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}
.

We note that by proper and smooth base change, the functor
\operatorname{HC}\circ\operatorname{CH}
admits a filtration
indexed by elements
w\in W
, and each subquotient
(\operatorname{HC}\circ\operatorname{CH})_{w}
intertwines
the initial
T
-action on
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))
with one twisted by the
action of
w
on
T
. In particular, each
(\operatorname{HC}\circ\operatorname{CH})_{w}
preserves the monodromic
subcategory. Hence, so does
\operatorname{HC}\circ\operatorname{CH}
.

∎

6.1.11.

Thus, we obtain that the adjoint functors
(\operatorname{CH},\operatorname{HC})
induce adjoint functors

(6.4)

\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))).

Since
\operatorname{HC}
is conservative (on all of
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
),
we obtain:

Corollary 6.1.12
.

The essential image of the functor

\operatorname{CH}:\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))

generates the target category.

6.1.13.

We further obtain:

Proposition 6.1.14
.

The inclusion functor

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))

admits a continuous right adjoint, which makes the diagrams

(6.5)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))@<{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@V{\operatorname{HC}}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{HC}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}@<{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))\end{CD}

and

(6.6)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))@<{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@A{\operatorname{CH}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{CH}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}@<{(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}}<{}<\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))\end{CD}

commute. Furthermore:

(a)
For a morphism
f:Y\to Y^{\prime}
, the natural transformations

(6.7)

(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\to(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\text{ and }(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\to(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}

are isomorphisms.

(b) The right adjoint
(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}
is strictly monoidal, and is given by
convolution with the (central idempotent) object

\delta_{1,G}^{\operatorname{ch}}:=(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{\operatorname{pt}})^{R}(\delta_{1,G})\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

Proof.

The commutation of diagram (
6.5
) follows by passing to right adjoints from the tautologically
commuting diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))@>{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@A{\operatorname{CH}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{CH}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T)).\end{CD}

For the commutation of (
6.6
), we need to show that the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))@>{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@V{\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}}V{}V@V{}V{\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))\end{CD}

commutes, where
\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}
is the
left
adjoint of
\operatorname{CH}
, i.e.,
\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}
is given (up to a cohomological shift)
by *-pull followed by !-push along (
6.2
).

Thus, we need to show that
\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}_{Y}
sends

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
to
\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}
.
Note, however, that the compact generators of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))
are constructible,
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}
is Verdier conjugate of the functor
\operatorname{HC}
. Now the required assertion follows from
the fact that Verdier duality defines anti-involutions on

(\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}})^{c}\text{ and }(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(Y\times(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))))^{c}.

For the former, this follows from the definitions. For the latter this follows from Corollary
6.1.12
, since the
functor
\operatorname{CH}
commutes with Verdier duality, up to cohomological shift.

To show that the maps in (
6.7
) are isomorphisms, it suffices to show that the maps

\operatorname{HC}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\to\operatorname{HC}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}

and

\operatorname{HC}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\to\operatorname{HC}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})^{!}

are isomorphisms (indeed, the functors
\operatorname{HC}
,
\operatorname{HC}
are conservative by Corollary
6.1.6
).

Note that we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{HC}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}@>{\sim}>{}>(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\circ\operatorname{HC}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{HC}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}&&(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\circ(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\circ\operatorname{HC}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\circ\operatorname{HC}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}@>{\sim}>{}>(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}})^{R}\circ(f\times\operatorname{id})_{*}\circ\operatorname{HC}.\end{CD}

Hence, the upper left vertical arrow is also an isomorphism.

The case of
f^{!}
is treated similarly.

Point (b) follows from the corresponding property of
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{mon}}
(see Sect.
5.3.3
),
using the diagram (
6.5
).

∎

Corollary 6.1.15
.

The 1-morphism (
6.3
) admits a right adjoint

(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

Remark 6.1.16
.

It is easy to see that Proposition
6.1.14
implies that the Verdier self-duality of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
induces a Verdier self-duality on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

so that

(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{\vee}\simeq(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}})^{R}.

6.1.17.

Denote

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)):=\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))(\operatorname{pt})\in\operatorname{DGCat}.

We claim:

Proposition 6.1.18
.

The canonical map

(6.8)

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Both sides embed fully faithfully in

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\text{ and }\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

respectively. Sunce

{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

is fully faithful
16
16

16

It is not an equivalence!
, functor (
6.8
) is fully faithful.
Hence, it remains to show that it is essentially surjective.

We have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(Y)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\\
@A{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\operatorname{CH}}A{}A@A{}A{\operatorname{CH}}A\\
\operatorname{Shv}(Y)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(Y\times(N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}.\end{CD}

Hence, the assertion follows from Corollary
6.1.12
, since the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\in\operatorname{AGCat}
is restricted
(by Proposition
4.4.5
).

∎

6.2.
Another interpretation of character sheaves

6.2.1.

Consider the object

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G))\in(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\subset(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{.}

In the above formula, we apply
(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}
to
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
either as an object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
along the action on one of the sides,
or as an object of
(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
. The result is the same by Lemma
5.3.2
.

6.2.2.

The (fully faithful) embedding

(6.9)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\overset{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

admits a right adjoint (by Theorem
5.1.10
(a)), and thus induces a fully faithful embedding

(6.10)

\mathbf{inv}_{\Delta(G)}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\mathbf{inv}_{\Delta(G)}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G))\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

We claim:

Proposition 6.2.3
.

The essential image of (
6.10
) equals

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\subset\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
6.2.3
. In the process,
we will describe the subobject (
6.9
) more explicitly, see formula (
6.11
).

6.2.4.

Note that by Theorem
5.2.4
, we have

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)

as objects of
(G\times G)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

Under this identification, we have

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}},

which we can also rewrite as

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}},

since the
\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}
-action on
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}
factors through the colocalization

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\twoheadrightarrow\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}.

6.2.5.

In particular, we obtain that
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
is the subobject of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
generated by the essential image of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\hookrightarrow\\
\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

We rewrite the latter functor as

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\to\\
\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

6.2.6.

Note that the essential image of the functor
