\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}

generates the target (when evaluated on every scheme).

Hence, we can further rewrite
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\subset\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)
as the essential image of

(6.11)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N)\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G).

This is the promised explicit description of the subcategory (
6.9
).

6.2.7.

Applying
\mathbf{inv}_{\Delta(G)}\simeq\mathbf{coinv}_{\Delta(G)}
to (
6.11
)
we obtain that the essential image of (
6.10
) is the essential image of

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))^{T\operatorname{-mon}}\subset\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

where the second arrow is the functor
\operatorname{CH}
.

∎[Proposition
6.2.3
]

6.3.
Character sheaves as trace

6.3.1.

Consider again the 1-morphism

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

It is adjointable, and hence induces a 1-morphism

(6.12)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

in
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

6.3.2.

We claim:

Theorem 6.3.3
.

The 1-morphism (
6.12
) is fully faithful, and its essential image
identifies with
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\hookrightarrow\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
.

Remark 6.3.4
.

Theorem
6.3.3
is due to D. Ben-Zvi and D. Nadler, see
[
BN2
]
. The proof presented below
is a mild variation on their argument.

6.3.5.

As an immediate consequence of Theorem
6.3.3
(combined with Proposition
6.1.18
), we obtain:

Corollary 6.3.6
.

There exists a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}})\simeq\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

whose image under
{\mathbf{i}}
produces the equivalence

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

of Theorem
6.3.3
.

Remark 6.3.7
.

Note that Corollary
6.3.6
gives a different proof of Proposition
6.1.18
:
we have

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{AGCat}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\otimes}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}})\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}))\simeq{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))).

6.3.8.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
6.3.3
.

The fact that (
6.12
) is fully faithful follows immediately from Corollary
1.7.10
.
Hence, it remains to identify its essential image.

6.3.9.

We have to identify the essential image of

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

Note, however, that the essential image of

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}

value-wise generates the target category, so we have to show that the essential image of

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

value-wise generates
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
.

Note that we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)\\
@V{=}V{}V@V{}V{=}V\\
\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{}>{}>\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
&&@V{}V{}V\\
&&\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
&&@V{}V{}V\\
&&\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),\end{CD}

where the third horizontal arrow is fully faithful.

Hence, the required assertion follows from the combination of Corollary
6.1.12
, the fact that the functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)\overset{\operatorname{Av}^{\operatorname{Ad}(T)}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))

value-wise generates the essential image and the next statement:

Proposition 6.3.10
.

The composition

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)=\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

identifies with

(6.13)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)\overset{\operatorname{Av}^{\operatorname{Ad}(T)}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}(T))\overset{\operatorname{CH}}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition
6.3.10
.

∎[Theorem
6.3.3
]

6.3.11.

For an associative algebra
A
(in a symmetric monoidal category
{\mathbf{O}}
), the map

(6.14)

A\to A\underset{A\otimes A}{\otimes}A\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},A{\operatorname{-mod}}({\mathbf{O}}))

can be interpreted as follows:

Consider the diagram

(6.15)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{O}}}

\scriptstyle{A\otimes(-)}
\scriptstyle{A\otimes(-)}
\scriptstyle{A\otimes(-)}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{Id}}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}

where
\alpha
is the map of
A
-modules

A\otimes\operatorname{oblv}_{A}(A)\to A,

given by right multiplication.

We regard (

) as an instance of (
1.6
) (in
{\mathbf{L}}({\mathbf{O}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})
), and the map (

)
is obtained by applying to it the trace construction.

6.3.12.

Hence, the composition

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)=\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})

is given by

\textstyle{\operatorname{AGCat}}

.

]

6.4.
Functional equation for the Grothendieck-Springer functor

In this subsection we will encounter a new feature of the Grothendieck-Springer functor
(in the spectrally finite situation); namely that for a pair of parabolics
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
,
there is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

as functors

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

The question of transitivity of these isomorphisms (for a triple of parabolics) will be discussed
in Sect.
7
.

6.4.1.

Let
P
be a parabolic in
G
. Recall that the (adjointable) 1-morphism

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

induces a map of traces of the identity 1-endomorphisms

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}),

which identifies with the Grothendieck-Springer functor

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

Similarly, the functor
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
induces a map

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}),

so that we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{ind}}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}).\end{CD}

Hence, we obtain a functor

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

so that the diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))@>{\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\\
@A{}A{}A@A{}A{}A\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))@>{}>{\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\end{CD}

commutes.

Evaluating on
\operatorname{pt}
we obtain a functor

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

6.4.2.

Let
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
be a pair of parabolics as in Sect.
3.3.5
.
Recall that the resulting two functors

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

are in general
not
isomorphic.

However, we claim:

Theorem-Construction 6.4.3
.

The (iso)morphism

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\overset{I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

as functors

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

Proof.

Follows from Sect.
1.5.5
using Proposition
5.6.6
.

∎

Remark 6.4.4
.

In Sect.
7.3
we will describe the isomorphism
i_{P_{1},P_{2}}
in
(somewhat) explicit terms.

6.4.5.

Recall that we have a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{G}\simeq\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{G}\circ\sigma_{M},

where
\sigma_{G}
and
\sigma_{M}
denote the inversion automorphisms of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
and

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))
, respectively (see (
3.6
)).

Hence, we obtain an isomorphism

(6.16)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\circ\sigma_{M}.

Unwinding, we obtain:

Lemma 6.4.6
.

The diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:GrothSpr and inv ch}}}V{}V@V{}V{\text{\eqref{e:GrothSpr and inv ch}}}V\\
\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\sigma_{M}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\sigma_{M}.\end{CD}

6.5.
Functional equation on the regular semi-simple locus

In this subsection, we will show that when
M=T
is the Cartan subgroup, the functional
equation boils down to something familiar.

6.5.1.

We will now calculate the isomorphism of Theorem
6.4.3
is a particular case. We take
P_{1}=B_{1}=B

and
P_{2}=B_{2}
be a Borel subgroup in relative position
w
with respect to
B
for some
w\in W
. Note
that we can then rewrite
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{B_{2}}
as

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{B_{1}}\circ w^{-1},

where
w
denotes the action of the Weyl group on
T
.

We start with a 1-dimensional character sheaf
\chi\in\operatorname{Shv}(T)
, which we can regard as an object of

\operatorname{Shv}(T/\operatorname{Ad}(T))
as the adjoint action of
T
on itself is trivial.

Consider the objects

(6.17)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)\text{ and }\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)

of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
.

It is well-known that both lie in
\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))^{\heartsuit}
and are the Goresky-MacPherson
extensions of their restrictions to the regular semi-simple locus
G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)
.

Lemma 6.5.2
.

The restrictions of the objects (
6.17
) to
G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)
are
canonically isomorphic.

Proof.

This statement is well-known; we include the proof for completeness.

By definition, for a Borel subgroup
B
, the object
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi)
is push-pull of
\chi
along the diagram

\begin{CD}\widetilde{G}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)@>{}>{}>G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
T/\operatorname{Ad}(T),\end{CD}

where
\widetilde{G}\to G
is the Grothendieck-Springer resolution.

Let
\widetilde{G}^{\operatorname{rs}}
denote the preimage of the open
G^{\operatorname{rs}}\subset G
in
\widetilde{G}
.
Consider the resulting diagram:

\begin{CD}\widetilde{G}^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)@>{}>{}>G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
T/\operatorname{Ad}(T).\end{CD}

Now, it is known that the action of
W
on
T
can be lifted to one on
\widetilde{G}^{\operatorname{rs}}
, so that the projection

\widetilde{G}^{\operatorname{rs}}\to G^{\operatorname{rs}}
is a
W
-torsor.

Thus, the action of
w
induces an isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)|_{G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\to\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)|_{G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}.

∎

6.5.3.

We claim:

Theorem 6.5.4
.

The isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)\overset{i_{B_{1},B_{2}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)

of Theorem
6.4.3
equals the Goresky-MacPherson extension of the isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)|_{G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)|_{G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}

of Lemma
6.5.2
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
6.5.4
.

6.5.5.
Interpreting the fibers of the Springer sheaf

It is sufficient that the two morphisms in question agree at the level of fibers at points
g\in G^{\operatorname{rs}}
.

Unwinding the definitions, the !-fiber of
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)
at
g\in G

is the object of
\operatorname{Vect}
equal to

\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{1})^{T,\chi}),

and similarly for
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)
.

Furthermore, the fiber at
g
of the isomorphism

(6.18)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)

of Theorem
6.4.3
is given by applying
\operatorname{Tr}(g,-)
to the

g
-equivariant 1-isomorphism

(6.19)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{1})^{T,\chi}\overset{\sim}{\to}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{2})^{T,\chi},

given by pull-push along the diagram

(6.20)

\begin{CD}G/N_{1}\cap N_{2}@>{}>{}>G/N_{2}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
G/N_{1}.\end{CD}

6.5.6.
Calculating the fibers

Explicitly, the object

\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{i})^{T,\chi})\in\operatorname{Vect}

is calculated as follows.

Consider the scheme

(G/B_{i})^{g}:=\{g^{\prime}\,|\,g^{\prime}{}^{-1}\cdot g\cdot g^{\prime}\in B_{i}\}/B_{i}.

We have a well-defined map

q_{i}:(G/B_{i})^{g}\to T,\quad g^{\prime}\mapsto\overline{g^{\prime}{}^{-1}\cdot g\cdot g^{\prime}},

where
b\mapsto\overline{b}
denotes the projection
B_{i}\to T
.

Then

\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{i})^{T,\chi})\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}((G/B_{i})^{g},q_{i}^{!}(\chi)).

6.5.7.
Geometric interpretation

Consider the diagram

\begin{CD}(G/B_{1}\cap B_{2})^{g}@>{}>{}>(G/B_{2})^{g}@>{}>{q_{2}}>T\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(G/B_{1})^{g}\\
@V{q_{1}}V{}V\\
T.\end{CD}

If
g
is regular semi-simple, both maps

(6.21)

\begin{CD}(G/B_{1}\cap B_{2})^{g}@>{}>{}>(G/B_{2})^{g}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(G/B_{1})^{g}\end{CD}

are isomorphisms.

The resulting identification

(6.22)

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}((G/B_{1})^{g},q_{i}^{!}(\chi))\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}((G/B_{2})^{g},q_{i}^{!}(\chi))

is the fiber at
g
of the isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi)|_{G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi)|_{G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}

of Lemma
6.5.2
.

6.5.8.
Comparing the two identifications

Let
Y_{i}
be the preimage in
G/N_{i}
of
(G/B_{i})^{g}\subset G/B_{i}
. Let

Y_{1,2}
be the preimage in
G/N_{1}\cap N_{2}
of
(G/B_{1}\cap B_{2})^{g}\subset G/B_{1}\cap B_{2}
.

We have a 1-isomorphism

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{1})^{T,\chi}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{2})^{T,\chi},

given by pull-push along

\begin{CD}Y_{1,2}@>{}>{}>Y_{2}\\
@V{}V{}V\\
Y_{1},\end{CD}

which is compatible with (
6.19
) via the
g
-equivariant 1-morphisms

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{i})^{T,\chi}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{i})^{T,\chi}.

In particular, we have a commutative diagram in
\operatorname{Vect}

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{1})^{T,\chi})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{2})^{T,\chi})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{1})^{T,\chi})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{2})^{T,\chi}),\end{CD}

which fits into the diagram

(6.23)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}((G/B_{1})^{g},q_{1}^{!}(\chi))@>{}>{}>\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}((G/B_{1})^{g},q_{2}^{!}(\chi))\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{1})^{T,\chi})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{2})^{T,\chi})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{1})^{T,\chi})@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{2})^{T,\chi})\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{1}}(\chi))_{g}@>{}>{\text{\eqref{e:Springer fibers}}}>(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B_{2}}(\chi))_{g}\end{CD}

in which the composite vertical arrows

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}((G/B_{i})^{g},q_{i}^{!}(\chi))\to\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(Y_{i})^{T,\chi})\to\operatorname{Tr}(g,\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N_{i})^{T,\chi})

are the identifications of Sect.
6.5.6
.

Hence, it remains to show that the top horizontal arrow in (
6.23
) is the identification of (
6.22
). However,
the latter is manifest, as the maps in diagram (
6.21
) are isomorphisms.

∎[Theorem
6.5.4
]

6.6.
Ambidexterity of the Grothendieck-Springer functor

6.6.1.

Recall that in Theorem
5.7.3
, we proved that the 1-morphism

\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

is 2-adjointable, and its (both left and right) adjoints identify with
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
.

Hence, by Proposition
1.7.6
, we obtain: an identification between
the functor

(6.24)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})

and the
left
adjoint of

(6.25)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}).

6.6.2.

Recall now that under the identifications

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\text{ and }\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)),

the functors (
6.24
) and (
6.25
) identify with

{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

and

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

respectively.

6.6.3.

Hence, we obtain:

Corollary 6.6.4
.

There exists a canonical identification between

{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

and a
left
adjoint of

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

Remark 6.6.5
.

Recall (see Sect.
3.3.11
) that in addition to identification of Corollary
6.6.4
we have a
(rather tautological) identification between

{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

and a
right
adjoint of

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

In particular, the
(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P},{\mathfrak{r}}_{P})
becomes
ambidexterous
once restricted
to character sheaves.

6.6.6.

Consider again the usual
(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P},{\mathfrak{r}}_{P})
-adjunction, and let us conjugate it by Verdier duality.
We obtain an adjunction

(6.26)

({\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)},{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}),

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))^{c}\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))^{c}.

6.6.7.

Note now that the functor
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}
satisfies

{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}.

Denote

\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}:={\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)},

and let us ind-extend this functor from
\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))^{c}
to all of
\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
.

Thus, (
6.26
) can be rewritten as an adjunction

(6.27)

(\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{P},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}),

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

6.6.8.

In particular, restricting (
6.27
) to character sheaves, we obtain an adjunction

(6.28)

(\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P},\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}),

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\leftrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)),

where

\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}|_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))}.

Hence, combining with Corollary
6.6.4
, we obtain:

Corollary 6.6.9
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

(6.29)

\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

Remark 6.6.10
.

As was explained to us by R. Bezrukavnikov, one can prove Corollary
6.6.9
directly, without involving
traces on
\operatorname{AGCat}
. We will return to this in Sect.
7.6.2
.

Remark 6.6.11
.

Note that by construction, the identification (
6.29
) is involutive in the sense that the composition

{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq{\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:restr and Verdier}}}{\simeq}{\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:restr and Verdier}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\simeq{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}

is the identity.

7.
Further properties of the Grothendieck-Springer functor

The main goal of this section is to establish the transitivity property of the functional
equation (this is Theorem
7.1.2
below).

In addition, we will use the Serre functor on
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
to give streamlined proofs
for assertions about the Harish-Chandra transform of character sheaves.

7.1.
Transitivity of the functional equation

7.1.1.

Let
P_{1},P_{2}
and
P_{3}
be three parabolic subgroups of
G
containing a common Levi subgroup
M
. Consider the natural transformations

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}},

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\overset{i_{P_{2},P_{3}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{3}}

and

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{3}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{3}}.

We will prove the following assertion:

Theorem 7.1.2
.

There exists
an
somorphism

(7.1)

i_{P_{2},P_{3}}\circ i_{P_{1},P_{2}}\simeq i_{P_{1},P_{3}}

as natural transformations
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\to\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{3}}
.

The next few subsections are devoted to the proof of this theorem.

Remark 7.1.3
.

Our construction of the isomorphism (
7.1
) depends on some combinatorial choices.
However, we conjecture that it is actually canonical, i.e., we have a Grothendieck-Springer functor

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{M,G}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

that only depends on the pair
M
and
G
, and moreover it does so in a canonical way.

In particular, we conjecture that the functor
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{M,G}
is acted on by
\operatorname{Norm}(M)/M
.

Note that for
M=T
, this means that the corresponding functor
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{T,G}
is acted
on by the Weyl group. (Note that this is
not
in contradiction with
[
Gu
, Theorem D]
, as the latter
talks about all of
\operatorname{Shv}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))
and not
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{M,G}
.

7.1.4.

Note that for a triple of parabolics, there is a well-defined notion of their relative positions adding up: this
means that

N(P_{1})\cap N(P_{3})\subset N(P_{2}).

For
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
as in Sect.
7.1.1
, if their relative positions add up, then

(7.2)

I_{P_{1},P_{3}}\circ I_{P_{1},P_{2}}\simeq I_{P_{1},P_{3}}

as 2-morphisms
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{1}}\to{\mathbf{ind}}_{P_{3}}
, and the assertion of Theorem
7.1.2
is
obtained by applying trace to (
7.2
).

7.1.5.

We now consider the case opposite to one in Sect.
7.1.4
.

The key ingredient in the proof of Theorem
7.1.2
is the following:

Theorem 7.1.6
.

For a pair of parabolics
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
, exists a canonical isomorphism

i_{P_{2},P_{1}}\circ i_{P_{1},P_{2}}\simeq\operatorname{id}

as endofunctors of
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}
.

7.2.
The implication Theorem
7.1.6

\Rightarrow
Theorem
7.1.2

7.2.1.

Let
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
be a pair parabolics sharing a Levi subgroup. We shall say that they are
adjacent

if there exists a parabolic
Q
containing
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}
, and such that the projections of
P^{\prime}
and
P^{\prime\prime}

to
L:=Q/N(Q)
are
sub-maximal opposite
parabolics in
L
.

7.2.2.

We have the following combinatorial statement
17
17

17

We learnt the proof of Lemma
7.2.3
from K. Lin; however, it is possible that
the assertion has been known classically.
:

Lemma 7.2.3
.

For a pair of parabolics
P_{1}\neq P_{2}
sharing a Levi subgroup, let
P^{\prime}_{1}
be another such parabolic adjacent to
P_{1}
.
Then:

(a) Exactly one of the following two scenarios occurs:

(1)

Either the relative positions
P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},P_{2}
add up;

(2)

Or the relative positions
P^{\prime}_{1},P_{1},P_{2}
add up.

(b) There exists
P^{\prime}_{1}
so that scenario (1) occurs.

Proof.

We will prove point (a); point (b) is proved along the same lines.

Recall that for a pair of parabolic subgroups
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
(with Levi quotients

L_{1}
and
L_{2}
, respectively), the image of the projection

Q_{1}\cap Q_{2}/Q_{1}\cap N(Q_{2})\to M_{2}

is a parabolic subgroup in
M_{2}
, and similarly for
1
and
2
swapped.

For
P_{1}
and
P^{\prime}_{1}
as in the lemma, let
Q
be a parabolic that contains
P_{1}
and
P^{\prime}_{1}
submaximally; let
L

denote its Levi quotient. Denote by
Q_{1}
and
Q^{\prime}_{1}
the images of

P_{1}\cap Q\to L\leftarrow P^{\prime}_{1}\cap Q,

respectively. They are two submaximal opposite parabolics with
Q_{1}\cap Q^{\prime}_{1}\simeq M
.

Let
Q_{2}
denote the image of
Q\cap P_{2}
in
L
. Since
M\subset P_{2}
, we obtain that
Q_{2}
contains
(the image of)
M
. Thus, we obtain that
Q_{2}
is
yet another
submaximal parabolic in
L
that contains
(the image of)
M
. This implies that either
Q_{2}=Q_{1}
or
Q_{2}=Q^{\prime}_{1}
.

By symmetry, let us assume the latter. We claim that in this case the relative positions
P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1},P_{2}
add up,
i.e.,

N(P_{1})\cap N(P_{2})\subset N(P^{\prime}_{1}).

Choose a maximal torus
T\subset M
, so that we can consider the root decomposition with respect to it.
Thus, let
\alpha
be a root in
N(P_{1})\cap N(P_{2})
. We claim that
\alpha
is in fact contained in
N(Q)
;
this would imply that
\alpha
is contained in
N(P^{\prime}_{1})
as
N(Q)\subset N(P^{\prime}_{1})
.

Indeed, if
\alpha\notin N(Q)
, then
\alpha\in N(Q_{1})
. Since
\alpha\in N(P_{2})
, we also have

\alpha\in N(Q_{2})
. However, since
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
are opposite parabolics in
L
, there
are no roots common to
N(Q_{1})
and
N(Q_{2})
, which is a contradiction.

∎

7.2.4.

Let us show how Lemma
7.2.3
and Theorem
7.1.6
imply Theorem
7.1.2
.

We argue by induction on the distance
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
, i.e.,
\dim(P_{1}/P_{1}\cap P_{2})=\dim(P_{2}/P_{1}\cap P_{2})
.

The base case is when
P_{1}=P_{2}
, in which case there is nothing to prove.

7.2.5.

Next we consider the case when
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
are adjacent. Then by Lemma
7.2.3
(a) there are
two scenarios:

(a) Either the relative positions of
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}
add up;

(b) Or the relative positions of
P_{2},P_{1},P_{3}
add up.

In case (a) we are done by Sect.
7.1.4
.

In case (b), we have

i_{P_{2},P_{3}}\circ i_{P_{1},P_{2}}\overset{\text{Sect.~\ref{sss:add up}}}{\simeq}i_{P_{1},P_{3}}\circ i_{P_{2},P_{1}}\circ i_{P_{1},P_{2}}\overset{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:involut}}}{\simeq}i_{P_{1},P_{3}},

as required.

7.2.6.

We now perform the induction step, i.e., we will show how to reduce the statement for
a given triple
(P_{1},P_{2},P_{3})
to another one
(P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2},P^{\prime}_{3})
for which the distance
between
P^{\prime}_{1}
and
P^{\prime}_{2}
is smaller than that between
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

We will take
P^{\prime}_{2}=P_{2}
and
P^{\prime}_{3}=P_{3}
, and we let
P^{\prime}_{1}
be as in Lemma
7.2.3
(b). Note
that in situation of Sect.
7.1.4
, the distances also add up, i.e., the distance between

P^{\prime}_{1}
and
P_{2}
is indeed smaller than the distance between
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

We have

i_{P_{2},P_{3}}\circ i_{P_{1},P_{2}}\overset{\text{Sect.~\ref{sss:add up}}}{\simeq}i_{P_{2},P_{3}}\circ i_{P^{\prime}_{1},P_{2}}\circ i_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}\overset{\text{induction hypoth.}}{\simeq}i_{P^{\prime}_{1},P_{3}}\circ i_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}\overset{\text{adjacent case}}{\simeq}i_{P_{1},P_{3}}.

∎[Theorem
7.1.2
]

7.3.
Explicit description of the functional equation

In this subsection we will give an explicit description of the natural transformation

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G).

7.3.1.

Recall that the functor
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}
is given by pull-push along the diagram

\begin{CD}P/\operatorname{Ad}(P)@>{{\mathsf{p}}_{P}}>{}>G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{{\mathsf{q}}_{P}}V{}V\\
M/\operatorname{Ad}(M).\end{CD}

Consider the functor

(7.3)

\operatorname{CH}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

given by pull-push along

(7.4)

\begin{CD}G/\operatorname{Ad}(P)@>{}>{}>G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M).\end{CD}

Let
\iota_{P}
denote the functor

(7.5)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

given by pull-push along

(7.6)

\begin{CD}(N(P)\backslash P/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)@>{}>{}>(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
M/\operatorname{Ad}(M).\end{CD}

We have

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}\simeq\operatorname{CH}_{P}\circ\iota_{P}

7.3.2.

Consider the category

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},

where the spec.fin condition is taken with respect to the left or equivalently right action of
M
(or both).

Set

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)):=\mathbf{inv}_{\Delta(M)}\left(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\right).

The functors
\operatorname{CH}_{P}
and
\iota_{P}
restrict to functors

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

and

\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)),

respectively, and we have

(7.7)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}.

7.3.3.

With the above notations, applying (
1.8
), we obtain that the isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\to}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

is equal to the composition

(7.8)

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1})\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}},

where the two arrows are described as follows.

7.3.4.

We start with the second arrow in (
7.8
). We claim that there is a canonical isomorphism

(7.9)

((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1})\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}.

To construct it, we rewrite the sought for isomorphism as

(7.10)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}.

The latter follows from

(7.11)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}(I_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota_{P_{2}},

which is a valid isomorphism, as the two sides are given by pull-push along

(7.12)

\begin{CD}\Bigl(N(P_{1})\backslash(N(P_{1})\cdot M\cdot N(P_{2}))/N(P_{2})\Bigr)/\operatorname{Ad}(M)@>{}>{}>\Bigl(N(P_{1})\backslash G/N(P_{2})\Bigr)/\operatorname{Ad}(M)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
M/\operatorname{Ad}(M).\end{CD}

7.3.5.

We now consider the first arrow in (
7.8
). We claim that there is a canonical isomorphism

(7.13)

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1})\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}).

To construct it, we rewrite the sought for isomorphism as

(7.14)

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\simeq\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}).

The latter follows from

(7.15)

\operatorname{CH}_{P_{1}}\circ(I_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\simeq\operatorname{CH}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I_{P_{1},P_{2}}),

which is a valid isomorphism, as the two sides are given by pull-push along

\begin{CD}(G\overset{M\cdot(N(P_{2})\cap N(P_{1}))}{\times}G)/\operatorname{Ad}(G)@>{}>{}>G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(G/N(P_{2})\overset{M}{\times}N(P_{1})\backslash G)/\operatorname{Ad}(G)\\
@V{}V{\sim}V\\
(N(P_{2})\backslash G/N(P_{1}))/\operatorname{Ad}(M).\end{CD}

7.4.
Proof of Theorem
7.1.6

This proof will be essentially a tautology modulo one ingredient: the role of the inversion involution on
G
,
which exchanges the roles of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

7.4.1.

Recall that we denote by
\sigma_{G}
and
\sigma_{M}
the inversion automorphisms of
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
and

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))
, respectively.

By Lemma
6.4.6
, we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}\circ\sigma_{M}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}\circ\sigma_{M},\end{CD}

Thus, the assertion of Theorem
7.1.6
is equivalent to the commutativity of the diagram

(7.16)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\sim}>{}>\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\sigma_{M}\\
@V{{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}}V{}V@A{}A{i_{P_{2},P_{1}}}A\\
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}@>{\sim}>{}>\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\sigma_{M}.\end{CD}

7.4.2.

We denote by
i^{\sigma}_{P_{1},P_{2}}
the isomorphism

(7.17)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\to\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}},

obtained from isomorphism
i_{P_{1},P_{2}}
defined in Sect.
7.3.3
by interchanging the left and the right factors.
Explicitly, it is defined as the composition

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}},

where the two arrows are deduced from isomorphisms

(7.18)

(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

and

(7.19)

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})\simeq\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}\otimes\operatorname{Id}),

obtained from the corresponding isomorphisms (
7.10
) and (
7.14
) by interchanging
the roles of
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
.

7.4.3.

Note that diagram (
7.16
) decomposes as

(7.20)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}=\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\sim}>{}>\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\sigma_{M}\\
@V{{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}}V{}V@A{}A{i^{\sigma}_{P_{2},P_{1}}}A@A{}A{i_{P_{2},P_{1}}}A\\
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}=\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}@>{\sim}>{}>\sigma_{G}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\sigma_{M},\end{CD}

so it remains to show that its both inner squares are commutative.

The commutation of the right inner square follows from the fact that the involutions interchange the left and the right factors.
So it remains to show the commutativity of the left inner square.

7.4.4.

By definition, the left inner square of diagram (
7.20
) is

(7.21)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{(\ref{e:intertwiner as compos 2 ch})}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}&&\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{(\ref{e:intertwiner as compos 1 ch})}V{}V@V{}V{(\ref{e:intertwiner as compos 1 ch})}V\\
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}&&\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{(\ref{e:intertwiner as compos 2 ch})}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}.\end{CD}

Let
\phi_{1,2}
denote the isomorphism

(7.22)

((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

equal to the composition

((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\simeq(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{(\ref{e:intertwiner as compos 2 ch})}{\simeq}\\
\simeq(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}.

The map
\phi_{1,2}
provides the two missing horizontal arrows in (
7.21
). Hence, to prove the commutation of
(
7.21
), it suffices to show that the resulting three inner squares commute.

7.4.5.

The resulting
middle
inner square in (
7.21
) is

\begin{CD}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\phi_{1,2}}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:intertwiner as compos 1 ch}}}V{}V@V{}V{\text{\eqref{e:intertwiner as compos 1 ch}}}V\\
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\phi_{1,2}}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}},\end{CD}

and it commutes tautologically.

The top and the bottom inner squares are

\begin{CD}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{{\text{\eqref{e:intertwiner as compos 2 ch}}}}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\phi_{1,2}}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\circ(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\end{CD}

and

\begin{CD}\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\phi_{1,2}}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})^{-1})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ((I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{2},P_{1}})^{-1}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P_{1},P_{2}})\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}@>{\text{\eqref{e:intertwiner as compos 2 ch}}}>{}>\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\circ\iota^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}},\end{CD},

respectively, and they commute by the definition of the map
\phi_{1,2}
.

∎[Theorem
7.1.6
]

7.5.
A theorem of [CY] and [GIT]

In this subsection we will use some of the ideas introduced in Sect.
7.3
to reprove a theorem from
[
CY
]

and
[
GIT
]
.

7.5.1.

Let

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)

be the functor given by pull-push along the diagram

(7.23)

\begin{CD}G\overset{P}{\times}G@>{}>{}>G\\
@V{}V{}V\\
G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G.\end{CD}

By construction, it is the counit of the
({\mathbf{ind}}_{P},\mathbf{jacq}_{P})
-adjunction.

7.5.2.

Set

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)),

where the spec.fin condition refers to either the action of
G
on one or both sides (the result is the same by Lemma
5.3.2
).

Set

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}:=\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}|_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}.

This is the counit of the
({\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P},\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})
-adjunction.

7.5.3.

By Theorem
5.8.4
and formula (
5.21
), we have:

(7.24)

\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}\simeq((\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P})^{R})^{R}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P}\circ I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}}))[-2\dim(N(P))].

7.5.4.

Let

\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)

be the functor given by pull-push along the diagram

(7.25)

\begin{CD}G\overset{P}{\times}G@>{}>{}>G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G\\
@V{}V{}V\\
G.\end{CD}

Denote:

\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}:=\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}|_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}},\quad\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

Note now that the functor
\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}
(resp.,
\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext,\sec.fin}}_{P}
) is the right adjoint of
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}
(resp.,
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext,\sec.fin}}_{P}
),
up to a cohomological shift by
2\dim(N(P))
.

7.5.5.

Let

\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

be the
left
adjoint of
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}
. It is given by
*-pull and !-push
along
the diagram (
7.25
).

Thus, we obtain that (
7.24
) is equivalent to

Corollary 7.5.6
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(7.26)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}})\circ\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1}))\circ\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext,spec.fin}}_{P}

as of functors

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P^{-})\backslash G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

7.5.7.

Applying
\mathbf{inv}_{\Delta(G)}
to
\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ext}}_{P}
, we obtain a functor

\operatorname{HC}_{P}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)),

which is given by pull-push along

(7.27)

\begin{CD}G/\operatorname{Ad}(P)@>{}>{}>(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
G/\operatorname{Ad}(G).\end{CD}

Let
\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}
be the
left
adjoint of
\operatorname{CH}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}
.
It is given by
*-pull and !-push
along
the diagram (
7.27
).

7.5.8.

Let

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)):=\mathbf{inv}_{G}(\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/N(P)\overset{M}{\times}N(P)\backslash G)^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}).

Denote

\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:=\operatorname{HC}_{P}|_{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))},\quad\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}((N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

7.5.9.

Applying
\mathbf{inv}_{G}
to both sides of (
7.26
), we obtain:

Corollary 7.5.10
.

There exists a canonical isomorphism

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}})\circ\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1})\circ\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}

as functors

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G)\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P)/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

7.5.11.

We evaluate the isomorphism of Corollary
7.5.10
on
\operatorname{pt}
and obtain an isomorphism

(7.28)

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P,P^{-}})\circ\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1})\circ\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P)/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

7.5.12.

Note now that we have a canonical identification

{\mathbb{D}}_{(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)}\simeq\widetilde{\operatorname{HC}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\operatorname{Shv}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P)/\operatorname{Ad}(M)).

In addition, we have

{\mathbb{D}}_{(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{-}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\simeq(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1}.

7.5.13.

Hence, from (
7.28
), we obtain:

Corollary 7.5.14
.

The functor

(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1})\circ\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-})/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

commutes with Verdier duality.

Remark 7.5.15
.

The statement of Corollary
7.5.14
has been known thanks to
[
CY
]
and
[
GIT
]
(in a different generality),
where it is proved by a different method.

7.6.
A zoo of (un)natural isomorphisms

In this subsection we will revisit some of the isomorphisms established earlier
(and will assert that we do not obtain anything new).

This subsection can be regarded as “bonus material” and can be skipped.

7.6.1.

Let us think of the functor
{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}
as the right adjoint of the functor
\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}
.
By adjunction, the isomorphism

\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}{\to}\operatorname{GrothSpr}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

induces an isomorphism

{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}},

which we denote by
i^{\mathfrak{r}}_{P_{2},P_{1}}
.

In this subsection we will explain a different way to obtain the isomorphism

i^{\mathfrak{r}}_{P_{,}P_{2}}:{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{1}}\overset{\sim}{\to}{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P_{2}}

in the particular case when
P_{1}=P
and
P_{2}=P^{-}
are opposite parabolics.

7.6.2.

Let
\jmath_{P}
denote the functor given by pull-push along the diagram

(7.29)

\begin{CD}(N(P)\backslash P/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)@>{}>{}>M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(N(P)\backslash G/N(P))/\operatorname{Ad}(M),\end{CD}

which is the transpose of the diagram (
7.6
) that defines
\iota_{P}
.

We have:

(7.30)

\jmath_{P}\circ\operatorname{HC}_{P}\simeq{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}.

7.6.3.

Note that we have

\jmath_{P}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes I_{P^{-},P})\simeq\jmath_{P,P^{-}},

where
\jmath_{P,P^{-}}
is as in Sect.
5.8.7
.

Hence, we obtain:

\jmath^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq\jmath^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P,P^{-}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1}).

Combining with (
7.30
) we obtain:

(7.31)

{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}\simeq\jmath^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P,P^{-}}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\otimes(I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{-},P})^{-1})\circ\operatorname{HC}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P},

7.6.4.

Note that

{\mathbb{D}}_{M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\circ\jmath_{P,P^{-}}\circ{\mathbb{D}}_{(N(P)\backslash G/N(P^{-}))/\operatorname{Ad}(M)}\simeq\jmath_{P,P^{-}},

since
\jmath_{P,P^{-}}
is restriction along an open embedding.

Combining this with Corollary
7.5.14
and using (
7.31
),
we obtain:
18
18

18

The idea to deduce Corollary
7.6.5
from Corollary
7.5.14
was explained to us by R. Bezrukavnikov.

Corollary 7.6.5
.

The functor

{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))

commutes with Verdier duality.

However, unwinding, we obtain:

Lemma 7.6.6
.

The datum of commutation with Verdier duality on
{\mathfrak{r}}^{\operatorname{ch}}_{P}
given by Corollary
7.6.5

equals one given by Corollary
6.6.9
.

7.6.7.

We now recall the following corollary of Braden’s theorem (see
[
DrGa3
, Theorem 3.1.6]
):

Lemma 7.6.8
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\widetilde{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}\simeq{\mathfrak{r}}_{P^{-}}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\operatorname{Shv}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))
.

7.6.9.

Combining Lemma
7.6.8
with the isomorphism of Corollary
7.6.5
we obtain an isomorphism:

(7.32)

{\mathfrak{r}}_{P}^{\operatorname{ch}}\simeq{\mathfrak{r}}_{P^{-}}^{\operatorname{ch}}.

7.6.10.

However, unwinding, we obtain:

Lemma 7.6.11
.

The isomorphism (
7.32
) equals the isomorphism
i^{\mathfrak{r}}_{P,P^{-}}
.

8.
Applications to the Deligne-Lusztig theory

In this section we will apply the theory developed in the previous sections and
derive (both old and new) results about the Deligne-Lusztig functor.

8.1.
Trace of Frobenius on restricted representations

In this subsection, we show that (unlike the case of the identity functor), if we take the trace
of Frobenius on
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
(resp.,
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}
), we recover all of

\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))
(resp.,
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))
).

8.1.1.

Consider again the 1-morphism

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

It is adjointable and compatible with the Frobenius automorphism on the two sides.
Hence, it induces a 1-morphism

(8.1)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

in
\operatorname{AGCat}
.

8.1.2.

We claim:

Theorem 8.1.3
.

The 1-morphism (
8.1
) is an equivalence.

Remark 8.1.4
.

Theorem
8.1.3
is due to A. Eteve. The proof that we give is a reformulation
of the argument in
[
Ete
, Theorem 1.3.1]
.

8.1.5.

As an immediate corollary of Theorems
8.1.3
and
5.1.10
(b), we obtain:

Corollary 8.1.6
.

There exists a canonical equivalence

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),

whose image under
{\mathbf{i}}
produces the equivalence of (
8.1
).

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
8.1.3
.

8.1.7.

The fact that (
8.1
) is fully faithful follows immediately from Corollary
1.7.10
.
Hence, it remains to identify its essential image.

8.1.8.

We have to show that the essential image of

(8.2)

\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}},{\operatorname{Frob}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\simeq\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}(\operatorname{AGCat}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(G))\simeq\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))

generates the target. It is sufficient to show that the precomposition of (
8.2
) with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\simeq\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}},{\operatorname{Frob}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}

generates the target.

We note that the resulting functor

(8.3)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}},{\operatorname{Frob}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\operatorname{mon}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:HH Frob H}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})

factors as

(8.4)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\underset{\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}\otimes\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}},{\operatorname{Frob}}}{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}_{\operatorname{co}}\simeq\\
\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}),

while the composition of the first two arrows in (
8.4
) identifies with

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\overset{\operatorname{Av}^{\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T)}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}.

Hence, the functor (
8.3
) factors as

(8.5)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}\overset{\operatorname{Av}^{\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T)}_{*}}{\longrightarrow}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}).

Moreover, we have a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(N\backslash G/N)\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}@>{}>{}>\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))\\
&&@V{}V{}V\\
&&\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\underset{\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\otimes\underline{{\mathcal{H}}},{\operatorname{Frob}}}{\otimes}\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\\
&&@V{}V{\sim}V\\
&&\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\underline{{\mathcal{H}}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
&&@V{}V{\sim}V\\
&&\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\end{CD}

In the above diagram, the two top right vertical arrows generate their respective essential images.
Hence, to prove our assertion, it suffices to prove the following:

Proposition 8.1.9
.

The functor

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash G/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))

is an equivalence.

∎[Theorem
8.1.3
]

8.1.10.
Proof of Proposition
8.1.9

The functor in question is fully faithful, so we only have to show that it is essentially surjective.
To do so, we can work on the strata of the Schubert stratification. I.e., we wish to show that

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash BwB/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}((N\backslash BwB/N)/\operatorname{Ad}_{{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))

is equivalence.

The above arrow identifies with

(8.6)

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T/\operatorname{Ad}_{w\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T))^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(T/\operatorname{Ad}_{w\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T)),

so we have to show that (
8.6
) is an equivalence.

However, we claim that the proof of Lang’s theorem applies, and we have

T/\operatorname{Ad}_{w\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}}(T)\simeq\operatorname{pt}/T^{w}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),

where
T^{w}
is the Cartan subgroup equipped with a
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-structure twisted by
w
.

This makes the assertion manifest, since the (left) action of
T
on
\operatorname{pt}/T^{w}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})

is given by the homomorphism

T\to\operatorname{pt}/T^{w}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),

corresponding to the
T^{w}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
-cover

T\overset{t\mapsto w(t)\cdot{\operatorname{Frob}}(t^{-1})}{\longrightarrow}T.

∎[Proposition
8.1.9
]

8.2.
Comparing classes

This subsection is not essential for the main narrative and can be skipped on the first pass.

8.2.1.

Let us consider the functor

{\operatorname{Frob}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

It induces a commutative diagram

(8.7)

\begin{CD}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}@>{{\operatorname{Frob}}}>{}>G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\\
@A{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}A{}A@A{}A{\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}A\\
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}@>{{\operatorname{Frob}}}>{}>G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.\end{CD}

Since the horizontal arrows in the above diagram are equivalences, the next diagram also
commutes

(8.8)

\begin{CD}G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}@>{{\operatorname{Frob}}}>{}>G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\\
@V{(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}}V{}V@V{}V{(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}}V\\
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}@>{{\operatorname{Frob}}}>{}>G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.\end{CD}

8.2.2.

Let
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
be an object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, equipped with a map

\alpha:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}\to{\operatorname{Frob}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}).

Denote

\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:=\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\circ(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}).

By (
8.7
) and (
8.8
), the data of
\alpha
induces a map

\alpha^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to{\operatorname{Frob}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}),

so that the diagram

\begin{CD}\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}@>{\alpha}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})\\
@A{\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}A{}A@A{}A{{\operatorname{Frob}}(\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})}A\\
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}@>{\alpha^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\end{CD}

commutes.

8.2.3.

Assume that
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
is dualizable. By Sect.
3.5.2
, to
(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\alpha)
,
we can attach an object

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\alpha)\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

Note that by Lemma
5.5.5
,
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
is also dualizable, so that we can consider

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},\alpha^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

Since the map

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}

is adjointable, by Sect.
1.5.5
,
we obtain a map

(8.9)

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},\alpha^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\alpha)

in
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))
.

8.2.4.

We claim:

Proposition 8.2.5
.

The map (
8.9
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We can consider the object

\operatorname{cl}\left((\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}),(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\alpha)\right)\in\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})(\operatorname{pt}),

and the map (
8.9
) as obtained using the adjoint pair

(8.10)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}):\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}):\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R})

from the identification

\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},\alpha^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\left(\operatorname{cl}((\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}),(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\alpha))\right)

and the isomorphism

\operatorname{cl}\left((\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}),(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R}(\alpha)\right)\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},(\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{R})\left(\operatorname{cl}(\underline{{\mathbf{C}}},\alpha)\right).

The assertion of the proposition follows now from the fact that the functors (
8.10
)
are mutually inverse equivalences, by Theorem
8.1.3
.

∎

Combining Proposition
8.2.5
with Corollary
3.5.5
, we obtain:

Corollary 8.2.6
.

For
\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}
as above and
g\in G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, the map

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha\circ g,\underline{{\mathbf{C}}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha\circ g,\underline{{\mathbf{C}}})

is an isomorphism (in
\operatorname{Vect}
).

8.2.7.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a quasi-compact AG Verdier-compatible algebraic stack acted on by
G
. From Corollary
8.2.6
, we obtain:

Corollary 8.2.8
.

The map

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}{\simeq}\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

is an isomorphism, where by a slight abuse of notation, we denote by
\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
the object of
\operatorname{AGCat}
, underlying the
same-named object of
G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
.

8.2.9.
An application: the Howlett-Lehrer theorem

We will now use Corollary
8.2.8
to reprove the following result:

Theorem 8.2.10
.

Let
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
be a pair of rational parabolic subgroups of
G
that share a Levi subgroup. Then the
intertwining map

\mathsf{Funct}((G/N(P_{1}))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/N(P_{2}))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}),

given by pull-push along

\begin{CD}(G/N(P_{1})\cap N(P_{2}))({\mathbb{F}}_{q})@>{}>{}>(G/N(P_{2}))({\mathbb{F}}_{q})\\
@V{}V{}V\\
(G/N(P_{1}))({\mathbb{F}}_{q})\end{CD}

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

This is obtained by combining Corollary
8.2.8
and Proposition
5.6.6
.

∎

8.3.
Trace of Frobenius on character sheaves

In this subsection we will show that (the usual) categorical trace of Frobenius on

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))
recovers the space of class functions on
G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

8.3.1.

Consider the functor

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{ch}}:\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

It is adjointable and compatible with the action of the Frobenius endofunctor. Hence,
it induces a map (in
\operatorname{Vect}
):

(8.11)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\overset{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}{\simeq}\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

In this subsection we will prove:

Theorem 8.3.2
.

The map (
8.11
) is an isomorphism.

8.3.3.

We will now derive from Theorem
8.11
a statement that does
not
involve
\operatorname{AGCat}
,
and which is the counterpart of the Trace Conjecture of
[
AGKRRV1
]
for character sheaves:

Corollary 8.3.4
.

The composition

(8.12)

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\overset{\operatorname{LT}}{\to}\\
\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

is an isomorphism.

Proof of Corollary
8.3.4
.

By
[
GRV
, Theorem 5.4.12]
, the map

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\overset{\operatorname{LT}}{\to}\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

is the same as

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\overset{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}{\simeq}\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

Hence, it suffices to show that the map

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},{\mathbf{i}}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\to\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))

is an isomorphism.

In the above composition, the second arrow is an isomorphism by Proposition
6.1.18
, while the last arrow
is an isomorphism by Theorem
8.3.2
.

∎

8.3.5.

Note that from Corollary
3.7.8
, we obtain that the diagram

(8.13)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}))@>{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\operatorname{Frob}}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}))\\
@V{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:Frob restr}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:ch sheaves}}}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\underline{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))&&\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@V{\text{taking characters}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:Tr Frob on ch}}}V\\
\mathsf{Funct}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})@>{\sim}>{}>\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\end{CD}

commutes, and hence so does the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}))@>{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}>{\sim}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\operatorname{Frob}}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}))\\
@V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Frob restr}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr id restr}}}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))&&\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)))\\
@V{\text{taking characters}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Frob on ch}}}V\\
\mathsf{Funct}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})/\operatorname{Ad}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})@>{\sim}>{}>\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).\end{CD}

8.3.6.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
8.3.2
. Let us apply
Corollary
1.8.7
to the 1-morphism
t
being

\mathbf{emb}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

with
F_{1}
and
F_{2}
given by
{\operatorname{Frob}}
.
