We obtain a commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}))@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}))\\
@V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:2 traces}}}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\operatorname{Frob}}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}))@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\operatorname{Frob}}),\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})),\end{CD}

where:

•

The top horizontal arrow is obtained by applying
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},-)
to the functor

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})

of (
8.1
).

•

We identify

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))\text{ and }\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)),

so that
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},{\operatorname{Frob}})
in both cases is given by
{\operatorname{Frob}}^{!}
; hence the bottom horizontal arrow
is the map (
8.11
).

Now, the top horizontal arrow is an equivalence by Theorem
8.1.3
. Hence, so is the bottom horizontal arrow.

∎[Theorem
8.3.2
]

8.4.
Trace of Frobenius and induction

In this subsection we will deduce the key down-to-earth (i.e., numerical) consequence
of the theory we developed: that the Deligne-Lusztig functor
at the level of characters

equals the trace of Frobenius on the Grothendieck-Springer functor, see Corollary
8.4.8

for the precise assertion.

8.4.1.

Recall the 1-morphism

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

By Sect.
5.6.1
, it induces a 1-morphism

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

in
2\operatorname{-AGCat}

and also a 1-morphism

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P}:M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}\to G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{restr}}

in
2\operatorname{-DGCat}
.

8.4.2.

In Sect.
3.4.2
, we have supplied
{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}
with a datum of compatibility
with the endomorphisms
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}
and
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
, acting on the source and target,
respectively:

{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\overset{\delta}{\to}{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{P}

This datum automatically induces a datum of compatibility for
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}

and also for
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P}
:

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\overset{\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}}{\to}{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}

and

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\overset{\delta^{\operatorname{restr}}}{\to}{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P}.

Hence, we obtain the functor

(8.14)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}),

which makes the diagram

(8.15)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\\
@V{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:Frob restr}}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:Frob restr}}}V\\
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\delta,{\mathbf{ind}}_{P})=\text{\eqref{e:DL functor}}}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\\
@V{}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{}V\\
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))@>{\operatorname{DL}_{P}}>{}>{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))\end{CD}

commute, and similarly for “restr” instead of “spec.fin”.

Remark 8.4.3
.

Since the 1-morphism
{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}
is 2-adjointable,
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}
and
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}
are equivalences, and

\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
is also an equivalence, we can apply Proposition
1.7.6
. We obtain that the 1-morphism

\mathbf{jacq}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}:G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\to M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}},

equipped with the natural transformation
((\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{\operatorname{BC}})^{-1}

((\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})^{\operatorname{BC}})^{-1}:\mathbf{jacq}_{P}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\to{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\circ\mathbf{jacq}_{P}

induces a functor

(8.16)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G},G\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\to\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M},M\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),

which is right adjoint to
\operatorname{DL}_{P}
.

However, this does not give us anything new: unwinding, we obtain that the functor (
8.16
)
is given by the bimodule

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(\mathcal{DL}_{P}),

which is dual to the bimodule

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(\mathcal{DL}_{P},\omega_{\mathcal{DL}_{P}}),

which defines the Deligne-Lusztig functor
\operatorname{DL}_{P}
.

8.4.4.

By Proposition
5.6.6
, the 2-morphism
\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

is actually an isomorphism, and hence is adjointable. Hence, by Sect.
1.5.5

it induces a natural transformation (in fact, an isomorphism)

(8.17)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})}{\Rightarrow}{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}

as functors

\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

By (
1.7
), from (
8.17
) we obtain a map

(8.18)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)))\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}),\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))).

Similarly, we obtain a natural transformation (in fact, an isomorphism)

(8.19)

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{restr}})}{\Rightarrow}{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

and a map

(8.20)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)))\overset{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{restr}}),\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}})}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))).

Remark 8.4.5
.

Unwinding, we obtain that the natural transformation
\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}
is the composition

{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\overset{I^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P,P^{\prime}}}{\simeq}{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P^{\prime}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}\simeq{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}_{P}.

From here, we obtain that the natural transformation
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})
in (
8.17
) is the composition

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\overset{i_{P,P^{\prime}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\simeq{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}.

8.4.6.

Thus, we can apply Corollary
1.8.7
and, taking into account (
8.13
) and (
8.15
), we obtain:

Corollary 8.4.7
.

The following diagram commutes

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\operatorname{DL}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))\\
@V{\rm{taking\,\,characters}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\rm{taking\,\,characters}}V\\
\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})&&\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\\
@A{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:Tr Frob on ch}}}A{\sim}A@A{\sim}A{\text{Theorem~\ref{t:Tr Frob on ch}}}A\\
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)))@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{spec.fin}}),\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{spec.fin}})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))).\end{CD}

Hence, we also obtain:

Corollary 8.4.8
.

The following diagram commutes

(8.21)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\operatorname{DL}_{P})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Id},{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})))\\
@V{\rm{taking\,\,characters}}V{\sim}V@V{\sim}V{\rm{taking\,\,characters}}V\\
\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})&&\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\\
@A{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Frob on ch}}}A{\sim}A@A{\sim}A{\text{Corollary~\ref{c:Tr Frob on ch}}}A\\
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M)))@>{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{restr}}),\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}})}>{}>\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))).\end{CD}

Remark 8.4.9
.

As was mentioned in the Introduction, a particular case of Corollary
8.4.8
for
q\gg 0
was established
in
[
Lu3
]
, and in
loc. cit
it was conjectured to hold without that assumption.

8.5.
What does Corollary
8.4.8
actually say?

8.5.1.

Let

\operatorname{ch}(\operatorname{DL}_{P}):\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

denote the map arising from the
upper
portion of (
8.21
).

This is the map on
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}K^{0}(-)
induced by the Deligne-Lusztig functor

\operatorname{DL}_{P}:{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})).

8.5.2.

Let us denote by
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P})
the map

(8.22)

\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

arising from the
lower
portion of (
8.21
). (Below,
we will characterize the map
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P})
concrete terms, see (
8.27
).)

Corollary
8.4.8
says that

(8.23)

\operatorname{ch}(\operatorname{DL}_{P})=\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P})

as maps

\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

8.5.3.

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a
Verdier-compatible
19
19

19

See
[
GRV
, Sect. 4.6.1]
for what this means.

quasi-compact algebraic stack and
\phi
its endomorphism. Let
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
be a compact object of
\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})

equipped with a map

\alpha^{\prime}:{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\to\phi^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}).

To this datum, we can attach an element

\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Tr}(\phi^{!},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})).

For a point
y

\operatorname{pt}\overset{i_{y}}{\to}{\mathcal{Y}}^{\phi},

we obtain an endomorphism
\alpha^{\prime}_{y}
of
i_{y}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
and we can consider

(8.24)

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha^{\prime}_{y},i_{y}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}))\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}.

Denote
{\mathcal{F}}:={\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})
. Then
\alpha^{\prime}
induces a map

\widetilde{\alpha}:{\mathcal{F}}\to\phi_{\blacktriangle}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq\phi_{*}({\mathcal{F}}),

and hence a map

\alpha:\phi^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}.

To the above data, we can attach an element

\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\alpha})\in\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{\blacktriangle},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})).

For
y
as above, the map
\alpha
induces an endomorphism
\alpha_{y}
of
i_{y}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})
, and we can consider

(8.25)

\operatorname{Tr}(\alpha_{y},i_{y}^{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\in\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}.

It is easy to see, however, that (
8.24
) equals (
8.25
)
and

(8.26)

\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\alpha})

as elements of

\operatorname{Tr}(\phi^{!},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\simeq\operatorname{Tr}((\phi^{!})^{\vee},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})^{\vee})\simeq\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{\blacktriangle},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}})),

see Sect.
1.2.8
.

8.5.4.

We will apply this to
\phi
being the geometric Frobenius of
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Denote the element
of
\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
given by (
8.24
) (equialently, (
8.25
)) by

\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}},\alpha).

According to
[
GaVa
, Theorem 0.8]
, the above function equals to the image of

\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime})=\operatorname{cl}({\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\alpha})

under the canonical map

\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{DGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle},\operatorname{Shv}({\mathcal{Y}}))\to\operatorname{Tr}_{\operatorname{AGCat}}({\operatorname{Frob}}_{\blacktriangle},\underline{\operatorname{Shv}}({\mathcal{Y}}))\overset{\operatorname{LT}^{\operatorname{AG}}}{\simeq}\mathsf{Funct}({\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

8.5.5.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M}
be a compact object of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))
, equipped with a map

\alpha^{\prime}_{M}:{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M}\to{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M}).

Set

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{G}:=\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M}).

By (
8.17
), the data of
\alpha^{\prime}_{M}
induces a data

\alpha^{\prime}_{G}:{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{G}\to{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{G}).

By Sect.
8.5.4
we have

(8.27)

\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P})(\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M},\alpha^{\prime}_{M}))=\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{G},\alpha^{\prime}_{G}),

where
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P})
is the map (
8.22
).

This is the promised concrete characterization of the map
\operatorname{Tr}({\operatorname{Frob}},\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P})
.

8.5.6.

Thus, from (
8.23
) we obtain:

Corollary 8.5.7
.

Let
\rho_{M}
be a compact object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(M({\mathbb{F}}_{q}))
and let
({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M},\alpha^{\prime}_{M})
be as above.
Assume that

(8.28)

\operatorname{ch}(\rho_{M})=\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M},\alpha^{\prime}_{M})

as elements of
\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
. Then for

\rho_{G}:=\operatorname{DL}_{P}(\rho_{M})\text{ and }({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{G},\alpha^{\prime}_{G})\text{ as in above},

we have

\operatorname{ch}(\rho_{G})=\mathsf{funct}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{G},\alpha^{\prime}_{G})

as elements of
\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})
.

8.6.
An application: characters of Deligne-Lusztig representations

In this subsection we will re-derive the (well-known) result that says that characters of the
(usual) Deligne-Lusztig representations are given by pointwise traces of Frobenius on the
Springer sheaf.

8.6.1.

Let
T
be a rational Cartan subgroup in
G
, and let
\chi
be a 1-dimensional character sheaf on it,
equipped with a Weil structure

\alpha_{T}:{\operatorname{Frob}}_{T}^{*}(\chi)\simeq\chi.

Let

\chi^{0}:=\mathsf{funct}(\chi,\alpha_{T})

be the corresponding character on
T({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
, cf. Sect.
3.5.10
, and let

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}}
denote the corresponding 1-dimensional representation of
T({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

Choose a (not necessarily rational) Borel
B
containing
T
. Consider

\operatorname{DL}_{B}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}})\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G({\mathbb{F}}_{q})),

and consider

\operatorname{ch}(\operatorname{DL}_{B}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}}))\in\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

8.6.2.

Consider now the Grothendieck-Springer sheaf

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi)\in\operatorname{Shv}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G)).

We equip it with the Weil structure

\alpha_{G}:{\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}_{G}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi))\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi)

as follows.

8.6.3.

Since
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi)
(and
{\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}_{G}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi))
) are Goresky-MacPherson extensions
of their respective restrictions to
G^{\operatorname{rs}}/\operatorname{Ad}(G)
, it suffices to specify the data of
\alpha_{G}
over
this locus.

Denote

B^{\prime}:={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{-1}(B).

A priori, we have

{\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}_{G}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi))\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B^{\prime}}({\operatorname{Frob}}^{*}_{T}(\chi))\overset{\alpha_{T}}{\simeq}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B^{\prime}}(\chi).

We now identify

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B^{\prime}}(\chi)\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi)

using Lemma
6.5.2
.

8.6.4.

We will now reprove the following result (originally due to Lusztig,
[
Lu2
]
):

Theorem 8.6.5
.

The functions

\operatorname{ch}(\operatorname{DL}_{B}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}}))\text{ and }\mathsf{funct}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi),\alpha_{G})

are equal.

Proof.

Denote
\chi^{\prime}={\mathbb{D}}(\chi)
, so that

\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi^{\prime})\simeq{\mathbb{D}}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi)).

We will apply Corollary
8.5.7
to

M=T,\,\,P=B,\,\,{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{M}:=\chi^{\prime},\,\,\rho_{M}:=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}}.

Note that (
8.28
) holds by construction. Hence, the equality

\operatorname{ch}(\operatorname{DL}_{B}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}^{\chi^{0}}))=\mathsf{funct}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi^{\prime}),\alpha^{\prime}_{G})=\mathsf{funct}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi),\alpha_{G})

holds with the following caveat:

The datum of

\alpha^{\prime}_{G}:\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}(\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{B}(\chi^{\prime}))

is constructed by (
8.17
) rather than by the procedure in Sect.
8.6.3
.

However, the two agree by Theorem
6.5.4
.

∎

8.7.
Independence of Deligne-Lusztig characters of the choice of a parabolic

8.7.1.

In this subsection we will prove the following result:

Theorem 8.7.2
.

Let
M
be a Levi subgroup of
G
defined over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. Then the map

\operatorname{ch}(\operatorname{DL}_{P}):\mathsf{Funct}((M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\to\mathsf{Funct}((G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})

does
not
depend on the choice of the parabolic
P
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of this theorem.

8.7.3.

By Corollary
8.4.8
, it suffices to show that the map
\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{restr}}),\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P}^{\operatorname{restr}})

does not depend on the choice of the parabolic.

Let
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
be two such parabolics. Consider the intertwining operator

i_{P_{1},P_{2}}:\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\simeq\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}.

It suffices to show that the diagram

(8.29)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ\operatorname{Frob}_{M}^{!}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ\operatorname{Frob}_{M}^{!}\\
@V{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{1}})}V{}V@V{}V{\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{id},\delta^{\operatorname{restr}}_{P_{2}})}V\\
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\end{CD}

as functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(M/\operatorname{Ad}(M))\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ch}}(G/\operatorname{Ad}(G))

commutes.

8.7.4.

Using Remark
8.4.5
, we can rewrite the above diagram as

(8.30)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\\
@V{i_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}}V{}V@V{}V{i_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}}V\\
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}&&\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}},\end{CD}

where
P^{\prime}_{i}={\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{-1}(P_{i})
.

Note that the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}@>{i_{P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\\
@V{\sim}V{}V@V{}V{\sim}V\\
{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>{\operatorname{Frob}}_{G}^{!}\circ\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\end{CD}

commutes tautologically.

Hence, in order to establish the commutativity of (
8.30
), it suffices to show that the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\\
@V{i_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}}V{}V@V{}V{i_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}}V\\
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}@>{i_{P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\circ{\operatorname{Frob}}_{M}^{!}\end{CD}

commutes.

The latter is equivalent to the commutativity of

(8.31)

\begin{CD}\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}@>{i_{P_{1},P_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}\\
@V{i_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{1}}}V{}V@V{}V{i_{P_{2},P^{\prime}_{2}}}V\\
\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{1}}^{\operatorname{restr}}@>{i_{P^{\prime}_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}}>{}>\operatorname{GrothSpr}_{P^{\prime}_{2}}^{\operatorname{restr}}.\end{CD}

8.7.5.

However, the required assertion follows from Theorem
7.1.2
: indeed, both circuits in (
8.31
)
identify with
i_{P_{1},P^{\prime}_{2}}
.

∎[Theorem
8.7.2
]

Appendix A
Sheaves on connected groups

In this section we let
G
be connected. We will supply proofs for some technical results from Sect.
4
.

A.1.
The categories
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
and
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}

In this subsection we will provide an explicit description of the categories
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
and
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
.

A.1.1.

Note that the categories
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
and
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
do not change when we replace

G
by its maximal reductive quotient.

Hence, for the rest of this subsection we will assume that
G
is reductive.

A.1.2.

Let
p:\widetilde{G}\to G
be an isogeny. It is again easy to see that the functor

p^{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(G)\to\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{G})

induces equivalences

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{G})^{\operatorname{q-const}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{G})^{\operatorname{alm-const}}.

Hence, we can assume that
G
is a product of a torus and a semi-simple group.

A.1.3.

We claim:

Lemma A.1.4
.

For a torus
T
and any (smooth, connected) scheme
X
, the functor

(A.1)

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:q mon torus}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

First we claim that (
A.1
) is fully faithful. This follows from the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}@>{\text{\eqref{e:prod by torus}}}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)@>{}>{}>\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X),\end{CD}

in which:

•

The right vertical arrow is fully faithful tautologically;

•

The left vertical arrow is fully faithful because
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
dualizable;

•

The bottom horizontal arrow is fully faithful, since it can be decomposed as

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(T)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)

with both arrows fully faithful (the first one since
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is dualizable).

Thus, it remains to show that the essential image of (
A.1
) generates the target. It suffices to show that the
pullback functor

\pi^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

generates the target.

It is easy to see that the functor

\pi_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X)

sends

\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
.

Hence, it suffices to show that
\pi_{*}
is conservative on
\operatorname{Shv}(T\times X)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
. Taking the !-fiber
at some/any point
x\in X
, we reduce the assertion to one about
T
itself, in which case it follows from
Proposition
4.9.2
.

∎

A.1.5.

Thus, using Lemma
A.1.4
, and given the description of

\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

in Sect.
4.9.5
, we can focus on the case when
G
is semi-simple.

A.1.6.

First, as in Sect.
4.9.5
, we have:

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G){\operatorname{-mod}},

where the functor
\leftarrow
sends

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)\in\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G){\operatorname{-mod}}

to

\omega_{G}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}.

Since
G
is semi-simple, we have

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(W),

where
W
is a finite-dimensional cohomologivally graded vector space concentrated in odd degrees
\geq 3
.

The fiber of !-fiber at
1\in G
identifies with the functor

(A.2)

(-)\underset{\operatorname{Sym}(W)}{\otimes}\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell},\quad\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}.

A.1.7.

Koszul duality identifies

\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}_{\{0\}},

where the subscript
\{0\}
corresponds to the condition that the generators of
W^{\vee}[-1]
act locally nilpotently.

In terms of this equivalence, the functor (
A.2
) corresponds to the functor

\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}_{\{0\}}\to\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect},

where the second arrow is the natural forgetful functor.

In particular, the t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
corresponds to the t-structure on the category

\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}_{\{0\}}
, induced by the natural t-structure on
\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}
.

Remark A.1.8
.

We should think of the vector space
W^{\vee}[-1]
as the abelian Lie algebra that governs the rational
homotopy type of
G
.

A.1.9.

Note now that as in Sect.
4.9.4
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
identifies with the
left-completion of
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}
with respect to its t-structure.

From here, we obtain:

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\simeq\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}.

A.1.10.

Note that the above description of
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
implies that it is compactly generated.
In particular, this implies Lemma
4.1.3
.

Note, however,
that the natural embedding

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G)

does
not
preserve compactness. Indeed, it sends the compact generator

\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1])\in\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}

to an unbounded complex, denoted

\operatorname{q-const}_{G}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}},

whose fiber at
1\in G
is
\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1])
.

It is also
makes it manifest that the embedding

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{alm-const}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}

is
not
an equivalence.

A.2.
The left adjoint for semi-simple groups

In this subsection we let
G
be semi-simple.

A.2.1.

We will prove the following assertion:

Proposition A.2.2
.

The embedding

\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{q-const}}:\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G)

admits a left adjoint.

The rest of the subsection is devoted to the proof of the proposition.

A.2.3.

Note that the Koszul duality in Sect.
A.1.7
extends to an
equivalence

\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{ren}}\to\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}},\quad M\mapsto{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Sym}(W)}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell},M),

where
\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{ren}}
is a renormalized version of the category
\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}
, in which we declare
finite-dimensional modules as compacts.

We will show that in terms of this equivalence, the left adjoint of
\operatorname{emb}^{\operatorname{q-const}}
is given by

{\mathcal{F}}\mapsto\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),

where
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}})
is viewed naturally as a module over
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)
.

A.2.4.

To prove this, we need to establish an equivalence

(A.3)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)}({\mathcal{F}},\operatorname{q-const}_{G}).

as
\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1])
-modules, where the
\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1])
-action on the left-hand side is via

\operatorname{Sym}(W^{\vee}[-1])\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Sym}(W){\operatorname{-mod}}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}).

A.2.5.

Let us describe the object
\operatorname{q-const}_{G}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
explicitly. Write

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{i},

where
{\mathsf{P}}_{i}
are perfect modules over
\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)
, and such that for every
n
, the system

i\mapsto\tau^{\leq n}({\mathsf{P}}_{i})

stabilizes.

Then

\operatorname{q-const}_{G}\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{i}^{\vee}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}{\otimes}\omega_{G},

where we note that for every
n
the system

i\mapsto\tau^{\geq-n}\left({\mathsf{P}}_{i}^{\vee}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}{\otimes}\omega_{G}\right)

stabilizes.

A.2.6.

Thus, we rewrite the right-hand in (
A.3
) as

\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathsf{P}}_{i}^{\vee}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}{\otimes}\omega_{G})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{i}^{\vee}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}{\otimes}{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(G)}({\mathcal{F}},\omega_{G})\simeq\\
\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathsf{P}}_{i}^{\vee}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}{\otimes}{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\simeq\underset{i}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G){\operatorname{-mod}}}({\mathsf{P}}_{i},{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}))\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G){\operatorname{-mod}}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell},{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Vect}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\underset{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}{\otimes}\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell})\simeq\\
\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G)}(\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}_{c}(G,{\mathcal{F}}),\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}),

which is the left-hand in (
A.3
).

∎[Proposition
A.2.2
]

A.3.
Proof of Lemma
4.1.5

A.3.1.

As in Sect.
A.1.1
, we can assume that
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
are reductive. Furthermore, as in
Sects.
A.1.2
and
A.1.5
, we can assume that both
G_{1}
and
G_{2}

are semi-simple.

A.3.2.

We now use the description of
\operatorname{Shv}(G_{i})^{\operatorname{q-const}}
as
\operatorname{Sym}(W_{i}^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}
in Sect.
A.1.9
.
We note that the corresponding vector space
W
for
G:=G_{1}\times G_{2}
equals
W_{1}\oplus W_{2}
. This follows
from the fact that

\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G_{1})\otimes\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G_{2})\simeq\operatorname{C}^{\cdot}(G_{1}\times G_{2}).

Under the above identifications, the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1})^{\operatorname{q-const}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G_{2})^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G_{1}\times G_{2})^{\operatorname{q-const}}

corresponds to

\operatorname{Sym}(W_{1}^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}(W_{1}^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Sym}((W_{1}\oplus W_{2})^{\vee}[-1]){\operatorname{-mod}},

which is manifestly an equivalence.

∎[Lemma
4.1.5
]

A.4.
Proof of Proposition
4.3.2

A.4.1.

Let
G\overset{p}{\to}G_{\operatorname{red}}
be the maximal reductive quotient of
G
. We have a
commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}@>{}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\\
@A{\sim}A{\operatorname{Id}\otimes p^{*}}A@A{}A{\operatorname{Id}\otimes p^{*}}A\\
{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G_{\operatorname{red}})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}@>{}>{}>{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G_{\operatorname{red}}),\end{CD}

in which the right vertical arrow is fully faithful (since
p^{*}
is fully faithfful and admits a
right adjoint).

This allows us to assume that
G
is reductive.

A.4.2.

Set

\widetilde{G}:=G^{\prime}\times Z^{0}(G)\text{ and }\Gamma:=G^{\prime}\cap Z^{0}(G),

where
G^{\prime}
is the derived group and
Z^{0}(G)
is the connected center of
G
.

We have

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\simeq({\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{G})^{\operatorname{q-mon}})^{\Gamma}\text{ and }{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\simeq({\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(\widetilde{G}))^{\Gamma}.

This reduces the assertion to the case when
G
is a product of
a semi-simple group and a torus.

A.4.3.

Thus, let
G
be semi-simple and
T
a torus. We factor the functor

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T\times G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T\times G)

as

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T\times G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\simeq{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to\\
\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(T\times G),

where:

•

The third arrow is fully faithful since
\operatorname{Shv}(T)^{\operatorname{mon}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(T)
is fully faithful and admits a right adjoint;

•

The fifth arrow is fully faithful since the functor
\operatorname{Shv}(T)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\to\operatorname{Shv}(T\times G)

is fully faithful and admits a right adjoint.

Thus, it remains to show that the fourth arrow is fully faithful.
This reduces to the assertion of the proposition to the case when
G
is semi-simple.

A.4.4.

It is easy to reduce the statement to the case when
G
is simply-connected (the assertion survives taking a finite
cover as in Sect.
A.4.2
).

When
G
is simply connected, we have
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-mon}}=\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}
, so it is enough to show that

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes{\mathbf{C}}

is fully faithful.

However, this is the case since

\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{q-const}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(G)

admits a
left
adjoint, see Proposition
A.2.2
.

∎[Proposition
4.3.2
]

A.5.
Proof of Theorem
4.1.16

The proof presented below was explained to us by S. Raskin.

A.5.1.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}
be an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}
. We claim that in this case its
perverse cohomologies sheaves
h^{i}({\mathcal{F}})
also belong to
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}
.

Indeed, this follows from the fact that the functor
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
is t-exact up to a cohomological
shift, and the functor

\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G)\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(G\times G)

is t-exact, so its essential image is preserved by the cohomological truncations.

A.5.2.

Similarly, if
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}
is perverse, then its irreducible subquotients
also belong to
\operatorname{Shv}(G)^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}
.

Hence, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is irreducible.

A.5.3.

The (cohomologically shifted) functor
\operatorname{mult}^{!}
sends irreducible perverse sheaves to irreducible perverse sheaves. Irreducible objects
of
(\operatorname{Shv}(G)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(G))^{\heartsuit}
have the form

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2},

Hence,

(A.4)

\operatorname{mult}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}.

Let
U\subset G
(resp.,
U_{1}
,
U_{2}
) be the maximal open subset over which
{\mathcal{F}}
(resp.,
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
)
is lisse.

We obtain that

\operatorname{mult}^{-1}(U)=U_{1}\times U_{2}.

However, it is easy to see that this forces
U=U_{1}=U_{2}=G
. Hence,
{\mathcal{F}}
(and hence
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
)
are irreducible local systems.

A.5.4.

Restricting (
A.4
) to
1_{G}\times G
, we obtain

{\mathcal{F}}\simeq({\mathcal{F}}_{1})_{1_{G}}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2}.

Since
{\mathcal{F}}
is irreducible, we obtain that
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
is one-dimensional, and
{\mathcal{F}}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.

Symmetrically, we obtain that
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
is one-dimensional, and
{\mathcal{F}}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
.

A.5.5.

Thus,
{\mathcal{F}}
is a one-dimensional local system, and we have an isomorphism

(A.5)

\operatorname{mult}^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}.

I.e.,
{\mathcal{F}}
is an abelian character sheaf on
G
.

∎[Theorem
4.1.16
]

A.6.
Another proof of Theorem
4.1.16
for
G={\mathbb{G}}_{a}

A.6.1.

In this subsection we will present another proof of Theorem
4.1.16
, specific to

G={\mathbb{G}}_{a}
. This proof will have a feature that it shows that the functor

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a})^{\operatorname{q-mon}}\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes\operatorname{Shv}({\mathbb{G}}_{a})^{\operatorname{mult-decomp}}

is an equivalence for
any

{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
.

Hence, by the same logic as in the proof of Theorem
4.2.7
, this would
imply the assertion of Conjecture
4.2.8
for
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
.

A.6.2.

Let
X
be an algebraic variety. Let

\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{diag-decomp}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}(X)

be the full subcategory consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}
, for which

(\Delta_{X})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X).

We will prove:

Theorem A.6.3
.

The subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{diag-decomp}}
equals the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ponct}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(X)

generated under colimits by the objects
\delta_{x}
for closed points
x\in X
.

A.6.4.

Note that the statement of Theorem
4.1.16
for
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
is equivalent to that of
Theorem
A.6.3
via Fourier-Deligne transform.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
A.6.3
.

A.6.5.

For a closed point

i:\operatorname{Spec}(k)\to X,

consider the canonical map

(A.6)

i^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\to i^{!}\circ i_{*}\circ i^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq i^{*}({\mathcal{F}}).

A.6.6.

Note that the map (
A.6
) is zero when
x
is a smooth point of (a positive-dimensional)
X
and
{\mathcal{F}}
is lisse near
x

(i.e., the (perverse) cohomologies of
{\mathcal{F}}

are colimits of perverse sheaves that are lisse on a neighborhood of
x
).

Indeed, if
x
is a smooth point, the map (
A.6
) is zero for
{\mathcal{F}}=\operatorname{const}_{X}
. Now, we have a commutative square

\begin{CD}i^{!}(\operatorname{const}_{X})\otimes i^{*}({\mathcal{F}})@>{}>{}>i^{!}({\mathcal{F}})\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:! to *}}\otimes\operatorname{id}}V{}V@V{}V{\text{\eqref{e:! to *}}}V\\
i^{*}(\operatorname{const}_{X})\otimes i^{*}({\mathcal{F}})@>{\sim}>{}>i^{*}({\mathcal{F}}),\end{CD}

where the top horizontal arrow is an isomorphism for
{\mathcal{F}}
lisse near
x
.

A.6.7.

We claim that if
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{diag-decomp}}
, then the map (
A.6
)
is an isomorphism. Indeed, we can interpret (
A.6
) as the natural transformation

(A.7)

i^{*}\circ(\operatorname{id}\times i)^{!}\to i^{!}\circ(i\times\operatorname{id})^{*},

corresponding to the diagram

\begin{CD}X\times\operatorname{pt}@>{\operatorname{id}\times i}>{}>X\times X\\
@A{i}A{}A@A{}A{i\times\operatorname{id}}A\\
\operatorname{pt}@>{i}>{}>\operatorname{pt}\times X\end{CD}

evaluated on
(\Delta_{X})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})
.

However, (
A.7
) is an isomorphism on the essential image of
\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)
.

A.6.8.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\notin\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{ponct}}(X)
. Let
Y\subset X
be the subvariety of maximal dimension such that the
*-fiber of
{\mathcal{F}}
at the generic point of
Y
is non-zero. Up to replacing
X
by
Y
and
{\mathcal{F}}
by its restriction to

Y
, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
does not vanish at the generic point of (a positive-dimensional)
X
.

Let
k^{\prime}
denote the algebraic closure of the field of fractions of
X
. Let
X^{\prime}
,
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
denote the
corresponding base-changed objects. Note that

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X^{\prime})^{\operatorname{diag-decomp}}.

Let

i^{\prime}:\operatorname{Spec}(k^{\prime})\to X^{\prime}

denote the canonical point. However, it is easy to see to the map (
A.6
)

(i^{\prime})^{!}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\to(i^{\prime})^{*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})

is zero for any
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
that comes by base change from
X
(see Sect.
A.6.6
).

Combining with Sect.
A.6.5
,
we obtain that
(i^{\prime})^{*}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})=0
, which is a contradiction.

∎
